Breaking off in the middle of his laugh, Gypo strode over to Aunt Betty. He took her by the arm, pointed his finger at the woman in the fur coat and whispered hoarsely: “I want her. Get me a room. I want to take her upstairs. Ye can have whatever money ye ask.”

“Never,” shrieked the woman in the fur coat.

She put her hands to her face. Then she took a tiny step forward with her right foot and stood leaning on the foot, trembling as if she had planted it on ice.

“None of this nonsense, Phyllis,” said Aunt Betty, coming forward to the centre of the room. She faced the fur-coated woman with her arms akimbo and her jaws squared. “I’m fed up with your swagger. You’re no better than yer board and lodging, an’ as long as I keep you, you’re no better than any other woman that takes bite and sup in my house. Put that in your pipe and smoke it. One man is as good as another. You’re going with him.”
===Chinese===
笑到一半，吉波猛地收住，大步走到贝蒂大妈跟前。他抓住她的胳膊，手指着那穿皮草的女人，哑着嗓子低声说：「我要她。给我开个房。我要带她上楼去。你要多少钱都行。」

「休想，」穿皮草的女人尖叫道。

她双手捂住脸。随后她右脚朝前挪了极小的一步，整个人倚在那只脚上，颤颤发抖，仿佛一脚踩在了冰上。

「少来这套胡闹，菲莉丝（Phyllis），」贝蒂大妈说着，走到屋子中央。她双手叉腰，下颌绷得方方正正，迎面对着那穿皮草的女人。「你那副神气我受够了。你并不比你的食宿钱高贵，只要我养着你一天，你就跟我这屋里任何一个吃我饭、喝我水的女人一样，不高一分。你自己好好琢磨去吧。一个男人跟另一个男人一样好。你就跟他去。」

===Original===
“That’s true, Aunt Betty,” cried several women, looking with hatred at the fur-coated woman.

“Rabble,” shrieked the fur-coated woman, stamping her feet and shaking her fists all round her at the women. “What filthy souls you have to be reduced to this level. I’m not a prostitute like you and that’s why you hate me. You hate me because I’m an educated woman and—”

“It’s nothin’ o’ the kind,” cried a big, large-boned, red-faced, strong, handsome woman, called Connemara Maggie. “We hate ye because yer a stuck-up, ignorant thing, that thinks she’s better than what God turned her into; an’ God forgive me for sayin’ so—”

“More power to ye, Maggie,” interrupted several, “tell her yer mind.”

“I don’t mind what you say, Connemara Maggie,” gasped the fur-coated one. “You’re not the worst of them and—”
===Chinese===
「说得对，贝蒂大妈，」几个女人喊道，怀着恨意望着那穿皮草的女人。

「贱民，」穿皮草的女人尖叫着，跺着脚，朝四面的女人们挥舞拳头。「你们得堕落到这个地步，灵魂多么肮脏。我不像你们是妓女，所以你们恨我。你们恨我，因为我是个受过教育的女人，而且——」

「根本不是那么回事，」一条骨骼粗大、红脸膛、结实漂亮的高个女人喊道，她叫康尼马拉的玛吉（Connemara Maggie）。「我们恨你，是因为你是个自高自大、没见识的东西，自以为比上帝把她造出来的样子还高贵；上帝饶恕我这么说——」

「说得带劲，玛吉，」几个人打断道，「把你的想法都倒给她。」

「你说什么我不在乎，康尼马拉的玛吉，」穿皮草的喘着气说。「你还不是她们里头最坏的，而且——」

===Original===
“Good God,” shouted Aunt Betty, suddenly putting her hands to her breasts.

She backed to the wall, staring furtively at the fur-coated woman. She was in the power of one of her “visions.” Gypo stared at the woman in the fur coat with his arms hanging loosely by his sides.

“Listen,” continued the woman, “I don’t bear any of you any malice. You can’t help it, any of you. I don’t bear even you any malice, Aunt Betty. I know very well that were it not for you I would starve or﻿ ﻿… or be in a worse place. I have been in your house a month now and you’ve been kind to me. I know very well nobody can help anything. I’m English, an army officer’s wife, so it’s only natural that you girls would be prejudiced against me—”

“It’s nothing o’ the kind,” cried Connemara Maggie; “it’s yer stuck-up ways that—”

“Let her have her say, Maggie,” cried another.

“I had no right to come in here,” cried the woman, bursting into tears. “I should have gone to the police and got them—”
===Chinese===
「老天爷啊，」贝蒂大妈喊道，猛地双手抓住胸口。

她退到墙根，贼忒兮兮地盯着那穿皮草的女人。她被她的一个「幻象」攫住了。吉波两臂松松垂在身侧，直盯着那穿皮草的女人。

「听着，」那女人接着说，「我对你们谁都没有恶意。你们谁也由不得自己。连你我也并不怀恨，贝蒂大妈。我心里很清楚，若不是你，我早就饿死了，或者……或者落在一个更糟的地方。我住在你这屋里已经一个月了，你一直待我不坏。我很明白，谁都帮不了任何事。我是英国人，一个陆军军官的太太，所以你们这些姑娘对我有偏见，也是自然的——」

「根本不是那么回事，」康尼马拉的玛吉嚷道；「是你那自高自大的做派才——」

「让她把话说完，玛吉，」另一个喊道。

「我本来就没有权利进这儿来，」那女人喊道，眼泪迸了出来。「我本该去报警，让他们——」

===Original===
“The police!” yelled Gypo suddenly, starting as if he had been awakened from his sleep. “None o’ that talk. Keep away from the police. What d’ye want the police for?”

“I want to get back home,” sobbed the woman.

“Where’s yer home?”

“It’s﻿ ﻿… it’s near London.”

“Well, what are ye doin’ over here then?”

“I got this,” cried the woman, becoming hysterical again. She put a trembling hand to her disfigured cheek. “I got this a year ago. It’s driven me mad. My husband took another woman. I sold everything I had and came over to Dublin. I wanted to go to work. Honest to God, I did. But I could get nothing. Then a man brought me down here. Good God, the shame of telling you all this in a place like this﻿ ﻿… the﻿…”
===Chinese===
「警察！」吉波猛地吼起来，像从睡梦中惊醒似的。「这种话不许说。离警察远点儿。你要警察干什么？」

「我要回家去，」那女人抽泣着说。

「你家在哪儿？」

「在……在伦敦附近。」

「那你跑到这儿来干什么？」

「我落了这个，」那女人喊道，又歇斯底里起来。她把一只发抖的手按在毁坏的半边脸上。「一年前我落了这个。它把我逼疯了。我丈夫另找了女人。我变卖了我所有的一切，渡海到了都柏林。我想找工作。老天在上，我真的想。可我什么也找不到。后来一个男人把我带到这儿来了。老天爷，在这样一个地方把这些都说给你们听，多丢人啊……这……」

===Original===
“D’ye want to go home now?” cried Gypo angrily.

She did not reply, but looked at him with large eyes, as if in amazement.

“What’ll bring ye home? How much will it cost?”

“A little over two pounds,” she replied in a low voice.

“Here,” he cried, taking out his money, “here’s yer fare. One, two, three,” he paused and was going to add a fourth, but he put it back. He handed her the three notes. She shrank backwards, looking at the money with large eyes.

“Don’t be afraid,” he said in a strange, dreamy voice. “Take the money an’ get outa here. That’s enough to take ye home. Go back home. Yer not wanted here. You an’ yer husband and the police. Ye better keep away from the police, I’m tellin’ ye. Go on. Beat it. Get outa here.”

Staring him in the face, trembling, with her mouth open, she seized the notes suddenly. Then uttering an exclamation, she looked about her once and rushed to the door.

“Go off now,” cried Gypo after her. “Go off now.”
===Chinese===
「你现在想回家吗？」吉波怒声问。

她没有回答，只睁大眼睛望着他，仿佛惊呆了。

「什么能送你回家？要多少钱？」

「两镑多一点，」她低声回答。

「给，」他喊道，掏出他的钱，「这是你的路费。一，二，三，」他顿了顿，本想再添第四张，又把它塞了回去。他把三张钞票递给她。她朝后一缩，睁大眼睛望着那钱。

「别怕，」他用一种古怪而恍惚的声音说。「拿上钱，离开这儿。这够送你回家了。回家去吧。这儿用不着你。你，你丈夫，还有警察。你最好离警察远点儿，我告诉你。走吧。快走。离开这儿。」

她直直地盯着他的脸，浑身发抖，嘴张着，猛地一把抓过钞票。随后喊了一声，朝四下里望了一眼，冲向门口。

「这就走吧，」吉波在她背后喊。「这就走吧。」

===Original===
Everybody stared at the door through which she disappeared, banging it after her. There was silence. Then Aunt Betty spoke:

“That’s all very well,” she sniggered; “but she owes me two pound ten. Who’s goin’ to pay me that? It’s all very well doin’ the—”

“Shut up yer gob,” cried Gypo, “here’s two pound for ye. That’s enough. Not another word outa ye.” He threw two pound notes at her. Then he threw out his arms. “Who’s comin’ to bed with me,” he cried, “before the bank is broke?”

“I am, me bould son o’ gosha,” cried Connemara Maggie, rushing to him, with her yellow curly hair streaming about her face and her blue eyes dancing.

She enveloped his neck with her brawny arms.
===Chinese===
人人都盯着那扇门，她人影一晃就没进去了，门在她身后砰地关上。一片寂静。随后贝蒂大妈开了口：

「好啊，痛快，」她窃笑着说；「可她还欠我两镑十先令呢。这笔钱谁替她还？痛痛快快充好人是容易的——」

「闭上你的臭嘴，」吉波喊道，「这两镑给你。够了。再多说一个字都不行。」他朝她甩过去两张一镑的票子。随后他张开双臂。「谁跟我上床去，」他喊道，「趁银行还没倒闭？」

「我去，我大胆的好小子，」康尼马拉的玛吉喊道，朝他扑过去，一头黄卷发在脸旁飞散，一双蓝眼睛滴溜溜直转。

她那两条粗壮的胳膊一下子搂住了他的脖子。

## V / 五

===Original===
"Gypo!"

Gypo had taken three steps down the street when his name came to him through the darkness, uttered in that long-drawn-out whisper which is the customary intonation among revolutionaries. He contracted his back suddenly like an ass that has been struck with violence. Then he halted. He did not turn about or reply. He waited. He listened with a beating heart to the slow footsteps that came up to him from behind. One, two, three, four … they stopped. Gypo looked to his left. Bartly Mulholland was standing there.

===Chinese===
「吉波！」

吉波沿着街刚走出三步，他的名字便穿过黑暗传了过来，用的是那种拖得长长的低语——革命者之间惯用的腔调。他猛地缩起背，像一头遭了狠揍的驴。随即他站住了。他没有转身，也没有应声。他等着。他心怦怦地跳，听着身后慢慢朝他逼近的脚步声。一步，两步，三步，四步……停了。吉波朝左边望去。巴特利·马尔霍兰德（Bartly Mulholland）就站在那儿。

===Original===
The two of them stood in front of a window through which lamplight was streaming, across Gypo's chest on to Mulholland's face. Mulholland's yellow face looked almost black in the lamplight. It was furrowed vertically from the temples to the jaws, with deep black furrows. The mouth was large and open, fixed in a perpetual grin that had absolutely no merriment in it, that fixed grin of sardonic contempt that is nearly always seen on the faces of men who make a business of concealing their thoughts. The nose was long and narrow. The ears were large. The forehead was furrowed horizontally. The skin on the forehead was very white in contrast to the dark skin on the cheeks. The furrows on the forehead were very shallow and narrow, like thin lines drawn with a sharp pencil. In fact, the whole appearance of the face was that of an artificial face, such as that produced in the dressing-room of an actor by means of paints, etc. This suggestion was strengthened by the appearance of the hair that straggled in loose wisps from beneath the shovel-shaped peak of the grey tweed cap. The hair appeared to be a dirty brown wig, much the worse for wear. But neither the hair nor any portion of the face was artificial. Everything had come from the hand of Nature, which seemed, by some peculiar whimsey, to have cast this individual for the role of a conspirator. The face was the face of a clown to hide the conspirator's eyes, except from a very close scrutiny. The eyes were the colour of sea water that is dirty with grey sand. These eyes are sometimes described as watery blue, but it is a totally wrong description. There was an indescribable coldness and depth in them which it is beyond the power of any colour to describe. They stared without a movement of the pupils or of the lashes at Gypo's face, expressing no emotion whatever. They were not doors of the soul like ordinary eyes, but spy holes. They stared glassily like a cat's eyes.

This curious creature was dressed like a workman, in heavy hobnailed boots, brown corduroy trousers with strings tied around the legs below the knees, a black handkerchief tied in sailor fashion around his neck and an old grey tweed coat that almost reached halfway down his thighs. His hands were stuck deep down in the pockets of his coat.

"Where's yer hurry takin' ye, Gypo?" he drawled in a low lazy voice, as if he were half-drunk or lying on his back in a sunny place on a hot summer's day.

===Chinese===
两人站在一扇窗前，灯光从窗里涌出来，掠过吉波的胸口，落在马尔霍兰德的脸上。马尔霍兰德那张黄脸在灯光下看着几乎是黑的。脸上从太阳穴到下颌刻着一道道竖纹，又深又黑。嘴巴大张着，凝成一个永远的咧笑，里头没有半点笑意——那是一种讥诮而轻蔑的固定笑容，凡是靠遮掩心思吃饭的人，脸上几乎总挂着它。鼻子又长又窄。耳朵很大。额头上布着横纹。额头的皮肤很白，与两颊的深色皮肤对比鲜明。额头上的纹路又浅又细，像用削尖的铅笔描出来的细线。其实，这整张脸看上去就像一张假脸，如同演员在化妆室里用油彩之类弄出来的那一种。灰色粗花呢帽那鸭嘴形的帽檐底下，几绺蓬乱的头发支棱出来，看着像一顶油腻的棕色假发，还戴得破旧不堪——这更加强了假脸的意味。可无论是头发，还是这张脸上的任何一处，都不是假的。一切都出自造化之手，而造化仿佛出于某种古怪的兴致，偏偏派给此人一个阴谋家的角色。这张脸是一张小丑的脸，专为遮住阴谋家的眼睛，除非凑得极近细看，才瞒不住。那双眼睛是海水的颜色，是混着灰沙的海水。这种眼睛有时被称作水蓝色，但那全然是错误的描述。那里面有另一种无法形容的冰冷与幽深，任何颜色都不足以描摹。它们死死盯着吉波的脸，瞳孔不动，睫毛不动，不露一丝情绪。它们不像寻常的眼睛那样是心灵的门户，而是窥孔。它们像猫眼一样，呆滞地瞪着。

这个怪物打扮得像个工人：沉重的钉靴，棕色灯芯绒裤子，膝盖以下用绳子扎着裤腿，脖子上照水手的样式系一条黑手帕，身上一件旧灰色粗花呢外套，几乎垂到大腿一半的地方。两只手深深插在外衣口袋里。

「吉波，你急着上哪儿去呀？」他懒洋洋地低声拖长腔调说，好像半醉着，又好像大热天里正仰面躺在一块向阳的地方。

===Original===
"Who's in a hurry?" growled Gypo. "How d'ye make out I'm in a hurry?"

"Oh, nothin' atall. Don't get yer rag out, Gypo. Ye might talk to the people. We never see ye atall now since ye left the Organization. Are ye workin'?"

"No," snapped Gypo angrily. The short ejaculation coming from his thick lips sounded like a solitary gunshot coming a long way over still air. "I ain't workin' an' all o' you fellahs, that were supposed to be comrades o' mine, take damn good care to keep out o' the way, for fear I might ask ye for the price of a feed or a flop. Yer a quare lot o' Communists."

Mulholland drew himself in at the middle, emitted his breath, shrugged his shoulders, thrust out his right foot and leaned his weight backwards heavily on his left foot. Then he turned his head up sideways to let the drizzling rain beat on the back of his neck instead of on his face. The grin left his mouth and for a moment he appeared to have become angry.

===Chinese===
「谁着急了？」吉波吼道，「你凭什么说我着急？」

「哦，没说啥。别冒火嘛，吉波。跟人说说话不就得了。自打你离开组织，我们如今压根儿见不着你。有活儿干吗？」

「没有。」吉波怒冲冲地厉声答道。这句短促的话从他厚嘴唇里迸出来，就像一声孤零零的枪响，越过凝滞的空气从老远的地方传来。「我没活儿干，可你们这帮家伙——本该是我同志的——倒是一个个躲得远远的，生怕我开口讨口饭钱、讨个睡处。你们这帮共产党，可真是怪得出奇。」

马尔霍兰德腰身往中间一收，吐出一口气，耸了耸肩，右脚朝前伸出，把整个身子的重量沉甸甸地压到左脚上。接着他把头朝侧上方一扬，好让蒙蒙细雨打在后颈上，而不是打在脸上。咧笑从他嘴上消失了，有一瞬间，他像是动了怒。

===Original===
"Ye don't seem to be in any need o' money tonight, Gypo," he breathed ever so gently.

Then just as suddenly he broke into an almost fawning and ingratiating smile. He continued in his ordinary lazy voice:

"Don't be tryin' to make out yer broke, after me seein' the money that fell outa yer pocket in the kitchen beyond just now. Aren't ye goin' to stand us a wet?"

Gypo had begun to shiver. He shivered with minute movements, just as a massive tree shivers, when the forest earth is shaken beneath it by a heavy concussion. Then suddenly he recovered himself. Without pausing to think, he shot out both hands simultaneously like piston-rods. Mulholland gasped as the two huge hands closed about his throat. He struck out helplessly with his own hands at Gypo's body. His blows were as ineffective as the flapping of a linnet's wings against its cage. Gypo's face was lit with a demoniac pleasure as he raised Mulholland's body from the ground, clutching it by the throat with his two hands. He raised it up like a book which he wanted to read, until Mulholland's eyes were level with his own. Then they both looked at one another.

===Chinese===
「吉波，你今晚可不像缺钱花的样子啊。」他极轻极柔地吐出这一句。

随即他又突然换上一副近乎谄媚讨好的笑脸，用平常那种懒洋洋的声调接着说：

「别装穷啦，刚才在那边厨房里，钱从你口袋里掉出来，我可是亲眼看见的。你不请我们喝上一盅吗？」

吉波开始发抖了。他抖得极细微，就像一棵参天大树，在林间大地被一阵猛烈的震荡摇撼时那样微微颤动。随即他猛然定住了神。他连想都没想，双手像活塞杆一样同时直捣出去。两只大手掐住喉咙，马尔霍兰德倒抽了一口气。他挥着自己的手朝吉波身上乱打，可那些拳头就像红雀在笼中扑腾翅膀，全无半点用处。吉波双手掐住马尔霍兰德的喉咙，把他整个人从地上提了起来，脸上燃着一种魔鬼般的快意。他把那身子像一本要读的书似的举到眼前，直到马尔霍兰德的眼睛与自己的眼睛平齐。两人便这样对望着。

===Original===
Mulholland's eyes were still cold and glassy, impenetrable and absolutely without emotion. Gypo's eyes were ferocious and eager, full of a mad savage joy. His mouth had shut tight and the skin had run taut over the glossy humps on his face, so that his face looked like tanned pigskin. Mulholland's tongue was hanging out.

Then Gypo groaned and prepared to crush out Mulholland's life between his thick fingers, when he was disturbed by a shout from behind. He dropped Mulholland to the street like a bag and whirled about. Tommy Connor had rushed up from the doorway of No. 44 where he had been waiting. He was standing now with his mouth wide open in astonishment and terror.

"What's wrong, boys?" he cried. "In the name o' God what are ye up to?"

"He suspects me," cried Gypo, "and …" Then he sank into silence, unable to say any more. His unsatisfied fury choked him.

===Chinese===
马尔霍兰德的眼睛依旧冰冷呆滞，深不可测，毫无半点情绪。吉波的眼睛凶狠而急切，满是一种疯狂的、野蛮的快意。他的嘴紧紧闭住，皮肤绷在脸上那些油亮的肉疙瘩上，整张脸看上去像鞣过的猪皮。马尔霍兰德的舌头吐了出来。

就在这时，吉波闷哼一声，正要用粗手指掐断马尔霍兰德的性命，身后一声喊惊动了他。他像扔一条口袋似的把马尔霍兰德撂在街上，霍地转过身去。汤米·康纳（Tommy Connor）原本在44号门口守着，这时已经飞奔过来，正张大了嘴站在那里，又是惊愕又是恐惧。

「怎么啦，弟兄们？」他喊道，「看在上帝份上，你们这是干什么？」

「他疑心我，」吉波喊道，「还有……」随后他便沉入沉默，再也说不出话。那股没处发泄的怒火噎住了他。

===Original===
"Suspects ye of what?" cried Connor. "What d'ye say he suspects ye of?"

"I didn't suspect him of anythin' atall," cried Mulholland, rising to his feet slowly. His face was contorting with pain. "I only asked him to—"

"Yer a liar, ye did," bellowed Gypo. "Ye suspect me, an' well I know ye, Bartly Mulholland. D'ye think I don't know ye an' all about ye? Ye got a grudge agin me an' Frankie McPhillip this long time. Don't I know yer Intelligence Officer for No. 3 Area an' that yer nosin' around now—"

"Shut up or I'll plug ye where ye stand," hissed Connor, ramming the muzzle of his revolver into Gypo's side. "Don't ye know there are people listenin'? D'ye want to let the dogs o' the street know the secrets o' the Organization that ye swore on yer oath to kape?" He panted and continued in a lower voice still: "Are ye mad or are ye lookin' to get plugged?"

===Chinese===
「疑心你什么？」康纳喊道，「你说他疑心你什么？」

「我压根儿没疑心他什么，」马尔霍兰德喊道，慢慢从地上爬起来。他的脸痛得直抽搐。「我只是要他——」

「你撒谎，你就是疑心了，」吉波咆哮道。「你疑心我，我还能不知道你，巴特利·马尔霍兰德。你以为我不了解你、不了解你的一切吗？你对我跟弗兰基·麦克菲利普记恨不是一天两天了。我岂不知道你是三号地区的情报官（Intelligence Officer），如今正四处打探——」

「闭嘴，不然我就地崩了你，」康纳嘶声说，把左轮枪口顶进吉波的肋间。「你不知道有人在听吗？你发过誓要保守组织的秘密，难道要让满街的野狗都知道吗？」他喘着气，把声音压得更低，接着说：「你是疯了，还是存心要挨枪子儿？」

===Original===
Gypo's mouth remained open in the act of beginning a word, but he did not utter the word. He half-turned his body in order to look into Connor's face. He saw it, big, angry, menacing, with the nostrils distended, so that the insides, blackened with coal, were visible. The face was within four inches of Gypo's face. Connor's revolver muzzle was pressing into Gypo's right armpit. Gypo feared neither the face nor the revolver. He stared with wrinkled forehead at Connor, knowing that he could crush him and Mulholland, both together, crush them to death, to a shapeless pulp, by clasping them in his arms.

But they were not merely two men, two human beings. They were something more than that. They represented the Revolutionary Organization. They were merely cogs in the wheel of that Organization. That was what he feared, what rendered him powerless. He feared that mysterious, intangible thing, that was all brain and no body. An intelligence without a body. A thing that was full of plans, implacable, reaching out everywhere invisibly, with invisible tentacles like a supernatural monster. A thing that was like a religion, mysterious, occult, devilish.

Frankie McPhillip had once told him that they tracked a man to the Argentine Republic, somewhere the other side of the world. Shot him dead in a lodging-house at night too, without saying a word. What d'ye think of that?

===Chinese===
吉波的嘴还张着，正要吐出一个字，却终究没有说出来。他侧过半个身子，去看康纳的脸。他看见了：一张大脸，怒气冲冲，杀气逼人，鼻孔张得老大，连里面熏黑的煤灰都看得见。那张脸离吉波的脸不到四英寸。康纳的左轮枪口顶在吉波的右腋下。吉波既不怕那张脸，也不怕那把枪。他皱着额头死盯住康纳，心里明白，自己只要双臂一合，就能把康纳和马尔霍兰德一起搂住，活活挤死他们，挤成一摊不成形状的肉泥。

可他们不只是两个人、两副血肉之躯。他们不止于此。他们代表着革命组织。他们不过是那个组织大轮子上的两枚齿轮。他怕的是这个，正是这才叫他浑身没了力气。他怕那个神秘莫测、无形无质的东西，一个只有头脑、没有躯体的东西。一种没有躯体的智慧。一种满腹算计的东西，毫不容情，无形地伸向四面八方，像一头超自然的怪物，伸出看不见的触手。一种近乎宗教的东西，神秘、诡秘、恶魔一般。

弗兰基·麦克菲利普有一次告诉他，他们曾追踪一个人，一直追到阿根廷共和国，追到世界的另一头。也是夜里，在一家寄宿公寓里一枪把他打死，一句话都没说。你说这事怎么样？

===Original===
"All right," he said at last, "put away yer gat, Tommy. I'll stay quiet."

A few people had gathered on the far side of the street and were looking on curiously. An immense crowd would have already gathered on ordinary occasions, but there was tension and anxiety in the district that night. Shooting might begin at any minute. It was always so. One death brings another in its train. Each man thought this in his own mind, although nobody breathed a word. It was a kind of silent terror.

"Come on, boys," said Connor, "let's get away from here. We're gatherin' a crowd."

"Come on down to Ryan's," whispered Mulholland to Gypo, in his usual lazy, insinuating voice, as if nothing had happened, "Commandant Gallagher is down there. He wants to see ye."

===Chinese===
「行啦，」他终于开口，「把枪收起来，汤米。我不吭声了。」

街对面已经聚起了几个人，好奇地朝这边张望。若在平常，早该围上一大群人了，可那天晚上，这一带人心惶惶。枪战随时可能打响。事情向来如此。一桩死亡会引来另一桩死亡。每个人心里都这么想，虽然谁也没吭一声。那是一种无声的恐怖。

「走吧，弟兄们，」康纳说，「咱们离开这儿。人越围越多了。」

「上瑞安家的酒馆去吧，」马尔霍兰德对吉波低声说，用的还是平常那种懒洋洋、话里带钩的腔调，好像什么事都没有发生过，「加拉格尔指挥官就在那儿。他要见你。」

===Original===
"What does he want with me?" growled Gypo. "I'm not a member o' the Organization any more. He's got nothin' to do with me. I'm not goin'."

"Come on, man," whispered Connor, "don't stand here chawin'. He's not goin' t'ate ye. Come on. Is it afraid o' the Commandant ye are? Why so?"

"I'm not afraid of any man that was ever pupped," growled Gypo. "Come on."

The three men walked off abreast, in step like soldiers, their feet falling loudly on to the wet pavement, heels first. At the corner the footfalls became confused. Gypo spat into the street. Mulholland sneezed. They entered the public-house by a little narrow side door that had a bright brass knob on it. They went along a narrow passage, through a stained-glass swing door, into a brightly lit oblong room.

===Chinese===
「他找我干什么？」吉波吼道。「我早不是组织的人了。他跟我毫无瓜葛。我不去。」

「走吧，老哥，」康纳低声道，「别站在这儿嚼舌头了。他又不会吃了你的。走吧。怎么，你还怕指挥官不成？怕他什么？」

「打从娘胎里生出来的人，我谁都不怕，」吉波吼道。「走。」

三个人并排走了，迈着士兵一样的步子，鞋跟先落地，踏在湿漉漉的人行道上，声音很响。到了街角，脚步声乱了起来。吉波朝街上啐了一口。马尔霍兰德打了个喷嚏。他们从一扇窄小的边门进了那家酒馆，门上装着一枚亮闪闪的黄铜圆把手。他们沿一条窄过道走去，穿过一扇彩色玻璃弹簧门，走进一间灯光明亮的长方形屋子。

===Original===
A man was sitting by a little gas fire on a high three legged stool facing the door. When Gypo saw the man he stopped dead.

The man was Commandant Dan Gallagher.

===Chinese===
有个人面对着门，坐在一小炉煤气火旁的三条腿高凳上。吉波一看见那人，猛地僵住了。

那人正是丹·加拉格尔指挥官（Commandant Dan Gallagher）。

## VI / 六

===Original===
During the previous autumn a terrific sensation had been caused all over Ireland by the farm-labourers’ strike in the M——— district. The sensation was brought to a crisis by the murder of the Farmers’ Union Secretary. For the first time it was discovered that the Revolutionary Organization had spread its influence among the farm-labourers and over the whole country. Something had been discovered. A Government secret organization had overlapped the Communist organization and there was a little effervescence, which was immediately suppressed by the Government. Very little leaked out publicly. The newspapers were forbidden to talk about it. The Conservative organs in Dublin had timid editorials demanding that the Government should take the people into its confidence. What really was the extent of this “conspiracy against the national safety”?

Then immediately Commandant Dan Gallagher became a public figure and a general topic of conversation. He came out of obscurity in a night as it were. People suddenly discovered that he was a power in the country. He was photographed and interviewed and his photographs appeared in all the newspapers both in this country and in England and in America. He promptly denounced the murder as a “foul crime against the honour of the working class and the whole revolutionary movement.” He began to be feared intensely in official quarters as a “slippery customer.” This phrase was used at a Government Cabinet meeting.

===Chinese===
前一年秋天，M——地区的农场雇工罢工在爱尔兰全境引起了莫大的轰动。农场主联合会秘书遇害，把这桩轰动推向了危机的顶点。人们头一回发现，革命组织（Revolutionary Organization）已经把影响扩散到了农场雇工中间，遍布全国。总之是有些名堂露了出来。政府手下一个秘密组织同共产党人的组织交叠在了一起，激起了一点沸腾的动静，随即便被政府压了下去。公开泄露出去的风声很少。报纸被禁止谈论此事。都柏林的保守派报纸登了些怯生生的社论，要求政府对人民开诚布公。这桩「危害国家安全的阴谋」，究竟真正闹到了多大？

紧接着，丹·加拉格尔指挥官（Commandant Dan Gallagher）就一举成了公众人物，成了街谈巷议的话题。他仿佛一夜之间就从默默无闻中冒了出来。人们突然发现，他在国内算得上一股势力。有人给他拍照，有人采访他，他的照片登遍了爱尔兰本国以及英格兰和美国的各家报纸。他随即公开谴责那桩谋杀，称之为一桩「玷辱工人阶级荣誉和整个革命运动的卑劣罪行」。官方场面上的人开始对他怕得厉害，说他是「一个滑头的角色」。这个字眼是在政府内阁会议上用的。

===Original===
Just about that time, the leading organ of the English aristocracy had a two-column leading article on the subject of Commandant Dan Gallagher. In the course of the article a short survey of Gallagher’s life was given sarcastically. The following is an extract from the article:

> “… This flower of Irish manhood grew on an obscure dunghill, in the daily practice of all these virtues, which are indigenous to the Irish soil, if one is to believe the flowery utterances of the politicians on St. Patrick’s Day. His father was a small peasant farmer in Kilkenny. Having assisted very probably in the gentle assassination of a few of his landlord’s agents in the past, reverently decided to devote the activities of his promising son to the service of his God. But Daniel would have none of it. He was meant for other fields of conquest. He succeeded in making himself famous in the ecclesiastical seminary in which he was being prepared for the priesthood, by smashing the skull of one of the Roman priests during a dispute on the playground. The instrument used in this display of boyish gaiety was the favourite Irish weapon, a hurling stick.
> “The young Fionn McCumhaill was expelled and fled the country. He drifted around for eight years without a trace of his whereabouts. Very possibly he spent the time in the United States. We can well imagine that he was favourably received among those organizations in the United States which are governed by Irishmen intent on the destruction of the British Empire by conspiracy, murder, slander, and all the other delectable schemes that come to life so readily in the Gaelic brain. We can imagine him perfecting himself in the arts of gunmanship, deceit and those obscure forms of libidinous vice which are said to be practiced by this morose type of revolutionary in order to dull his sensibilities into an apathy which the consciousness of even the most horrible enormities cannot penetrate. …
> “At any rate he has returned to his beloved motherland endowed liberally with those qualities which make him dear to the hearts of all Irishmen of murderous inclinations. These latter unfortunately form as yet a considerable portion of the population of Ireland. Mr. Gallagher has a powerful and enthusiastic following.
> “His brand of Communism is of the type that appeals most to the Irish nature. It is a mixture of Roman Catholicism, Nationalist Republicanism and Bolshevism. Its chief rallying cries are: ‘Loot and Murder.’” …

===Chinese===
也就在那前后，英国贵族阶层的首席机关报就丹·加拉格尔指挥官其人刊出一篇占两栏的社论。文章以冷嘲的笔调，对加拉格尔的生平做了一番简短的追述。以下是这篇文章的摘录：

> 「……这朵爱尔兰男儿之花，就开在一座不起眼的粪堆上，日日修行着那一切美德——照圣帕特里克节（St. Patrick's Day）上政客们花团锦簇的演说来，这些美德本是在爱尔兰土壤里土生土长的。他的父亲是基尔肯尼（Kilkenny）的一个小农。老爷子过去多半帮着体体面面地暗杀过几名地主的代理人，如今虔虔诚诚地拿定主意，要把这位前程无量的儿子的才力奉献给天主的事工。可是丹尼尔偏不领这个情。他命里注定要去另辟疆场。他在那所为他预备神职的教会修院里一举成名——办法是在操场上争执的时候，敲碎了一个罗马神父的脑壳。这场少年意气的表演所用的器械，是爱尔兰人钟爱的武器：一根曲棍球棒。
> 「这位年轻的芬恩·麦克库尔（Fionn McCumhaill）被修院开除，逃出了国门。此后八年，他四处漂泊，行踪杳然。那段时间他多半是在美国混的。我们尽可以想象，他在美国那些由爱尔兰人当家做主的组织里如何大受欢迎——这些爱尔兰人一心一意，要靠阴谋、暗杀、诽谤，外加盖尔人的脑袋里信手便能滋生的其他种种妙计，来搞垮大英帝国。我们还可以想象，他如何精研枪法与骗术，还修炼那些见不得人的淫邪勾当——据说此类阴郁的革命家就是要靠这些，把心智磨成一片麻木，磨到连最骇人的滔天大罪的自觉都透不进去。……
> 「无论如何，他回到了心爱的祖国，且浑身满满当当带着那些品质——正是这些品质，叫他深得全体嗜杀成性的爱尔兰人的欢心。不幸的是，这类人至今还占着爱尔兰人口相当可观的一份。加拉格尔先生身后，跟着一支声势浩大、热情高涨的队伍。
> 「他那一号的共产主义，正是最合爱尔兰人天性的那一类。它是罗马天主教、民族主义共和主义和布尔什维主义的混合物。它主要的集合口号是：『掠夺与谋杀。』」……

===Original===
The following is an extract from an article which appeared a little while later in the columns of the official organ of the American Revolutionary Organization:

> “When the glorious history of the struggle for proletarian liberation in Ireland comes to be written, the name of Comrade Dan Gallagher will stampede from cover to cover in one uninterrupted blaze of glory. … No other living man has given nobler service to the world revolution than this sturdy fighter, who rules the workers of Dublin with greater power than is wielded by the Irish bourgeoisie, who are still nominally in the saddle. The collapse of the farm-workers’ strike need not dishearten those comrades who expected great things from the hoisting of the red flag at M——— last October. Comrade Gallagher has not seen fit as yet to call the Irish bourgeois bluff. When the time arrives. …”

===Chinese===
下面这段摘录，出自稍后一些时候美国革命组织官方机关报的版面：

> 「等到爱尔兰无产阶级解放斗争的光辉史册有朝一日付诸史笔，丹·加拉格尔同志的大名将从头一页奔腾到末一页，烧成一道不间断的荣耀之焰。……世间再没有一个活人，比这位壮实的战士为世界革命出过更高的力了——他统治着都柏林的工人，所握的权柄比爱尔兰资产阶级的还要大，虽说资产阶级名义上还骑在鞍上。农场雇工罢工的垮台，不必叫那些同志灰心——他们原指望去年十月在M——升起红旗，能干出一番大事业。加拉格尔同志至今尚未觉得时机已到，还不去叫破爱尔兰资产阶级的虚张声势。时候一到。……」

===Original===
In November a representative of the International Executive of the Revolutionary Organization was sent over from the Continent to make a special report on the situation in Ireland. The following is an extract from a secret report drawn up by him, after spending three months in Ireland secretly touring the country:

> “… For the moment it would be a tactical blunder to expel Comrade Gallagher from the International. At the same time there can be no doubt that the Irish Section has deviated entirely from the principles of revolutionary Communism as laid down in the laws of the International. Comrade Gallagher rules the national Organization purely and simply as a dictator. There is a semblance of an Executive Committee but only in name. The tactics are guided by whatever whim is uppermost in Comrade Gallagher’s mind at the moment. Contrary to the orders issued from Headquarters, the Organization is still purely military and has made hardly any attempt to come into the open as a legal political party. This is perhaps not entirely due to Comrade Gallagher’s fault. There are local causes, arising out of the recent struggle for national independence, which has left the working class in the grip of a romantic love of conspiracy, a strong religious and bourgeois-nationalist outlook on life and a hatred of constitutional methods. This makes it difficult for the moment to check Comrade Gallagher’s hold …”

===Chinese===
十一月，革命组织的国际执行委员会从欧洲大陆派来一名代表，专门就爱尔兰局势撰写报告。此人在爱尔兰各地秘密巡视了三个月，随后拟就一份秘密报告，以下是其中一段摘录：

> 「……眼下便把加拉格尔同志开除出国际，在战术上是一着错棋。但与此同时，爱尔兰支部已经全然背离了国际章程所定的革命共产主义原则，这一点毫无疑义。加拉格尔同志统治全国组织，彻头彻尾是独裁者做派。执行委员会徒具形骸，不过挂个名罢了。一切战术，全凭加拉格尔同志此刻脑子里最占上风的念头来摆布。组织违背总部下达的命令，至今仍旧是一支纯粹的军事力量，几乎没有做过半分努力，公开出面成为一个合法政党。这也许不能全怪加拉格尔同志。还有本土的成因，出自晚近的民族独立斗争——那场斗争使得工人阶级受制于三样东西：对密谋的浪漫痴恋，一种浓厚的宗教兼资产阶级民族主义的人生观，以及对宪政手段的憎恶。凡此种种，使得眼下一时难以遏制加拉格尔同志的掌控……」

## VII / 七

===Original===
Gallagher’s eyes had opened wide when the three men came into the room. Then they narrowed until they became thin slits under their long black lashes. He nodded to Mulholland and Connor. Then he stared at Gypo.

Gypo returned the stare. The two men, unlike in their features and bodies, were exactly alike in the impassivity of their stare. Gypo’s face was like a solid and bulging granite rock, impregnable but lacking that intelligence that is required by strength in order to be able to conquer men. Gallagher’s face was less powerful physically, but it was brimful of intelligence. The forehead was high and it seemed to surround the face. The eyes were large and wide apart. The nose was long and straight. The mouth was thin-lipped. The jaws were firm but slender and refined like a woman’s jaws. The whole face had absolutely no colour, but there was a constant movement in the cheeks, as if tiny streams were coursing irregularly beneath the smooth glossy skin. The hair was coal-black and cut close. The ears were large. The neck opened out gradually from the base of the shoulders on either side, like a hill disappearing into a plain.
===Chinese===
三个人走进屋子时，加拉格尔的眼睛睁大了。随后那双眼睛眯了起来，在长长的黑睫毛底下眯成两道细缝。他朝马尔霍兰德和康纳点了点头。然后他盯住吉波。

吉波回瞪过去。这两个人，容貌与身躯截然不同，对视时那份木然却一模一样。吉波的脸像一块坚硬鼓凸的花岗岩，固若金汤，却缺少力量为征服他人所必需的那种智性。加拉格尔的脸在体格上不那么雄强，却满蓄着智性。额头很高，仿佛把整张脸环抱在中间。眼睛大，两眼离得开。鼻子长而直。嘴是薄唇。下颌坚牢，却纤细文雅，像女人的下颌。整张脸毫无血色，可双颊却有一种持续不断的骚动，仿佛细小的溪流正在光滑油亮的皮肤底下无规则地奔流。头发乌黑，剪得极短。耳朵很大。脖颈从两侧肩膀的根部渐渐舒展开去，像一座山丘渐渐隐入平原。

===Original===
Then he jumped off his high stool and stood with his legs wide apart in front of Gypo. He was five feet eleven inches and a half in height, but Gypo towered over him with his extra two inches. Gallagher wore a loose brown raincoat, from his throat almost to his ankles, that made his well-built frame look larger and stouter. Yet Gypo, standing bare in his dungarees that were now almost sodden with rain, looked immense compared to him. Gallagher held his hands in his raincoat pockets thrust in front of his body, as if he were pointing pistols at Gypo. Gypo held his hands loosely by his sides, two vast red hands hanging limply from whitish round wrists. Gallagher wore a broad-brimmed black velour hat of a fashionable make. Gypo’s tattered little round hat was still perched on his skull, like a tiny school cap on an overgrown youngster.

They looked at one another, the one, handsome, well dressed, confident and indifferent; the other crude, ragged, amazed, anxious.
===Chinese===
随后他从高凳上一跃而下，叉开两腿站在吉波面前。他身高五英尺十一英寸半，可吉波凭着多出来的两英寸，居高临下地罩着他。加拉格尔穿一件宽松的棕色雨衣，从脖颈几乎垂到脚踝，把他那副结实的骨架衬得愈发高大粗壮。可吉波只穿一条粗布工装裤，几乎让雨水泡透了，相形之下仍显得巍然如山。加拉格尔双手插在雨衣口袋里朝身前顶着，仿佛正用两把手枪指着吉波。吉波两手松松地垂在身侧，两只巨大的红手从发白的圆腕子上无力地耷拉下来。加拉格尔戴一顶时新样式的黑丝绒宽边帽。吉波那顶破烂的小圆帽仍旧扣在脑壳上，像一个发育过快的少年头上扣着的一顶小学生便帽。

两人对望着：一个英俊、衣着讲究、自信而冷漠；一个粗野、褴褛、惊愕而焦灼。

===Original===
“Well, Gypo,” drawled Gallagher, in the irritating, contemptuous tone that he affected. “Ye don’t seem glad to see me.”

“Can’t say that I am,” replied Gypo curtly, almost without moving his lips. “I don’t see no reason to be glad to see ye, Commandant Gallagher. Ye were never a friend o’ mine, an’ I ain’t in the habit o’ crawlin’ on me belly to anybody that don’t like me. I’m not one o’ yer pet lambs any more, so ye needn’t do any bleatin’ as far as I’m concerned. One man is as good as another in this rotten ould world. I’m usin’ yer own words, amn’t I?”
===Chinese===
「哟，吉波，」加拉格尔拖长了腔调说，用的是他惯常装出来的那种惹人冒火、居高临下的口吻。「见了我，你好像不怎么高兴啊。」

「高兴谈不上，」吉波生硬地答道，嘴唇几乎没动。「我看不出有什么理由见了你该高兴，加拉格尔指挥官。你从来就不是我的朋友，我也没有趴在肚皮上、朝不喜欢我的人爬过去的习惯。我不再是你们的小宠羊羔了，所以在我这儿，你也用不着咩咩叫。在这个烂透了的旧世界里，一个人跟另一个人一样好。我这是拿你自己的话回敬你，没说错吧？」

===Original===
Gallagher laughed out loud, a merry laugh that showed his white teeth. He shrugged his shoulders and took a turn around the room. He took a packet of cigarettes from his pocket as he walked and selected one. He kept laughing until he paused to light the cigarette over near the stained-glass window.

“Yer a queer fish, Gypo,” he said, again laughing, as he paused to throw the used match into a spittoon.

Then he cast a glance all round the room and came back again to Gypo. Mulholland and Connor watched him all the time with that loving interest with which a crowd watches the movements of a champion boxer who is walking around the ring in his dressing-gown, preparatory to a big fight. They smiled when Gallagher laughed. They stopped smiling when he stopped laughing.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔放声大笑，笑得露出雪白的牙齿，一副快活样子。他耸了耸肩，在屋里踱了一圈，边走边从口袋里掏出一包香烟，抽出一支。他一直笑个不停，直到走到彩色玻璃窗附近停下点烟。

「你真是条怪鱼，吉波，」他说着又笑了，停下脚，把划过的火柴扔进痰盂。

接着他朝屋里四下扫了一眼，又回到吉波跟前。马尔霍兰德和康纳自始至终望着他，带着观众看拳王大战之前穿着晨衣绕场踱步的那份痴醉。加拉格尔笑，他们就跟着笑；加拉格尔不笑了，他们的笑也随即收住。

===Original===
Gypo, on the other hand, watched Gallagher’s movements angrily. He felt a desire to pounce on him and crush him to death before he could do any harm.

Then Gallagher came up to him and caught him by the right shoulder in a friendly and confidential manner.
===Chinese===
吉波却不然，他怒冲冲地盯着加拉格尔的一举一动。他心里生出一股欲望，想扑上去，趁他还没来得及作恶，就把他活活挤死。

这时加拉格尔走上前来，一副亲热而机密的模样，一把抓住他的右肩。

===Original===
“Listen Gypo,” he said. “You’ve got a grudge against me no doubt for getting you expelled from the Organization, but you have nobody to blame but yourself. I sent ye down, on the orders of the Executive Committee, you and Frank McPhillip, to look after the defence work of the strikers. What orders did I give the two of you? Can you remember? Well, I’ll remind you. To *keep off the booze and not to use the lead unless you were attacked*. But what did you do? The very first thing, the two of you got hold of two women. That, of course, must have been Frankie’s work because I don’t suppose you were ever a great magnet among the women. Women were Frankie’s weak spot, damn it. But anyhow, it doesn’t matter very much which of you started the hunt. You tasted the honey as well as he did it, as far as was reported to me. The two of you got drunk at M﻿—﻿— in company with these two women. You got so mad drunk that McPhillip went to shoot up the town. You might have assisted him in that pastime, but your time was occupied trying to pull a lamppost up by the roots in Oliver Plunket Street, for a bet of a gallon of stout. In the very middle of your entertainment, McPhillip met the secretary of the Farmers’ Union and shot him dead. That made you get over your drunkenness damn quick, didn’t it? The two of you bolted without making any attempt to cover your tracks. You ran like two hares. You came into Dublin with a red herring of a story about an attack and whatnot. It was a tall yarn. Well? D’ye know what I’m going to tell you, Gypo?”
===Chinese===
「听着，吉波，」他说。「你因为我把你开除出组织，心里记恨，这毫无疑问，可这怪不得别人，只能怪你自己。我奉执行委员会的命令派你们下去——你，还有弗兰克·麦克菲利普——去照管罢工工人的防卫工作。我给你们两个人下的什么命令？还记得吗？好吧，我提醒你。*不许沾酒，除非受到攻击，不许动枪*。可你们干了什么？头一件事，你们俩就弄到了两个女人。那当然准是弗兰基干的好事，因为我想你在女人堆里从来就算不上一块大磁铁。女人是弗兰基的死穴，妈的。不过话说回来，是谁先起的头，倒也无关紧要。据报到我这里来的，那口甜头你跟他一样尝了个够。你们俩在 M—— 陪着那两个女人喝得烂醉。醉得发了疯，麦克菲利普跑出去朝满城开枪。那桩余兴你本来兴许也搭过一手，可惜你的工夫全用在奥利弗·普伦基特街上了——打赌一加仑黑啤，看能不能把一根灯柱连根拔起。你们的余兴正酣，麦克菲利普撞上了农夫工会的书记，一枪把他打死了。这么一来，你们的酒醒得倒快得很，是不是？你们俩撒腿就跑，连一点掩盖行踪的打算都没有。跑得像两只野兔。回到都柏林，还编出一套什么遭到袭击之类的遮眼法鬼话。那可真是一篇天大的瞎话。怎么样？你知道我要跟你说什么吗，吉波？」

===Original===
He paused dramatically and looked Gypo closely in the eyes. Gypo never moved a muscle in his face. He grunted interrogatively from somewhere deep down in his chest. Gallagher continued very slowly:

“I’m going to tell you this much, Gypo. Only for me, you wouldn’t have got away with it as easily as you did that time. There were others who wanted to give you this, for disobeying orders.”

He moved his right hand suddenly beneath his raincoat, thrusting it forward against Gypo’s lower ribs. Gypo felt the contact of a blunt hard metal. He knew it was the muzzle of Gallagher’s Colt automatic pistol, but Gypo took no notice of the pistol. He was not afraid of the pistol. But he was afraid of Gallagher’s eyes into which he was looking steadily. He didn’t like them. They were so cold and blue and mysterious. Goodness knows what might be hidden behind them. His face began an irregular chaotic movement. His jaws, cheekbones, nose, mouth and forehead convulsed in opposite directions, as if a draught of wind had stolen in under the skin of his face and caused it to undulate. Then the face set again. The neck swelled and the little eyes bulged.
===Chinese===
他戏剧性地顿了顿，凑近盯着吉波的眼睛。吉波脸上连一块肌肉都没动。他从胸腔深处的什么地方发出一声探询的哼声。加拉格尔放得极慢地接着说：

「我就告诉你这么多，吉波。要不是我，那回你们脱身绝脱不了那么痛快。还有旁人想为你们抗命的事，给你这个呢。」

他猛地把右手伸到雨衣底下朝前一顶，抵住吉波的下肋。吉波感觉到一块钝而坚硬的金属贴上身来。他知道那是加拉格尔的柯尔特自动手枪的枪口，可吉波没把那枪当回事。他不怕枪。他怕的是加拉格尔的那双眼睛，他正直直地望进那双眼睛里。他不喜欢它们。它们那么冷，那么蓝，那么神秘。天知道那后面藏的是什么。他的脸开始一阵不规则的骚乱。下颌、颧骨、鼻子、嘴巴和额头朝着相反的方向抽搐，仿佛一股贼风钻进了脸皮底下，吹得它起伏不定。随后那张脸又定了下来。脖颈涨起，一双小眼睛鼓了出来。

===Original===
“No use tryin’ yer tricks on me, Danny Gallagher,” he growled, knocking the pistol muzzle away with a slight movement of his right hand. Although the blow was slight, it caused Gallagher to reel backwards two paces before he regained his balance. His face darkened for a moment and then again he broke into a smile. Gypo continued in a thunderous melancholy voice: “Gallagher, I got no use for you. Them’s all lies ye were tellin’ just now about tryin’ to save me life when I was before the Court of Inquiry last October. I know very well they was. Yerrah, are ye goin’ to tell me that yer not the chief boss an’ God knows what in the Organization? Who else has got any authority in it except yersel’? Yah. I got no use for ye. Yer a liar. Yer no good. An’ I’d be in my job yet in the police only for ye an’ yer soft talk. It was you that got me outa me job with yer promises o’ the Lord knows what. I declare to Almighty God that I done more for yer bloody Organization than any other man in Ireland. I done things that no man unhung could do. An’ ye went an’ threw me out on account of an ould farmer gettin’ plugged. Me an’ McPhillip. What did we get for it? Wha’﻿ ﻿… ye rotten﻿ ﻿…”

Gypo rambled off incoherently into a long string of blasphemous curses, raising his voice as he did so. His arms were raised outwards in a curve and his head was lowered, as if he were in the act of performing a swimming exercise. He frothed at the mouth and glared from one to the other of the three men, as if undecided which to attack first.
===Chinese===
「丹尼·加拉格尔，在我身上耍你那套把戏可没用，」他咆哮道，右手轻轻一挥，把枪口磕开。这一下虽轻，却打得加拉格尔踉跄着倒退两步，才重新站稳。他的脸阴沉了一瞬，随即又绽出笑容。吉波用一种雷鸣般忧郁的嗓音接着说：「加拉格尔，我看你一点用处也没有。你刚才净撒谎——什么去年十月我上调查法庭（Court of Inquiry）受审的时候，你想方设法救过我的命。我心里透亮得很，全是谎话。得了吧，你还想告诉我，说你在组织里不是大头目、不是天晓得的什么角色？除了你自己，那里面还有谁握着一点权？哼。我看不上你。你是个骗子。你不是个东西。要不是你和你那套甜话软语，我这会儿还在警察里当差呢。正是你，拿天知道什么样的许愿，把我从那份差事里哄了出来。我向全能的天主起誓，我为你那血淋淋的组织出的大力，爱尔兰随便哪个男人都比不上。我干过的事，还没上绞架的人都干不出来。可你就为了一条老农夫挨了枪，转身把我一脚踢了出去。我和麦克菲利普。我们落着什么好了？啥……你们这帮烂货……」

吉波颠三倒四地一路骂下去，骂出一长串渎神的咒骂，嗓门越骂越高。他双臂朝外弯举着，头低垂着，仿佛正在做一节游泳操。他嘴角挂着白沫，眼睛轮流瞪着那三个人，像是拿不定主意先扑哪一个。

===Original===
Then suddenly a little wooden panel in the wall to the right was raised up and a pretty red head was pushed through. It was Kitty the barmaid.

“Lord save us,” she cried, putting her fingers to her lips as she looked at Gypo. “Who is that fellah? What’s he doin’ here, Dan?”

“That’s all right, Kitty,” said Gallagher with a light laugh; “he’s a friend of mine. We are having a cursing competition.”

And he laughed heartily as he walked to the spittoon with the stub of his cigarette.
===Chinese===
忽然，右边墙上一块小木板推了上去，一颗漂亮的红头发脑袋探了进来。是酒吧女招待基蒂（Kitty）。

「老天爷保佑，」她叫道，一面望着吉波，一面把手指抵在唇上。「那家伙是谁？他在这儿干什么，丹？」

「没事，基蒂，」加拉格尔轻笑着说，「他是我的一个朋友。我们正举行骂人比赛呢。」

他捧着烟头朝痰盂走去，一路开怀大笑。

===Original===
Gypo turned around and looked at the terrified face of the barmaid. As he looked at her beautiful face and her pretty soft hair that shimmered in the artificial light, his head swam and his eyes went watery. His anger left his body immediately so that it seemed to empty and collapse. It had been rigid and like a tree. Now it became loose and jointless. He stood with stooping head and wondering eyes, looking at the barmaid.

The barmaid, seeing the change she had effected by her presence in the unruly giant, grew conceited. She smiled in a superior way and dabbed at her hair. She looked around at the others with an air of: “D’ye all see that now?”

Then Gallagher came up to the aperture jauntily, took her two hands in his and looked enticingly into her eyes. Her eyes winced for a moment as if she had become suddenly afraid. Then she smiled softly, wearily, like a woman passionately in love. Gallagher bent down his head and whispered something in her ear. She burst into a loud laugh. Gallagher smiled, listening to her. Then he suddenly sighed and rapped the counter curtly.
===Chinese===
吉波转过身，望着女招待那张惊惶的脸。他望着她那美丽的脸和她那头在灯光里闪着柔光的漂亮头发，脑袋一阵发晕，眼睛变得水汪汪的。他的怒气立刻离了体，整个人仿佛被抽空、瘫塌下去。方才他还僵硬得像棵树。此刻却松散下来，没了骨节。他垂着头站在那里，眼里带着惶惑，望着那女招待。

女招待眼见只凭自己一露面，就降住了这无法无天的巨汉，不由得自负起来。她带着优越感笑了笑，拢了拢头发。她朝旁人们环视一圈，那副神气像在说：「都瞧见了吧？」

随后加拉格尔轻快地走到窗口前，把她两只手都握在手里，含着勾引的意味望进她的眼睛。她的目光瑟缩了一下，仿佛忽然害怕起来。随即她温柔地、倦怠地笑了，像一个爱得炽烈的女人。加拉格尔俯下头，在她耳边低语了几句。她放声大笑起来。加拉格尔含笑听她笑。然后他忽然叹了口气，短促地敲了敲柜台。

===Original===
“Four glasses of Jameson’s quickly,” he said in a low sharp cold voice.

The barmaid stopped laughing as suddenly as if she had been stricken by a pain. She pulled down the shutter, lisping as she did so: “Yes Dan.”

Gallagher came back to Gypo and put his hand again on Gypo’s shoulder. Gypo had his two hands now in his trousers pockets. After his unsuccessful outburst he felt tired. He wanted to go away somewhere and lie down and sleep for days and days. His mind was in a maze. He was very tired. As he looked at Gallagher he even felt a longing to confide his secret to him. Gallagher’s eyes were so devilishly attractive. They seemed to draw things out of Gypo towards themselves. They would be able to form a plan and﻿ ﻿…

Gypo had uttered one syllable of Gallagher’s name before he realized the real identity of the man and the consequences of a confession to him. The name died on his lips. Gallagher smiled.
===Chinese===
「快，四杯詹姆森威士忌，」他低声说，嗓音尖利而冰冷。

女招待的笑声戛然而止，仿佛被一阵疼痛击中。她拉下窗板，一面咬着舌音说：「好的，丹。」

加拉格尔回到吉波身边，又把手搭在吉波的肩上。吉波这会儿两手插在裤袋里。发过那一场没有结果的火，他觉出疲乏来了。他想到什么地方去躺下，睡上几天几夜。他脑子里一团乱麻。他累极了。望着加拉格尔，他甚至生出一股渴望，想把秘密向他吐露。加拉格尔的那双眼睛，有那样一种魔鬼般的吸引力。它们仿佛正把吉波心底的东西朝它们自己那边拽。它们准能谋划出一个计划来，然后……

吉波刚吐出加拉格尔名字的第一个音节，就猛然醒悟了这人的真实身份，以及向他招供的后果。那个名字在唇上咽了回去。加拉格尔微微一笑。

===Original===
“Gypo, old boy,” he said in a friendly tone, “ye had better forget all that’s past. We’ve got something on hand that’s as much your business as ours. So we can act together on it. That’s why I sent Bartly Mulholland into McPhillip’s house to look for ye. A pal of yours has been done in by the police. D’ye hear? It looks like an informer’s job. We have to get that informer. It’s really no business of the Organization because Frank had ceased to be a member. He was only an ordinary civilian criminal as far as we are concerned. But an informer is an informer. He’s got to be wiped out like the first sign of a plague as soon as he’s spotted. He’s a common enemy. He’s got to be got, Gypo. And it’s up to you to give us a hand in tracking the traitor that sent your pal to his death. Because﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
「吉波，老伙计，」他用友善的口吻说，「过去的事，你最好统统忘掉。我们手头正有一件大事，与你有份，也与我们有份。咱们可以一起动手。我就是为这个，才派巴特利·马尔霍兰德上麦克菲利普家找你的。你的一个哥们儿让警察干掉了。听见没有？瞧那架势，是一桩告密的勾当。我们必须揪出那个告密者。这本不关组织的事，因为弗兰克早已不是成员。在我们看来，他不过是个普通的平民罪犯。可告密者就是告密者。他一露头，就得像瘟疫初起的头一个病兆一样，立刻扑灭。他是公敌。必须把他抓到，吉波。追查那个断送你哥们儿性命的叛徒，你有责任搭一把手。因为……」

===Original===
At that moment the slide was drawn up again sharply and the barmaid appeared at the aperture with four glasses of whisky on a tray. Gallagher went to the aperture, paid for the whisky, handed glasses to Connor and Mulholland, received his change, pinched the barmaid’s cheek and made her scream, laughed, pulled down the shutter himself and then advanced smiling to Gypo with a glass of whisky in each hand. He held out one glass to Gypo. Gypo stared at it without making any movement to take it or reject it.

He had followed all Gallagher’s movements with the stupid and suspicious wonder of a terrified wild animal that thinks some trick is being played on it. Now he stared at the glass as if he suspected some trick in that too.

“Take it,” said Gallagher coldly. “Take it, man, if you’ve any sense. It’s better to have me as a friend than as an enemy. If you are not going to help us in this job﻿ ﻿… er﻿ ﻿… people might think﻿ ﻿… er﻿ ﻿… that﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
正在这时，滑窗又猛地推了上去，女招待端着托盘出现在窗口，托盘上摆着四杯威士忌。加拉格尔走到窗前，付了酒钱，把酒杯递给康纳和马尔霍兰德，接了找头，捏了一把女招待的脸蛋，捏得她尖叫，笑了，亲手拉下窗板，然后一手端一杯威士忌，笑吟吟地朝吉波走来。他把其中一杯朝吉波递过去。吉波直瞪瞪地望着那杯酒，既不做动作去接，也不推拒。

他一直带着一种又蠢又疑的惊奇，盯着加拉格尔的一举一动，像一头吓坏了的野兽，疑心有人在对它耍花招。此刻他盯着那杯酒，仿佛疑心那里面也藏着花招。

「拿着，」加拉格尔冷冷地说。「拿着，伙计，你要是还有点脑子的话。让我做你的朋友，总好过做你的敌人。这件事你若不肯帮我们……呃……人家会想……呃……想……」

===Original===
“Uh,” began Gypo with a shrug of his whole body. Then he stopped panting. He went on, speaking at a very high pitch. “It’s not that but﻿ ﻿… Look here﻿ ﻿… It’s how﻿ ﻿…” His voice suddenly deepened into a hoarse shout, “It’s how I don’t know what I’m doin’.”

He stopped. Gallagher glanced at Mulholland. Mulholland’s cat’s eyes both winked imperceptibly.

“I’ve been starvin’ here for the past six months,” continued Gypo, suddenly breaking out into a torrent of words. He talked like a Negro, hollow, thunderous and melancholy. “I’ve been kicking about this town an’ every one o’ you fellahs I met passed me by without a word as if I never knew ye. I been over in the House there, livin’ from hand to mouth on whatever I could bum from sailors and pimps and dockers. I got no clothes. I got no money. I got nothin’. An’ then you come up all of a sudden with yer soft talk. Well﻿ ﻿… uh﻿ ﻿… how is it that﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
「呃，」吉波开口，整个身子耸了一下。随即他止住喘息，接着往下说，嗓门拔得极高。「不是那个，是……你听我说……是我……」他的声音忽然沉下去，成了一声嘶哑的吼：「是我不知道自己干的是什么。」

他住了口。加拉格尔瞥了马尔霍兰德一眼。马尔霍兰德那双猫眼，不易察觉地双双眨了一下。

「这六个月我在这儿挨饿，」吉波接着说，忽然迸出一大串话来。他讲话像个黑人，空洞、轰鸣、忧郁。「我在这城里晃荡，碰见的你们这帮家伙，一个个擦身而过，一句话也没有，好像我从来不认识你们。我一直待在那边那座宿舍里，靠从水手、皮条客和码头工那儿讨来的仨瓜俩枣，过一天算一天。我没有衣裳。我没有钱。我一无所有。然后你就冷不丁冒出来，带着你那套软话。那么……呃……这算怎么档子事……」

===Original===
He came to a stop once more with his chest heaving. He seemed to be about to go into a rage once more, but suddenly Gallagher moved closer to him and whispered gently and soothingly:

“Look here, Gypo. I’m going to make a fair deal with you. I’ll admit you have done a lot for the movement. You have paid the penalty during the last six months for the dangerous position you placed the whole Organization in last October. We’ll call that quits on one condition. If you can give us a clue to the man that informed on Francis Joseph McPhillip I’ll get ye taken back again into the Organization at yer old job on Headquarters Staff. Here. Take this drink.”
===Chinese===
他又停住了，胸膛起伏着。他似乎又要发作一场，可加拉格尔突然凑近他，轻柔地、抚慰地低声道：

「听我说，吉波。我要跟你做一笔公平交易。我承认，你为运动出过不少力。去年十月你把整个组织置于险地，这六个月你也受了罚了。咱们就算一笔勾销——只消一个条件。你要是能给我们一条线索，找到那个告发弗朗西斯·约瑟夫·麦克菲利普的人，我就设法让你重新回到组织里去，回总部参谋部（Headquarters Staff）干你的老差事。来。把这杯酒喝了。」

===Original===
Gypo’s hand shot out immediately. He grasped the glass and Gallagher’s hand both together in his immense paw. The two men almost struggled trying to disengage their hands. As soon as the glass was free Gypo put it to his lips and drained it. Then he stalked slowly over to the mantelpiece and placed the empty glass on it. With his back to his companions he paused to wipe his mouth with his sleeve.

He wanted time to compose himself. Gallagher’s proposal had taken him so completely by surprise that he was beside himself. Since that infernal moment when he kicked open the door of the police-station, his whole life had been submerged in a pitch-black cloud that was impenetrable and offered no escape. He had been alone, outcast, encompassed by a universal horde of enemies. Now, suddenly, he was offered a means of escape by the great Gallagher himself. Gallagher, the great Gallagher, had made him an offer. He would get back again into the Organization. Again people would be afraid of him. Again clever men would be always at hand to make plans for him, to provide him with money for doing daring things, to protect him, to praise his recklessness, his strength and his﻿ ﻿… Mother of Mercy! What luck!

As he wiped his mouth on his sleeve at the counter an insane idea struck him, such was his eagnerness to qualify immediately for readmission to the Organization. For a moment he contemplated the man who had gone into the police-station as a being apart from himself. Sound began to gurgle up his throat. It was an attempt on the part of his present personality to speak and deliver information against that dazed Gypo Nolan who had stumbled into the police-station. But the sound froze in his throat, in a ball, hurting him as if his tonsils had swollen suddenly. He realized that he himself was one with that ponderous fellow, wearing a little tattered round hat, who had gone into the police-station. It was only another artifice on the part of something within him, his conscience maybe, to persuade him to make a confession of his betrayal.
===Chinese===
吉波的手立刻射了出去。他那只巨掌一把攥住，连酒杯带加拉格尔的手一起攥在掌心。两个人几乎挣扎了一番才把手抽开。酒杯一得脱，吉波便举到唇边一饮而尽。随后他迈着大步慢慢踱到壁炉架前，把空杯搁在上面。他背对着伙伴们，停了停，用袖子抹了抹嘴。

他需要时间定一定神。加拉格尔的提议太出于他意料，把他整个人击懵了。打从踹开警察局大门那个恶魔时刻起，他的一生就一直浸在一片漆黑的浓云里，那云密不透光，也无路可逃。他一直孤身一人，弃于人间，被天下乌泱泱的一伙仇敌团团围着。此刻，忽然之间，伟大的加拉格尔竟亲手递给他一条生路。加拉格尔，了不起的加拉格尔，向他开了口。他将重新回到组织里去。人们又会怕他。那些聪明人又会随时守在旁边，替他定计划，给他钱去干胆大包天的事，护着他，夸他的亡命、他的力气，还有他的……仁慈的圣母啊！这是何等的运气！

他在柜台边用袖子抹嘴的当口，一个疯狂的念头击中了他——他急着立刻够上重新入会的资格，竟到了这般地步。有一瞬间，他把那个走进警察局去的人，当作另一个存在来打量。一个声音开始在他喉咙里咕咕上涌。那是他此刻这个人格在试图开口，出首告发那个跌跌撞撞闯进警察局去的、失魂落魄的吉波·诺兰。可那声音在他喉咙里冻成了一个球，折磨着他，仿佛他的扁桃体突然肿了起来。他意识到，他自己与那个戴着一顶破烂小圆帽、走进警察局去的笨重家伙，本是一体。那不过是他体内某个东西——也许是他的良心——耍的又一个伎俩，要诱他把叛卖的事招认出来。

===Original===
That same impulse had confused him all the time that he was looking at Mrs. McPhillip.

And then, just as in the public-house, when he had been terrified by Katie Fox, his mind had given birth to an insane plan about a sailor in a tavern, so now also his mind conceived an amazing fabrication. It entered his brain suddenly, like a thunderstorm, with noise and fury. His face and eyes lit up. He opened his mouth. He walked over to Gallagher quickly and spoke in a hissing whisper.

“I’ll tell ye who informed,” he gasped. “It’s the Rat Mulligan. It’s him as sure as Christ was crucified.”

The three men gathered up close to him. They all looked behind them suspiciously and then stared at him with narrowed eyes. There was a moment of tense silence. Then each drew a deep breath. Connor slipped his finger over the trigger of his revolver.

“The Rat Mulligan!” exclaimed Gallagher at length. “How d’ye make that out, Gypo?”

“I’ll tell ye,” cried Gypo triumphantly. Then he paused again and looked about him with furrowed brows dramatically. “I didn’t like to say anythin’ mesel’ for reasons that everybody knows. A man can never be sure of a thing like that. An’ God knows it’s a quare charge to bring agin a man. But as ye put it the way ye put it, Commandant, about him bein’ me pal an’ me duty to the Cause, well﻿ ﻿… Still! Poor Mulligan!”
===Chinese===
在他望着麦克菲利普太太（Mrs. McPhillip）的那整段时间里，搅得他心神不宁的，正是这同一种冲动。

接着，正如先前在酒馆里，他被凯蒂·福克斯吓破了胆，脑子里生出一个关于酒馆里一个水手的疯狂计划一样，此刻他的脑子又孕育出一个惊人的编造。它像一场雷雨，带着喧声与狂怒，骤然灌进他的脑际。他的脸和眼睛亮了起来。他张开嘴，快步走向加拉格尔，用嘶嘶的耳语说起话来。

「我告诉你谁告的密，」他喘着气说。「是『耗子』穆利根（the Rat Mulligan）干的。就是他，就像基督被钉上十字架一样千真万确。」

三个人紧紧围拢过来。他们全都疑心地朝身后看了看，然后眯起眼睛盯住他。有一瞬紧张的死寂。随后每个人都深吸了一口气。康纳的手指悄悄扣上了左轮枪的扳机。

「『耗子』穆利根！」加拉格尔终于叫出声来。「你凭什么这样断定，吉波？」

「我这就告诉你，」吉波得意洋洋地喊道。随即他又顿住，皱起眉头，戏剧性地朝四下里望了望。「我一直不肯自己开口，原因大家都清楚。这种事，谁也断不准。上帝知道，往一个人头上安这么一桩罪名，可是件玄乎事。可你既然把话说到这个份上，指挥官，说什么他是我的哥们儿、我负着对事业的本分，那么……罢了！可怜的穆利根！」

===Original===
“Oh, come on,” cried Gallagher twitching with excitement “Get finished with what you have to say. Make your statement, man.”

But Gypo was not to be hurried. An amazing arrogance had taken possession of him. He reached out towards the glass of whisky that Gallagher still held untasted in his hand.
===Chinese===
「哦，快说吧，」加拉格尔喊道，激动得直抽动。「把要说的说完。把你的供词摆出来，伙计。」

可吉波不容催促。一种惊人的傲慢已经攫住了他。他伸手去够加拉格尔手里那杯还没沾唇的威士忌。

===Original===
“Gimme that, Commandant,” he said, “seein’ as yer not tastin’ it.” Gallagher nervously handed him the drink. “Thanks. Here’s luck. Ah! Good stuff that. Well. This is how it was. Just after Frankie left me in the dining-room, I suddenly thought to mesel’ that I had better run after him and try an’ head him off from goin’ home. I had been tryin’ to make him clear out of town again an’ not go near Titt Street, but the same cranky fellah that he always was wouldn’t listen to a word of what I said. So that I said to mesel’, Lord have mercy on him, ‘Well, me fine fellah, I’m not goin’ to get mesel’ into a fever, tryin’ to keep ye outa harm’s way an’ get cursed upside down for doin’ so.’ Well anyway, as soon as he had gone, I decided to follow him and give him a last shout. I ran out into the hall an’ who do I see but the Rat sneakin’ around the corner. I ran down the hall. There was the Rat at the door with his hands in his overcoat pockets peerin’ up the lane. Then he dived out into the street, I chased after him. I was just in time to see Frankie turnin’ the corner into the road with the Rat crawlin’ after him. It’s as clear as daylight. So it is. Lord have mercy on the dead, if I had only thought of it at the time, Frankie might have been alive at this minute instead o’ bein’ a frozen corpse. Give us another drink, Commandant. Me throat is parched.”
===Chinese===
「给我吧，指挥官，」他说，「反正你也不喝。」加拉格尔神经质地把酒递给了他。「多谢。祝走运。啊！好酒。是这样。事情的原委是这样。弗兰基刚在饭厅里离开我，我突然心里寻思，最好追上去，拦住他，别让他回家。我一直劝他再出城去躲躲，别走近蒂特街，可他还是那副倔脾气，我的话一个字也听不进。于是我心里说，主啊可怜他吧：『得嘞，我的好伙计，我总不能为了让你躲灾避祸、反倒挨你一顿狗血淋头的臭骂，把自己急出一场热病来。』反正，他一走，我就打定主意跟出去，最后喊他一嗓子。我跑进过道，一眼看见谁了？耗子正绕着墙角鬼鬼祟祟地蹭。我顺着过道跑下去。耗子就站在门口，两手插在外衣口袋里，朝巷子那头张望。随后他一头窜上街，我在后面追。我正赶上看个正着：弗兰基拐过街角进了那条路，耗子贴在他后面爬也似的溜过去。这事明白得跟白昼一样。真的。主啊怜悯亡者吧，我当时要是想到了，弗兰基这会儿兴许还活着，而不是成了一具冻僵的尸首。再给我们来一杯，指挥官。我喉咙冒烟了。」

===Original===
Without a word or a glance Gallagher walked up to the counter and rapped at the aperture. Gypo did not even condescend to follow his movements. His conceit was now boundless. He realized that he himself was amazingly cunning. He even felt a contempt for Gallagher in his mind. As for Mulholland and Connor﻿ ﻿… He glanced at them appraisingly, as a man might glance at a useful pair of dogs. It was the same kind of glance that Gallagher was in the habit of directing towards everybody.

Gallagher brought a fresh glass of whisky and handed it to him. He took it without a word of thanks. He walked to the spittoon and emptied his mouth into it. Then he swallowed the drink again at one draught. He put the empty glass on the mantelpiece and coughed deeply. He clasped his hands behind his back with a loud sound. He began to balance himself backwards and forwards on his heels like a policeman.

“How didn’t I think of it before?” he cried, looking thoughtfully at the ceiling.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔一言不发、一眼不看地走到柜台前，敲了敲窗口。吉波甚至不屑于追随他的动作。他此刻的自负已无边无际。他意识到他自己惊人地狡猾。他心里甚至对加拉格尔生出几分鄙夷。至于马尔霍兰德和康纳……他朝他们估量地瞥了一眼，像一个人瞥一双顶用的狗。那正是加拉格尔惯于朝所有人投去的同一类目光。

加拉格尔端来一杯新斟的威士忌，递给他。他接过，一句道谢也没有。他走到痰盂前，把嘴里的东西吐净，随后又一口把酒灌了下去。他把空杯搁上壁炉架，沉沉地咳了一声。他啪的一声把双手扣到背后，开始像警察那样，用脚跟一起一落地前后摇晃着身子。

「我先前怎么就没想到呢？」他喊道，若有所思地望着天花板。

===Original===
He was completely immersed now in the contemplation of his own cleverness. He did not notice the utter silence with which his story had been received by Gallagher and the other two men. He was contemplating with pleasure the old days, when he had a criminal in his charge, in the cells, at the police-station. He used to stand for a whole hour in the stillness of the night, baiting the prisoner, terrorizing him with his eyes, with a sudden display of strength, with a mad laugh, with silent staring. He was feeling that same sensation now. Exhilarated by the whisky he had drunk and carried away by the concentrated nerve strain of the past few hours, he imagined that he had Gallagher and the other two men at his mercy, that he was a policeman and that they were civilians who were asking a favour of him, an illegal favour that put them in his power. It was just that way in the old days, when he used to sell Gallagher little titbits of information over a drink; little harmless, he thought, bits of information, about headquarters routine and the disposition of the detective-force personnel.

“Think of what before?” Gallagher remarked coldly.

He spoke slowly and casually, looking at Gypo in a brooding way.

“Why, I mean the grudge that the Rat had in for Frankie,” Gypo replied confidentially and with an air of great importance.
===Chinese===
此刻他完全沉浸在对自己聪明的回味里。加拉格尔和另外两个人以彻骨的沉默听着他的故事，他竟毫无察觉。他正快意地回味着旧日时光——那时他在警察局，在牢房里看守犯人。他惯于在夜的死寂中一站整点钟，撩拨那囚犯，用眼睛、用突然亮出一手力气、用一声狂笑、用无言的瞪视，把他吓个半死。此刻他感到的正是当年那股滋味。灌下肚的威士忌给他壮着兴，过去几个钟头紧绷的神经又推着他往前涌，他恍惚觉得，加拉格尔和另外两个人都攥在他手心里，他是警察，他们是求他办事的平民——求的是一桩非法的恩情，一桩把他们置于他掌中的恩情。旧日正是这个样子：那时他惯于一面喝酒，一面卖给加拉格尔一些小道消息；那些消息，他想，无伤大雅，无非是总部例行公事和侦探队人马布置的零碎。

「先前没想到什么？」加拉格尔冷冷地说。

他说话又慢又随便，用一种阴沉沉盘算的目光望着吉波。

「嗐，我是说耗子对弗兰基怀的那份仇，」吉波答道，一副交底的口吻，架子里带着了不得的分量。

===Original===
“What grudge are you referring to?”

“Oh, it’s a long story,” said Gypo with a sigh, as he walked over to the spittoon and spat into it. Then he hitched up his trousers. He cleared his throat with a tremendous noise. It was very tantalizing. “Stand us another drink, Commandant, before they close,” he cried suddenly, with amazing nonchalance.

“By the lumpin’ Moses!” ejaculated Gallagher. “You’re a cool customer, Gypo. Ha, ha, ha! Well now! You’re worth another drink anyway.”

He winked secretly at Mulholland and Connor as he walked over to the aperture. Gypo called after him almost contemptuously.

“Hurry up,” he said, as he looked at the clock with a scowling face, “we only have another minute. It’s a minute to eleven.”
===Chinese===
「你说的是哪门子仇？」

「哦，那可说来话长，」吉波叹了口气说，一面踱到痰盂前，朝里啐了一口。随后他提了提裤子，又惊天动地地清了清嗓子。吊人胃口吊足了。「趁没打烊，再请我们喝一轮吧，指挥官，」他突然喊道，那份若无其事真叫人吃惊。

「凭摩西老爷起誓！」加拉格尔脱口叫道。「你这家伙可真是面不改色，吉波。哈，哈，哈！好啊！不管怎么说，再请你喝一杯也值。」

他朝窗口走去，一路朝马尔霍兰德和康纳暗暗递眼色。吉波在他背后喊，口气里几乎带着轻蔑。

「快点，」他说，皱着眉看钟，「咱们只剩一分钟了。差一分就十一点了。」

===Original===
Again four glasses of whisky were passed around. Gypo took his and swallowed it at a draught. This time he took the glass from Gallagher’s hand without asking for it. He swallowed that also at a draught, as if he were going through a public exhibition of his drinking powers. Mulholland and Connor swallowed their drinks hurriedly, as if they were afraid that he was going to take theirs too. He walked over to the mantelpiece and put the two empty glasses on it. He looked at the five glasses he had emptied and smiled broadly. He whacked his chest with a loud sound.

“Come on now, comrade,” said Gallagher sharply, “out with your news. No fooling.”

“All right,” said Gypo, thrusting forward his huge head so that it looked like a battering-ram, suddenly attached to his collarbone. “D’ye remember the Rat’s sister Susie? She used to be a member o’ the Organization. She﻿—”

“All right,” snapped Gallagher angrily. “I remember her. What about her? What has she got to do with it?”
===Chinese===
又一轮四杯威士忌传了开来。吉波接过自己那杯，一口灌了下去。这一回他连招呼也不打，径自从加拉格尔手里把酒杯拿了过来。那一杯他也是一口灌下，仿佛在当众表演他的海量。马尔霍兰德和康纳慌忙把各自的酒吞了，像是生怕他连他们那两杯也端走。他踱到壁炉架前，把两只空杯搁上去。他望着自己喝空的五只杯子，咧开大嘴笑了，又砰砰地捶了两下胸膛。

「好了，同志，」加拉格尔厉声道，「把你的消息倒出来。别耍花样。」

「行，」吉波说着，把那颗大脑袋朝前一探，看着像一具忽然焊在锁骨上的攻城槌。「你记得耗子的妹妹苏茜（Susie）吧？她从前也是组织的成员。她——」

「行了，」加拉格尔怒冲冲地厉声道。「我记得她。她怎么着？她跟这事有什么相干？」

===Original===
“Well, why wouldn’t she have a lot to do with it? She had a baby, didn’t she? Didn’t she leave﻿—”

“What d’you know about her baby?” hissed Gallagher. He was deadly pale.

“Don’t get yer rag out, Commandant,” leered Gypo with a broad laugh. He was slightly drunk and insolent. “Hit a sore spot, wha’? Well, I don’t know anythin’ about that. Ye can set yer mind at rest. Frank McPhillip was the father o’ that kid an’ he refused to marry her. I remember me an’ him were at the back o’ Cassidy’s havin’ a pint one night, when somebody came in an’ asked Frankie to step around the corner a minute. He was gone a long time so I followed him, suspectin’ that there might be a bit o’ foul play. But I found him an’ Susie jawin’ away to beat the band. She was cryin’ an’ askin’ him to take her away with him somewhere. O’ course he didn’t budge. Next day she went to the ’Pool. Gone on Lime Street, as far as I can hear. Well! You bet yer life that’s why the Rat did it. That’s why he informed.”
===Chinese===
「嘿，怎么会不相干？她生了个孩子，不是吗？她不是离开——」

「她那孩子，你知道些什么？」加拉格尔嘶声问。他脸色死一般苍白。

「别冒火嘛，指挥官，」吉波斜着眼咧嘴大笑。他微醺了，放肆起来。「戳着痛处了，是吧？嗐，那档子事我一概不知。你尽管放心。弗兰克·麦克菲利普就是那孩子的爹，可他不肯娶她。我记得有天夜里，我和他正在卡西迪酒馆后头喝一品脱，有人进来，请弗兰基到拐角去一趟。他去了老半天，我疑心要出什么奸计，就跟了过去。却看见他和苏茜吵得昏天黑地。她哭着，求他带她到什么远远的地方去。他当然纹丝不动。第二天她就去了利物浦。据我所闻，在莱姆街（Lime Street）上堕入风尘了。嘿！你拿性命打赌，耗子干的就是这个。他告密就为的这个。」

===Original===
Gallagher looked at Mulholland. Mulholland wrinkled his forehead and shook his head slightly. Then he looked at Gypo curiously. Connor’s mouth was wide open and there was a look of wonder in his eyes as he gaped at Gypo. Gypo was tightening his trousers belt.

“Well, Commandant,” he said, when he had finished, “Yer word holds good about takin’ me back into the Organization?”

“Steady on,” murmured Gallagher dreamily, staring at the ground. “We have to verify your statement first. If your statement is true you’ll get back all right.” Suddenly he looked up, smiling, with sparkling eyes. He seized Gypo by the right hand and smiled into his face in a friendly intimate way. “Listen. There’s a Court of Inquiry tonight at half-past one. Be there. Mulholland will take you up there. You can arrange to meet him somewhere. You can rely on me, comrade, to fix you up again. You did good work before, comrade, and you’ll do good work again for the liberation of your class.”
===Chinese===
加拉格尔望向马尔霍兰德。马尔霍兰德皱起眉头，微微摇了摇头。随后他好奇地打量吉波。康纳张大了嘴，目瞪口呆地望着吉波，眼里满是惊奇。吉波正在勒紧裤带。

「那么，指挥官，」他勒完了，说道，「你许下的带我回组织的话，算数不算数？」

「别急，」加拉格尔望着地面，恍恍惚惚地喃喃道。「我们得先核实你的供词。你的供词若是真的，你自然回得去。」他忽然抬起头，含着笑，眼睛发亮。他抓住吉波的右手，友好而亲热地朝他的脸笑道：「听着。今晚一点半有一个调查法庭。你要到场。马尔霍兰德会带你去。你们可以约个地方碰头。你尽管信我，同志，我保你重新安排妥当。你从前出过力，同志，为了你那个阶级的解放，你还会再出力的。」

===Original===
Gypo gripped Gallagher’s hand and squeezed it eagerly. Then he clicked his heels and saluted in a grandiose fashion. Then he turned to Mulholland.

“I’ll be at Biddy Burke’s place,” he whispered; “about one o’clock. I’ll see ye there.”

“Right ye are,” answered Mulholland.

“Good night, boys,” cried Gypo in a loud hearty voice.

Then he stalked out of the room, striking the floor with his heels fiercely and clearing his throat.

They all looked after him in silence for two seconds. Then somebody called, “Time, gentlemen, time.” Gallagher started.
===Chinese===
吉波攥住加拉格尔的手，热切地紧握。随后他咔的一声并拢脚跟，煞有介事地行了个礼。接着他转向马尔霍兰德。

「我在碧蒂·伯克家，」他低声道，「一点钟光景。咱们那儿见。」

「就这么说定，」马尔霍兰德答道。

「晚安，弟兄们，」吉波朗声喊道，嗓音洪亮热诚。

随后他昂首阔步走出屋去，鞋跟狠狠地砸着地板，一路清着嗓子。

他们三人默默望着他的背影，足有两秒钟。随后有人喊：「时间到了，先生们，时间到了。」加拉格尔猛地一惊。

===Original===
“Well, I’ll be damned,” he cried, striking his left hand into his right.

“It’s him,” hissed Connor, rushing up to Gallagher with open mouth.

“Shut up, you fool,” snapped Gallagher.

“Listen, Commandant,” cried Mulholland excitedly; “it’s him. I’ll swear it is, because﻿—”

“Damn you,” snarled Gallagher, “who is asking your opinion? Give me your report. Quick, quick. Don’t make a song of it.”

In short jerky statements, with rapid gestures, Mulholland described all that had happened at No. 44 Titt Street, Gypo’s excitement, the falling of the money to the floor, Gypo’s giving it to Mrs. McPhillip, his rush from the house. Then suddenly he began in a whining voice to recount all he had done since he had been mobilized at eight o’clock on receipt of the news of Francis McPhillip’s death. But Gallagher cut him short.
===Chinese===
「嘿，我真是见鬼了，」他喊道，左手一拳击进右掌。

「是他，」康纳嘶声道，张着嘴扑向加拉格尔。

「闭嘴，你这蠢货，」加拉格尔厉声道。

「听我说，指挥官，」马尔霍兰德激动地喊道；「是他。我敢发誓是他，因为——」

「见你的鬼，」加拉格尔狺声骂道，「谁问你的高见了？报告。快，快。别唱起戏来。」

马尔霍兰德用短促的句子、飞快的手势，把蒂特街44号发生的一切描绘了一遍：吉波的失态，钱掉在地板上，吉波把钱塞给麦克菲利普太太，他从屋里夺路而出。接着他忽然用一种哭腔，絮絮叨叨地讲起自己自八点钟接到弗朗西斯·麦克菲利普的死讯、奉命集合以来所做的一切。可加拉格尔打断了他。

===Original===
“Cut that out,” he cried. “Did the police find any papers at No. 44? No. Good. Was anything found on the body? You don’t know. Well, you better find out tomorrow at the inquest. Now beat it. Keep at Gypo’s heels like a pot of glue. Find out every damn thing you can. Bring him along sharp to the Bogey Hole at one-thirty. Off.”

Mulholland disappeared without a word. Gallagher turned to Connor.

“You Connor. Mobilize six men of your section. Round up Mulligan. Get him to the Bogey Hole. Get busy.”

Connor mumbled something and disappeared.

Gallagher remained staring at the ground, alone, lost in thought. Drunken voices were singing in the next compartment. Feet were shuffling. A droning voice cried constantly: “Time, please, gentlemen, time.”
===Chinese===
「那些打住，」他喊道。「警察在44号搜出什么文件没有？没有。好。尸身上搜出什么没有？你不知道。那么，明天验尸的时候，你去打听清楚。现在滚吧。像一罐胶水似的粘住吉波的脚后跟。能挖出什么就都给我挖出来。一点半准时把他带到博吉洞（Bogey Hole）去。去吧。」

马尔霍兰德一言不发地消失了。加拉格尔转向康纳。

「康纳，你。把你小队的六个人动员起来。把穆利根抓来。带到博吉洞去。动手。」

康纳咕哝了一句什么，也消失了。

加拉格尔独自留下，盯着地面出神。隔壁的分隔间里，醉汉的嗓音在唱歌。脚步拖沓着。一个瓮声瓮气的嗓音在不断喊：「时间到了，请吧，先生们，时间到了。」

===Original===
Gallagher’s eyes distended dreamily. He sighed.

“The least little rift,” he murmured to himself, “and everything is burst open. Then it’s all up with me. I’ve got to stamp out this damned informer whoever he is. It may be Gypo. It might be the Rat, though that’s very doubtful. That’s of no consequence. What is of consequence is the fact that there is an informer.﻿ ﻿… Good God! An informer is the great danger. Every man’s hand is against me. It’s only fear that protects me. I must make an example of this fellow.”

His voice had gradually died out. Now silence reigned in the room again. The room was hot and stifling, full of the smell of stale drink and tobacco. He stared at the floor.

A cockroach peered out of its hole, contemplated a blotch of drink four inches away from its snout and then disappeared again. It would come out later on and suck the blotch.

The distance was full of sound as if many things were happening there.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔的眼睛恍恍惚惚地睁大。他叹了口气。

「哪怕裂开最小的一丝缝，」他喃喃自语，「一切就都崩开了。那我就全完了。这个该死的告密者，不管他是谁，我都得把他扑灭。可能是吉波。也可能是耗子——虽然那很靠不住。这不打紧。要紧的是：有这么一个告密者。……老天爷！告密者是心腹大患。人人的手都与我作对。只有恐惧护得住我。我必须拿这家伙杀一儆百。」

他的声音渐渐逝去。屋里重又归于寂静。屋里闷热窒息，满是残酒和烟草的气味。他盯着地板。

一只蟑螂从洞里探出头来，端详着离它口鼻四英寸远的一摊酒渍，又缩了回去。待会儿它还会再出来，把那摊酒渍吮干。

远处充满声响，仿佛那里正发生着许多事情。

===Original===
Then Gallagher raised his head with a start. He sighed and walked rapidly over to the aperture. He tapped the panel with his knuckles. It was raised up almost immediately. The pretty red head appeared. Gallagher nodded. The red head disappeared again and the slide was pulled down. Gallagher waited. After three seconds a little door to the left was opened quietly and the barmaid stepped into the room, shutting the door carefully behind her. She rushed immediately to Gallagher and threw her arms around his neck. He kissed her lips several times rapidly. Then he unwound her arms.

“Got anything for me?” he asked.

She nodded and took a piece of paper from within the breast of her black dress. He stuck it within his raincoat.

“Right,” he muttered dreamily.
===Chinese===
这时加拉格尔猛地抬起头。他叹了口气，快步走向窗口，用指节敲了敲木板。木板几乎立刻推了上去。那颗漂亮的红头发脑袋露了出来。加拉格尔点了点头。红脑袋又缩了回去，滑窗拉了下来。加拉格尔等着。三秒钟后，左边一扇小门轻轻开了，女招待闪进屋来，随手小心地带上门。她立刻扑向加拉格尔，双臂搂住他的脖子。他对着她的嘴唇急促地亲了几下，随后把她的手臂解了开来。

「有东西给我吗？」他问。

她点点头，从黑连衣裙的怀里掏出一张纸。他把它塞进雨衣里。

「好，」他恍恍惚惚地咕哝道。

===Original===
Then he kissed her again on the lips and patted her cheek. He took a pace away, but she grabbed at him. She held him, looking beseechingly into his face.

“Have ye got nothin’ to say to me, Dan?” she whispered, almost sobbing.

“For goodness’ sake, Kitty, have sense,” he muttered savagely. “This is no time for jig-acting.” He put a finger to his throat. “I’m up to here in it. The whole Organization is in danger.”

“O Lord! What is it, Dan? Tell me.”

“An informer. See ye tomorrow. Let me go. Good night.”

He kissed her on the forehead. Her arms loosened. He was gone. She looked after him dejectedly. Then she shivered and gripped her breasts.
===Chinese===
随后他又吻了吻她的嘴唇，拍了拍她的脸颊。他迈开一步要走，她却一把抓住他。她挽着他，一脸恳求地望着他。

「你就没有一句话跟我说吗，丹？」她低声道，几乎要哭出来。

「看在老天份上，基蒂，讲点道理，」他野声野气地咕哝道。「现在不是调情胡闹的时候。」他把一根手指横在喉咙上。「我已经陷到这儿了。整个组织都在危险里。」

「哦，主啊！出了什么事，丹？告诉我。」

「一个告密者。明天见。放开我。晚安。」

他吻了吻她的额头。她的手臂松开了。他走了。她怏怏地望着他去的方向。随后她打了个寒噤，双手捂住了胸口。

===Original===
Gallagher walked up Titt Street. Here and there a workman recognized him and saluted respectfully. He did not acknowledge the salutes. He wheeled sharply in at the door of No. 44 and knocked. The door was opened almost immediately by Mary McPhillip. She also started and put her hand to her breast when she saw him.

“Good evening, Mary,” he said gently, holding out his hand. “May I come in? I want to speak to your mother.”

“Yes,” said Mary excitedly; “mother is in the kitchen, but you had better come into the parlour. Father is in the kitchen, too, and there would surely be a row if he saw you.”

“Oh, that’s all right,” said Gallagher. “Is there anybody else there?”

“No, everybody else is gone.”
===Chinese===
加拉格尔沿蒂特街走去。一路上不时有工人认出他，恭恭敬敬地行礼。他没有答礼。他一个急转，拐进44号的门，敲了敲门。门几乎立刻开了，开门的是玛丽·麦克菲利普（Mary McPhillip）。她一见他，也是一惊，手按住了胸口。

「晚上好，玛丽，」他温和地说，伸出手来。「我能进来吗？我想跟你母亲说几句话。」

「行，」玛丽激动地说；「妈妈在厨房，可你最好进客厅来。爸爸也在厨房，他要是一看见你，准得吵翻天。」

「哦，没关系，」加拉格尔说。「那里还有别人吗？」

「没有了，旁的人都走了。」

===Original===
“Who is that yer talkin’ to, Mary?” came Jack McPhillip’s voice from the kitchen.

“Nobody atall, father,” cried Mary.

“Don’t I hear a man’s voice,” cried the father. “Who is he?”

“Hist! It’s all right,” whispered Gallagher, pushing past her as she tried to speak again. “He won’t bite me. It’s just me, Mr. McPhillip. How are you? I’m very sorry to hear of your trouble.”
===Chinese===
「玛丽，你在跟谁说话？」厨房里传来杰克·麦克菲利普（Jack McPhillip）的声音。

「没跟谁，爸爸，」玛丽喊道。

「我不是听见一个男人的声音吗，」父亲喊道。「他是谁？」

「嘘！没事的，」加拉格尔低声说，趁她还想开口，从她身边挤了过去。「他咬不了我。是我呀，麦克菲利普先生。您好吗？听说您遭了难，我很难过。」

===Original===
The two of them met at the kitchen door. They stared at one another for a moment. Then Gallagher made a movement to come forward and McPhillip with a little start, moved backwards. He did not speak until he was near the bed again.

“Oh, it’s you, is it?” he said angrily. “An’ what brings you here at this hour of the night?”

Gallagher took no notice of him. He turned to Mrs. McPhillip who was still in the same position by the fire, telling her rosary beads.

“I am sorry to trouble you, Mrs. McPhillip,” he said gently and respectfully, “in the middle of your﻿ ﻿… eh﻿ ﻿… but there’s a question or two I have to ask you for the sake of him that’s dead. Would you be kind enough to﻿—”
===Chinese===
两人在厨房门口撞了个对面。他们对望了片刻。随后加拉格尔做出一个往前的动作，麦克菲利普小小一惊，朝后退去。直到重新挨近床边，他才开口。

「哦，是你啊？」他怒气冲冲地说。「深更半夜的，你跑来干什么？」

加拉格尔不理会他。他转向麦克菲利普太太，她还照先前的样子坐在火边，数着玫瑰经念珠。

「对不起，打扰您了，麦克菲利普太太，」他温和而恭敬地说，「在您正……呃……的时候。可为了逝者的缘故，有一两个问题我不得不问您。能不能劳您的驾——」

===Original===
“And what right have you to ask a question or two?” cried McPhillip, raging because Gallagher had refused even to talk to him.

He was sitting on the bed now. He sat on the bed timidly, as if he were in somebody else’s house.

Gallagher turned to him slowly and looked at him fiercely in the eyes.

“I have the right,” he said, “of a revolutionary to track a traitor to the cause.”
===Chinese===
「你又有什么权利问一两个问题？」麦克菲利普喊道，因为加拉格尔连话都不肯跟他说一句，正憋着一腔火。

他这时在床沿上坐下了。他怯生生地坐在床沿上，仿佛是在别人家里。

加拉格尔缓缓转向他，凶狠地直视着他的眼睛。

「我有的权利，」他说，「是一个革命者追查事业叛徒的权利。」

===Original===
“Ha!” sneered McPhillip. “An’ what kind of a revolutionary d’ye call yersel’?”

“A revolutionary Communist,” answered Gallagher.

Then he turned about insolently and bent down his head to talk to Mrs. McPhillip.

“Communist be damned,” cried McPhillip, jumping off the bed. “D’ye know what I’m goin’ to tell ye? Ye﻿—”
===Chinese===
「哈！」麦克菲利普讥笑道。「你管你自己叫哪一门子的革命者？」

「一个革命的共产主义者，」加拉格尔答道。

随后他傲慢地转过身去，俯下头跟麦克菲利普太太说话。

「共产主义个屁，」麦克菲利普喊道，从床上跳了起来。「你知道我要跟你说什么吗？你——」

===Original===
“Father,” cried Mary, wringing her hands, “don’t﻿—”

“Shut up, you young rip,” stamped the father; “am I master in me own house or am I not? You, ye Communist, as ye call yersel’! Yer the greatest scoundrel in Ireland. Yer the greatest enemy o’ yer class. Now, let me alone, Mary, or I’ll tan yer skin for ye. Let me tell him.﻿ ﻿… Let me﻿ ﻿… Let go,” he screamed shrilly, as she seized him tightly about the body and began to push him forcibly from the room.

He placed his hands and feet against the jambs of the door and turning his head around, he continued in a half-hysterical voice:

“It’s the likes o’ me that’s the revolutionaries, but we get no credit for it. It’s the likes o’ me that does the hard work, eddicatin’ me fellow-men, an’ at the same time strikin’ an honest blow for better conditions. But men like you are criminals. Criminals, criminals, that’s what yez are. Don’t lay hands on yer father, Mary. Don’t﻿—”
===Chinese===
「爸爸，」玛丽绞着双手喊道，「别——」

「闭嘴，你这小泼皮，」父亲跺着脚吼道；「我自己的家里，我做不做得了主？你，你这自称共产主义者的！你是爱尔兰头一号的恶棍。你是你们那个阶级的头号敌人。放开我，玛丽，不然我剥了你的皮。让我跟他说。……让我……撒手，」他尖声叫起来，因为她紧紧拦腰抱住了他，开始连推带搡地把他往屋外弄。

他把手脚抵住门框，扭过头来，用一种半歇斯底里的嗓音接着说：

「我们这号人才是革命者，可没人念我们的好。我们这号人干的是苦活，教育自己的同胞，同时又为改善处境打出老实的一拳。可你们这种人全是罪犯。罪犯，罪犯，你们就是这号东西。别对你老子动手，玛丽。别——」

===Original===
“I’m not touching you,” cried Mary. “Come on now. Get to bed.”

She got him into the hall. He sighed and broke into half-stifled sobs. Going up the stairs he kept saying in a low melancholy voice:

“If I had only put him on the scaffoldin’ with me, instead of eddicatin’ him, maybe he’d be alive an’ an honest man today. If I had only﻿ ﻿…”

Then his voice died away into a mumble as a door closed behind him upstairs.
===Chinese===
「我没碰你，」玛丽喊道。「走吧。上床去。」

她把他弄进了过道。他叹了口气，迸出半噎住的呜咽。上楼梯时，他一直低声地、忧郁地念叨着：

「想当年要是带他跟我一起上脚手架，而不是供他念书，他今天兴许还活着，还是个正派人。要是……」

随后他的声音低成了含混的咕哝，一扇门在他身后的楼上关上了。

===Original===
When Mary returned to the kitchen after putting him to bed, she found Gallagher sitting beside her mother, writing rapidly in a notebook. He had taken off his hat. His close-cropped black head looked very handsome to her. Still she shivered looking at it. The side face looked very cruel, with the brooding expression on it, as he looked downwards at the notebook.

She stood watching him until he finished writing. Then he sighed. He got up. He said a few words to Mrs. McPhillip. Then he shook hands with her and turned to Mary.

“I want to speak to you,” he said.
===Chinese===
玛丽安顿父亲睡下后回到厨房，看见加拉格尔坐在她母亲身边，正往一个小本子上飞快地写着什么。他已经摘了帽子。他那剪得短短的黑脑袋，在她眼里显得很好看。可她望着它，还是打了个寒噤。他低头望着小本子的时候，那张侧脸显得非常冷酷，带着沉思盘算的神情。

她站在一旁看着他，直到他写完。随后他叹了口气，站起身，对麦克菲利普太太说了几句，同她握了握手，然后转向玛丽。

「我想跟你说句话。」他说。

===Original===
She led him into the parlour excitedly. It was dark there and she had to fumble around for matches to light the gas. She couldn’t find them. Gallagher offered his box. He lit a match. She went to take it from him. Their fingers touched. She started and stumbled over something. The match fell from his fingers and went out. He reached out his hands to catch her as she stumbled. He caught her by the wrists and held her tightly. They had not spoken a word. It was very queer in the darkness. Their faces were very close together, but they could not see one another. They stood still, each of them mastered by some strange impulse, that bound their tongues. They stood still, in the utter darkness and silence of the little stuffed room, for almost a minute. Then Gallagher spoke. He spoke in a soft whisper. The sound of his voice was soft and caressing. His lips were so close to hers that his breath came moist to her lips. There was a catch in his voice, as if the volume of sound were not strong enough to steady itself on the air.
===Chinese===
她激动地把他领进客厅。客厅里黑着，她得摸索着找火柴点煤气灯。她找不到。加拉格尔递上自己的火柴盒。他划着一根火柴。她走过去接。两人的手指碰在了一起。她一惊，绊了一下，脚下踢着了什么东西。火柴从他指间落下，灭了。她踉跄的一瞬，他伸手去扶。他抓住她的双腕，紧紧握住。两人一句话也没说。黑暗里透着说不出的古怪。两张脸离得极近，彼此却看不见对方。他们站着不动，各自被某种奇怪的冲动辖制着，舌头都被缚住了。在那间塞满杂物的小屋里，在彻底的黑暗与寂静中，他们一动不动地站了将近一分钟。随后加拉格尔开口了。他用柔和的耳语说话。他的嗓音轻软温存。他的嘴唇离她的那么近，呼吸湿湿地喷在她的唇上。他的声音里带着一丝颤，仿佛那音量太弱，在空气里立不稳似的。

===Original===
“Mary,” he said, “I want you to come to a Court of Inquiry with me tonight.”

She made no attempt to reply. Neither did he seem to expect a reply. It seemed that the words and their implication were foreign to the purpose of their meeting here. It seemed that the coursing of their blood and the confused beating of their hearts, was in response to some prearranged assignation of declared love.

But there had never been a question of amorous relations between them. They had never met in privacy like this before. Their previous meetings were more in the nature of quarrels. Mary had always disputed with Gallagher, particularly of late, when she had become violently opposed to him. But now in the darkness, in the solitude, both she and he were mastered by some amazing emotion that was inexplicable.

“Dan,” she whispered suddenly, “you make me afraid. Why are we standing here in the darkness? What do you want with me?”
===Chinese===
「玛丽，」他说，「我要你今夜随我出席一个调查法庭。」

她没有想要回答。他似乎也不指望回答。那些话连同话里的意味，仿佛同他们此刻相会的目的格格不入。仿佛他们奔流的血液、纷乱的心跳，才是在应一场早经约定的、两情相诉的幽会。

可两人之间从不曾有过儿女私情的问题。他们从不曾这样单独相会过。先前他们的见面，倒更像是争吵。玛丽总是同加拉格尔争辩，尤其是近来，她已经激烈地站到了他的对立面。可此刻，在黑暗里，在幽寂中，她和他都被某种说不清道不明的惊人情感辖制住了。

「丹，」她忽然低声道，「你叫我害怕。我们为什么站在这黑暗里？你要我怎么样？」

===Original===
“I want you to revenge your brother,” said Gallagher suddenly, as if he had obeyed an unforeseen impulse and broached an unexpected subject, with which his mind had hitherto only toyed nervously. “I want you to join me, Mary. I want you to take your brother’s place in the Organization. But a greater place than he held. No. It’s not your brother’s place I want you to take but﻿ ﻿…”

“Dan, what are you talking about?” she panted in a terrified voice.

There was a pause during which Gallagher imperceptibly moved his face closer to hers. Their lips met. They kissed gently. Then she drew back suddenly, shivering violently. She wanted to rush away and to shout, but the fascination of his voice was upon her. His voice and the glamour of his face. His face and the romance of his life. She was bound suddenly by it. Suddenly too, it became apparent to her why she had been eager to convert him. It had been in order to meet him, with a plausible excuse.
===Chinese===
「我要你为你哥哥报仇，」加拉格尔突然说，仿佛听从了一股事先不曾料到的冲动，捅破了一个出乎意料的话题——这话题，他的脑子此前只是紧张地拨弄过而已。「我要你加入我，玛丽。我要你在组织里接下你哥哥的位置。比他担任过的更高的位置。不。我要你接的不是你哥哥的位置，而是……」

「丹，你在说些什么呀？」她喘着气，声音里满是惊恐。

有一阵停顿，停顿中加拉格尔不易察觉地把脸凑得更近。他们的嘴唇相遇了。他们轻轻地吻了。随即她猛地退开，剧烈地发着抖。她想逃开、想喊叫，可他嗓音的魔力罩住了她。他的嗓音和他脸庞的光彩。他的脸庞和他生命的传奇。她突然被这一切缚住了。也是突然间，她明白了先前自己为什么那样急切地想感化他。原来是为了能见着他——有一个说得过去的借口。

===Original===
And she was almost engaged to Joseph Augustine Short, who was a “gentleman,” who would place her in a respectable sphere of life, who would free her forever from the hated associations of her slum life with its squalor, its revolutionary crises, its damnable insecurity, its soul-devouring monotony.

Mother of Mercy! Was she in love with Gallagher? Was she going to be drawn into the web of his conspiracies by the deadly fascination of his face and of his voice, by the romance of his life?
===Chinese===
而她差不多已经同约瑟夫·奥古斯丁·肖特（Joseph Augustine Short）订了婚——那是个「上等人」，能把她安放进体面的生活圈层，让她永远摆脱她所憎恶的贫民窟生活的种种纠葛：那肮脏、那一次次的革命风潮、那要命的朝不保夕、那磨蚀灵魂的单调。

仁慈的圣母啊！她爱上加拉格尔了吗？她就要被他脸庞与嗓音那致命的魔力、被他生命的传奇，吸进他阴谋的罗网里去了吗？

===Original===
“Mary,” he murmured at last, “you are the remainder of me. The two of us together would make a complete whole. There would be nothing else wanting to the two of us, no unfulfilled﻿ ﻿… er﻿ ﻿… well﻿ ﻿… it’s not that either. I have not fully worked out that part of the theory. I have approached it from another point of view.”

“What is it, Dan?” She drew away her face farther and loosened one hand. He was wrapped in dreams now and he did not attempt to stop her. In fact he let her go altogether suddenly and sat on the table, simply holding her right hand in his. “What do you want with me?” she said again.

“I want you to join me,” he muttered almost inaudibly, wrapped in his thoughts.
