“Dan, I don’t understand,” she gasped, afraid of his voice.

“How? How?” he muttered. “Why don’t you understand? I want you to join me.”

“Do you mean﻿ ﻿… to﻿ ﻿… to﻿ ﻿… to marry you?”
===Chinese===
「玛丽，」他终于喃喃道，「你是我的剩余部分。我们两个合在一起，就是一个完整的整体。我们俩便再无所缺，再没有未完成的……呃……唉……也不是那么说。理论的那一部分，我还没有完全想透。我是从另一个角度接近它的。」

「那是什么，丹？」她把脸挪远了些，松开一只手。他这会儿沉浸在幻梦里，并不想拦她。事实上，他忽然索性放开了她，坐在桌上，只把她的一只右手握在自己手里。「你要我怎么样？」她又问了一遍。

「我要你加入我，」他几不可闻地咕哝道，整个人还裹在自己的思绪里。

「丹，我不明白，」她喘着气说，怕的是他的嗓音。

「怎么？怎么？」他咕哝道。「你为什么不明白？我要你加入我。」

「你是说……要……要……要嫁给你？」

===Original===
“Oh rot,” he cried irritably, waking from his half-reverie and turning towards her. “These ridiculous conventions don’t enter my consciousness. Not only have I no respect for them, but they don’t enter my consciousness. You understand the significance of that. My personality is entirely in keeping with my mission in life. For me all these words attain their true values. Marriage, for instance, is truly a capitalist word meaning an arrangement for the protection of property so that legitimate sons could inherit it. So I don’t have to argue with it in my own mind in order to rid myself of a belief in it. Most men have to do that. I am a hundred years before my time. I want to destroy the idea of property. It is my mission. I don’t want to leave property to my children. I don’t want children. They are nothing to me. The perpetuation of my life is in my work, in men’s thoughts, in the fulfillment of my mission. That’s why I want you to join me, because I feel something, an affinity maybe﻿—that’s a wrong word though﻿—between you and myself. I am sure there is a natural relationship, chemical maybe, between the two of us. We are two parts of one whole. I am sure of that. No, damn it all. What a ridiculous idea! I don’t want you to join me for the purpose of cohabitation. I have no time to make sentiment a main impulse of my desire to live. Neither have you. I am certain of it. You are governed by other impulses. Maybe you don’t know it. Probably you are afraid to analyze yourself. But I know it. I don’t know it. I feel it. ‘Know’ is not a proper word. It’s out of use. ‘Feel’ is better. It is an outcome of the new consciousness that I am discovering. But I haven’t worked that out fully yet. It’s only embryonic.”
===Chinese===
「哦，胡扯，」他烦躁地喊道，从半迷梦中醒来，朝她转过身去。「这些可笑的俗套进不了我的意识。我对它们不但毫无敬意，而且它们根本进不了我的意识。你明白这句话的分量。我的人格与我的人生使命完全相称。在我这里，这些字眼全都取得它们的真值。譬如『婚姻』，实实在在是一个资本主义字眼，意思是一种保护财产的安排，好让嫡子继承。所以我无须在心里同它辩驳一番，好摆脱对它的信仰。大多数人都得这样费一道功夫。我超前我的时代一百年。我要摧毁财产观念。这是我的使命。我不想把财产留给子女。我不要子女。他们于我是无。我生命的延续，在我的工作里，在人们的思想里，在我使命的完成里。所以我要你加入我，因为我感觉到某种东西，也许是一种亲和——不过这个词不对——在你我之间。我确信我们两人之间有一种天然的关系，也许是化学的关系。我们是一个整体的两半。这一点我确信。不，见鬼。多么可笑的念头！我要你加入我，不是为了同居。我没有工夫把情感奉为我求生欲望的主冲动。你也没有。这一点我有把握。你受着别的冲动支配。也许你自己不知道。多半你是不敢剖析你自己。可我知道。不，我不知道。我感觉到。『知道』不是个恰当的词。它过时了。『感觉』更好。它是我正在发现的新意识的一个产物。可那我也还没想透。还只是胚胎。」

===Original===
He paused. She started when he stopped. She had not been listening to what he had been saying. She had been arguing with herself. She had not succeeded in settling with her conscience what she had been discussing when he stopped. She bit her lip and started. She was blushing.

“Tell me, Dan,” she whispered, “do you believe in anything? Do you even believe in Communism? Do you feel pity for the working class?”

Gallagher uttered an exclamation of contempt and shrugged his shoulders. He panted as he spoke, such was the rapidity of his words, in an effort to keep pace with the rapidity of his tempestuous thoughts.

“No,” he said, “I believe in nothing fundamentally. And I don’t feel pity. Nothing fundamental that has consciousness capable of being understood by a human being exists, so I don’t believe in anything, since an intelligent person can only believe in something that is fundamental. If I could believe in something fundamental, then the whole superstructure of life would be capable of being comprehended by me. Life would resolve itself into a period of intense contemplation. Action would be impossible. There would be no inducement for action. There would be some definite measurement for explaining everything. Men seek only that which offers no explanation of itself. But wait a minute. I haven’t worked that out fully yet. It’s only in the theoretical stage yet. I have no time.
===Chinese===
他停住了。他一停，她倒吃了一惊。他方才说的话，她一句也没听进去。她一直在同自己争辩。直到他停口，她也没能同自己的良心把心里争执的事情了断。她咬住嘴唇，一惊。她脸红了。

「告诉我，丹，」她低声问，「你究竟信不信什么？你连共产主义也信吗？你怜悯工人阶级吗？」

加拉格尔发出一声轻蔑的呼喊，耸了耸肩。他说话时直喘，他的语词那么急，就是为着追赶他那狂风骤雨般的思绪的急速。

「不，」他说，「从根本上说，我什么都不信。我也不怜悯。凡具有人类所能理解的意识的根本之物，根本就不存在，所以我什么都不信，因为有智识的人只能信根本的东西。我若能信一件根本的东西，那么生命的整座上层建筑，便都能为我所理解。生命就会化成一段凝神观照的时期。行动将不复可能。行动将失去一切诱因。就会有一种确定的度量，可以解释一切。而人寻求的，恰恰只是那自身不作解释的东西。可是且慢。这个我也还没想透。还只停留在理论阶段。我没有工夫。

===Original===
“But you spoke of pity. Pity? Pity is a ridiculous sensation for a man of my nature. We are incapable of it. A revolutionary is incapable of feeling pity. Listen. The philosophy of a revolutionary is this. Civilization is a process in the development of the human species. I am an atom of the human species, groping in advance, impelled by a force over which neither I nor the human species have any control. I am impelled by the Universal Law to thrust forward the human species from one phase of its development to another. I am at war with the remainder of the species. I am a Christ beating them with rods. I have no mercy. I have no pity. I have no beliefs. I am not master of myself. I am an automaton. I am a revolutionary. And there is no reward for me but the satisfaction of one lust, the lust for the achievement of my mission, for power maybe, but I haven’t worked out that yet. I am not certain that the lust for power is a true impulse, a true﻿ ﻿… but listen. That can come later. Can you give me an answer now? Will you join me?”
===Chinese===
「可你说到怜悯。怜悯？对一个像我这种禀性的人，怜悯是一种可笑的感觉。我们没有能力怜悯。一个革命者没有能力感觉怜悯。听着。革命者的哲学是这样。文明是人类物种发展中的一个过程。我是人类物种的一粒原子，在前面摸索着，被一种力量推着走——这力量，我和人类物种都做不了主。我受宇宙法则的驱迫，把人类物种从它发展的一个阶段推向另一个阶段。我同这物种的其余部分处于交战状态。我是一个挥着鞭子抽打他们的基督。我没有仁慈。我没有怜悯。我没有信仰。我不是自己的主人。我是一具自动机。我是一个革命者。而我没有报偿，只有一种欲望的满足——成就我使命的欲望，也许还有权力的欲望，可那个我也还没想透。我不能断定权力欲是不是一个真正的冲动，一个真正的……可是听着。那个可以往后再说。现在你能给我一个答复吗？你愿不愿意加入我？」

===Original===
“No﻿ ﻿… no, Dan. Stop. Listen.” She gasped, holding him back. “Not now. Later on I’ll tell you. On a night like this, with death in the house, how can you talk of﻿ ﻿… ?”

“Why?” he uttered fiercely. “What night would be better suited for you to join me? Don’t you want to avenge your brother’s death? Don’t you want﻿ ﻿…”

“Dan, Dan,” she gasped, struggling away as he attempted to seize her in his arms, “don’t touch me or I’ll scream. I’m so excited.”

There was a pause. Their breathing was loud in the silence. A noise came from the kitchen.

“That’s mother going to bed, Dan,” said Mary hurriedly. “You must go, Dan.”
===Chinese===
「不……不，丹。停下。听着。」她喘着气，拦住他。「现在不行。往后我会告诉你的。这样一个夜晚，家里停着死人，你怎么能谈……？」

「为什么？」他凶狠地说。「哪一夜能比今夜更适合你加入我？你不想为你哥哥的死报仇吗？你不想……」

「丹，丹，」她喘着气，趁他伸臂要搂她，拼命挣脱，「别碰我，不然我要喊了。我心乱极了。」

又是一阵停顿。寂静里，两人的呼吸声很响。厨房里传来一声响动。

「是妈妈去睡了，丹，」玛丽急忙说。「你得走了，丹。」

===Original===
“Will you come to the Court of Inquiry tonight?”

“Dan, I’d rather﻿—”

“You must come, Mary. You must. You﻿—”

“All right, Dan, I’ll come.”

“Good. I’ll come for you. Be ready at one o’clock.”

“All right, I’ll be ready.”

“Be waiting in the parlour here. I’ll knock on the window.”

“All right, Dan. Go now immediately. I’m coming, mother. Good night.”

He bent hurriedly and kissed her lips. Then he stumbled from the room. She waited until the hall door closed behind him. Then she shuddered as the barmaid had done.
===Chinese===
「今晚的调查法庭，你来不来？」

「丹，我宁可——」

「你必须来，玛丽。你必须。你——」

「好吧，丹，我来。」

「好。我来接你。一点钟准备好。」

「好，我会准备好的。」

「就在这客厅里等着。我敲窗户。」

「好的，丹。现在马上走。我来了，妈妈。晚安。」

他匆匆俯身吻了她的嘴唇，随后踉跄着出了屋。她一直等到过道的门在他身后关上。然后她打了个寒噤，跟那女招待先前一样。

===Original===
Gallagher walked away northwards furiously, with glittering eyes, thinking.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔朝北走去，怒气冲冲，目光灼灼，一路盘算着。

## VIII / 八

===Original===
Walking out from the public-house into the street, Gypo felt as if he had leapt suddenly into an arena, where he was to perform astounding feats, while an amazed audience, with two million eyes, gazed silent and spellbound. He thrust his head into the air. He let his arms hang limply from his shoulders in front of his body. He took two staggering steps forward and uttered a long-drawn-out yell.

It was that peculiar yell that mountaineers will utter in the west of Ireland, when the fair is over in the district town and night is falling, as they issue from the public-houses, bareheaded and wild-eyed, dragging their snorting and shivering mares after them by the halter.

Gypo’s yell was just such a one. It was like a challenge to mortal combat issued to all and sundry. He felt beside himself with strength. He was free again. Had not Gallagher given him his word that everything would be all right? Would he not be taken back again into the Organization? Had he not thrown suspicion on to the Rat Mulligan? He was free again. Ye-a-a-aw!
===Chinese===
从酒馆里走出来，到了街上，吉波觉得自己仿佛猛然跳进了一座竞技场，他要在场中表演惊人的绝技，而一群瞠目结舌的看客，用两百万只眼睛，默默无言、屏息凝神地注视着。他把头朝空中挺去。他任凭两条胳膊松软地从肩头垂在身前。他踉跄着朝前迈了两步，发出一声拖得长长的呐喊。

那是爱尔兰西部山里人特有的一种呐喊：县镇的集市散了，夜幕正在落下，他们从酒馆里出来，光着头，瞪着野眼，手里拽着笼头，把身后打着响鼻、瑟瑟发抖的母马拖出来。

吉波这一声呐喊正是那一路的。它像一纸下给张三李四、人人有份的生死决斗书。他觉得自己力气大得不能自持。他又自由了。加拉格尔不是拿话许过他，说一切都会妥帖吗？他不是就要重新被接纳回组织里去了吗？他不是已经把嫌疑栽到「耗子」穆利根头上了吗？他又自由了。咿——啊——啊——噢！

===Original===
He staggered to the kerbstone and yelled, letting his body go completely limp with ecstasy. Then, breathing heavily through his nostrils, he stood erect and looked about him to see what effect his yell had produced. There was a small crowd of people near by. They had just come out of Ryan’s public-house and from Shaughnessy’s, another public-house ten yards away at the corner of a lane. The corner was brilliant with light, from the public-houses, from a fried-fish and potato shop, and from a drapery shop where the lights were kept on all night by the owner, with the idea that the light might terrify gunmen and housebreakers.

Gypo stood out in the blaze of light, on the kerbstone, with the beads of rainwater on his white woollen muffler reflected like dewdrops in the artificial light. The people looked at him in amazement and with that intense satisfaction which the proletariat of the slums always derives from something unexpected and extraordinary happening, at no cost to themselves. A spectacle had presented itself. The crowd began to swell.

Gypo had not intended to carry the affair any further. In fact he had not intended to yell at all. But when he saw the crowd he became amused. He pitched on a man who stood near, a tall, thin, respectably dressed man, who had a sour expression on his face.
===Chinese===
他踉跄着走到路缘石上，又呐喊了一声，任凭身子在狂喜里彻底瘫软下去。随后，他鼻孔粗重地喘着气，直挺挺站起来，环顾四周，看看他的呐喊产生了什么效果。近旁聚着一小群人。他们刚从瑞安酒馆出来，也有从肖内西酒馆（Shaughnessy’s）出来的——那是十码开外、巷口上的另一家酒馆。街角一片灯火辉煌，光亮来自那两家酒馆，来自一家炸鱼薯条店，还来自一家布店——店主让店里的灯通宵亮着，指望那灯光能吓退枪手和撬门的贼。

吉波站在那片耀眼的灯光里，站在路缘石上，白羊毛围巾上的一颗颗雨水珠，在灯光下映得像露珠。人们惊愕地望着他，带着贫民窟的无产者每逢有意外的稀奇事白白发生时总能得到的那份酣畅满足——反正自己一文不花。有好戏送上门来了。人群开始膨胀。

吉波本没打算把这桩事再闹下去。其实他压根儿没打算呐喊。可是看见了人群，他来了兴致。他挑中站在近旁的一个人，一个瘦高个、衣着体面的男人，脸上带着一副酸刻相。

===Original===
“What are ye lookin’ at me for?” cried Gypo, staring the fellow in the face insolently.

“I’m not lookin’ at ye,” snapped the man irritably.

“Yer a liar,” bellowed Gypo, “don’t I see ye lookin’ at me?”

“Well a cat can look at a king,” cried the stranger, thrusting out his chin and spitting venomously to his left.

“What are ye sayin’ about kings?” said Gypo angrily. “Better say nothin’ about kings around here, me lad. I think yer lookin’ for trouble. I’ve got a good mind to give ye a wallop in the jaw.”

“Ye would, would ye?” cried the stranger, making a move to take his hands out of his coat pockets.
===Chinese===
「你瞧我干什么？」吉波嚷道，放肆地直瞪着那家伙的脸。

「我没瞧你，」那男人烦躁地顶了一句。

「你撒谎，」吉波吼道，「难道我没看见你在瞧我？」

「哼，猫还有资格瞧瞧国王呢，」陌生人嚷道，下巴朝前一伸，恶狠狠地朝左边啐了一口。

「你说国王又怎么了？」吉波怒道。「在这一带，国王的事你最好一个字也别提，小子。我看你是自找麻烦。我真想照你下巴上给一记狠的。」

「你敢，你敢？」陌生人叫道，做出要把双手从外套口袋里抽出来的架势。

===Original===
But he was too late. Gypo’s right hand swung around. The man went down like a bag of nails dropped to an iron deck. Somebody cried: “Lord, save us.” Gypo stood over the fallen man with his chest heaving. A policeman appeared from somewhere in the rear. He advanced rapidly, shouldering the people and trying to snatch something from under his cape as he made for Gypo.

“Look out, look out,” cried an old woman, through her cupped hands.

Gypo looked on either side hurriedly and then he heard the excited breathing of the policeman approaching from behind. He tried to turn about, but the policeman was upon him. The policeman’s hands closed about his biceps and jerked back both his arms to lock them behind his back. The arms were halfway back before Gypo could mobilize his vast strength to arrest their retreat. There was a loud snap of bones being strained taut when Gypo’s strength collided with the policeman’s strength at the point on Gypo’s biceps where the policeman’s hands rested.

Both men groaned loudly. The policeman’s boots tore at the wet pavement, making a noise like dry cloth being rent, as he struggled to keep firm. Slowly Gypo leaned forward until the policeman’s body was on his back.
===Chinese===
可他太迟了。吉波的右手抡了过去。那男人应声倒下，像一袋钉子砸落在铁甲板上。有人喊：「老天爷，救命啊。」吉波站在倒地的人跟前，胸膛一起一伏。一个警察从后面什么地方钻了出来，飞快地挤上前来，用肩膀开路，一面朝吉波逼近，一面想在披风底下摸什么东西。

「当心，当心，」一个老妇人拢着双手喊道。

吉波慌忙朝两边张望，随即听见背后那警察激动的喘息声逼近来。他想转过身，可警察已经扑到他身上。警察的双手扣住他的上臂，猛地把两条胳膊往后拽，要反剪到他背后。双臂都拽过半程了，吉波才调度起他那庞大的膂力，止住双臂的败退。吉波的力气同警察的力气，就在警察双手扣着的那一点上迎头相撞，绷得笔紧的骨头发出一声响亮的脆响。

两个人都大声哼了出来。警察的靴子在湿漉漉的人行道上乱蹬，发出像撕干布一样的声响，拼命想站桩立稳。吉波慢慢朝前俯下身去，直到警察的身子整个吊在了他背上。

===Original===
Then he thrust back his head with a snarl. His poll collided with the policeman’s chin. There was a dull thud and a snap. Gypo uttered an oath and thrust his head downwards towards his knees, holding his thighs rigid. Before the head had reached the knees, the policeman had hurtled through the air with a scream of terror, right over Gypo’s head.

He fell with three separate soft sounds to the street, with his right side against the concrete wall of a house. He fell on his back. He rose again in the middle, resting on his right hand and on his heels. He brandished his left hand towards Gypo and at the same time he tried to grip a fleeing spectator with it. Then he moaned and subsided again.

“Run, Gypo,” said somebody.
===Chinese===
接着他咆哮一声，猛地把头向后掼去。他的后脑壳撞上警察的下巴。一声闷响，一声脆响。吉波骂了一句，头朝膝盖直扎下去，两条大腿绷得铁硬。头还没够到膝盖，警察已经恐怖地尖叫着腾空飞起，从吉波头顶上翻了过去。

他三声各自分明的闷响摔在街上，右侧贴着一栋房子的水泥墙。他仰面朝天。又半撑起来，靠右手和两脚跟支着。他朝吉波挥舞左手，同时还想用这只手去抓一个逃跑的看客。随后他呻吟一声，又瘫了下去。

「跑啊，吉波，」有人说。

===Original===
Gypo ran towards a lane at a fast run. He was followed by a crowd. Others gathered around the fallen policeman.

Gypo halted at the far end of the lane, in a dark corner. The crowd gathered around him. Everybody was panting with excitement. They all stared down the lane towards the blaze of light where the policeman lay. They began to jabber.

“I can see trouble comin’,” said one. “The sojers’ll be here shortly. Then yer goin’ to see some pluggin’.”

“Gwan,” said another contemptuously. “There’s no sojers goin’ to come down here. Ye wouldn’t get a sojer in the town to dare come within a mile of Titt Street on this blessed night, after what happened today.”

At the mention of “what happened today,” a man cursed, a woman crossed herself piously under her shawl, an angry silence fell.

Gypo stood with his hands in his pockets, paying no heed to the talk. With his lips stuck out, he was looking gloomily down the lane towards the blaze of light. He was enjoying himself immensely.
===Chinese===
吉波撒腿朝一条巷子飞跑。一群人跟在他后面。另一些人围住了倒地的警察。

吉波在巷子尽头一个黑角落里停住。人群围拢过来。个个兴奋得直喘。他们都顺着巷子朝那片警察躺着的灯光盯望。他们七嘴八舌地议论开了。

「我看要出乱子了，」一个说。「大兵们一会儿就到。到那时有你们瞧的，枪子儿横飞。」

「去你的，」另一个不屑地说。「才不会有哪个大兵上这儿来。今天出了这样的事，这该遭瘟的晚上，全城你找不出一个大兵，敢走近蒂特街一英里之内。」

一听「今天出的事」这几个字，一个男人咒骂起来，一个女人在披巾底下虔诚地画了个十字，一片愤怒的沉默落了下来。

吉波两手插在口袋里，全不理会那些议论。他撅着嘴唇，阴沉沉地顺着巷子朝那片灯光望去。他痛快到了极点。

===Original===
“Hist, hist!” somebody cried, “Look, look.”

Two policemen crossed the blaze of light, bearing their fallen comrade between them. A few women and small boys followed them. Then two more policemen came, hauling along the man whom Gypo had struck. They were dragging him unceremoniously, holding him by the armpits, with his feet trailing along the ground and his arms dangling. They were probably under the impression that it was he who had felled their comrade. The man made an effort to wrench himself free, but they tightened their hold on his arms. He writhed and went limp again, allowing himself to be dragged lifelessly. A woman, with straggling red hair and a child on her back in a black shawl, danced in front of the policemen, screaming and gesticulating, demanding the man’s release. Then the procession passed out of sight with a mad rush of feet and a medley of indiscriminate noises.

“Let’s go back,” muttered a young man who had a slight hump.

Gypo grunted and hitched up his trousers. He put his hand to his head to settle his hat jauntily before leading the way back. But instead he uttered an oath. His little round torn hat was not there. His massive round skull stood bare under the night. It stood naked, hummocked and gashed here and there, like a badly shorn sheep. He traversed the skull with his right palm, in little flurried rushes, as if he had had a vague suspicion that the hat was hiding somewhere along the expanse of skull. Then he set out at a wild rush down the lane, followed by the crowd, to retrieve the hat, as if his life depended on it. For the first time, since Gallagher had given his word, terror again invaded his mind. If they discovered the hat they might be able to discover the identity of that ponderous fellow who had gone into the police-station. …
===Chinese===
「嘘，嘘！」有人喊，「看哪，看哪。」

两个警察抬着他们倒下的同伙，穿过那片灯光。几个女人和小男孩跟在后面。接着又来了两个警察，拖曳着吉波打倒的那个男人。他们架着他的胳肢窝，毫不客气地拖行，他两脚蹭着地，两条胳膊悬荡着。他们多半认定，撂倒他们同伙的就是此人。那男人奋力想挣脱，他们却把他双臂攥得更紧。他扭动几下，又软了下来，任凭自己被死气沉沉地拖走。一个女人，披一头散乱的红发，黑披巾里背着个孩子，在警察面前又蹦又跳，尖叫着、比划着，要求放人。随后这一行人便在一阵疯乱的脚步和乱糟糟的嘈杂里走出了视线。

「回去吧，」一个微微驼背的年轻人咕哝道。

吉波哼了一声，把裤子往上提了提。他抬手想把帽子潇洒地正一正，好领头回去。可他反倒骂了一句。他那顶破烂的小圆帽不在了。他那颗滚圆的大脑壳光秃秃地立在夜空底下。它赤裸裸地立着，这里一座鼓包，那里一道豁口，像一只剪得乱七八糟的羊。他用右手掌在脑壳上一阵一阵慌乱地扫摸，仿佛隐隐疑心那帽子就藏在这片脑壳上的什么地方。随后他撒腿朝巷子里狂奔而去，人群跟在后面，去寻那顶帽子，仿佛性命系在它上面。自加拉格尔许下诺言以来，恐惧头一回又侵入了他的脑子。他们若是发现了那顶帽子，兴许就能查明那个走进警察局的沉重大汉的身份……

===Original===
But no. He rushed into the road and brought up with a slither of his right foot on the wet pavement. The hat was lying in the gutter before his eyes. It lay crushed beside a flattened little cardboard chocolate box and an orange skin. It had been trodden on by a small bare foot. The impress of a wet heel was on its right side.

He grabbed it up hurriedly, punched it into shape and crammed it on to his skull with both hands. Then he laughed aloud and turned to the people.

“I thought I had lost it,” he cried affectionately. “I had it this two years.”

The crowd gaped at the hat as if it had magical properties. Others who had run up without knowing what had already happened gaped at Gypo’s humpy face, at his ruminative eyes and his eyebrows that were like snouts, at the red fat backs of his hands, as he held them to his throat tightening the white woollen muffler about his neck. There were agitated whispers on the outskirts of the ragged crowd.
===Chinese===
可是并没有。他冲进马路，右脚在湿漉漉的人行道上打了个滑，收住了步。帽子正躺在阴沟里，就在他眼前。它瘪瘪地趴着，旁边是一块压扁的小纸板巧克力盒和一片橘子皮。它被一只光着的小脚踩过。右边印着一个湿鞋跟的印子。

他慌忙抓起它，捶打出原形，双手把它扣上脑壳。然后他放声大笑，转向人群。

「我还当把它丢了呢，」他亲亲热热地嚷道。「这顶帽子我戴了两年啦。」

人群目瞪口呆地盯着那顶帽子，仿佛它有什么魔法。另一些不知就里跑来的人，则呆望着吉波那张疙瘩脸，望着一双反刍似的眼睛和两道像猪拱嘴一样的眉毛，望着他抬到喉前、收紧脖子上白羊毛围巾的那两只红胖的手背。褴褛人群的外圈上，一阵阵骚动的低语。

===Original===
“He’s stronger than any bull.”

“How? Why? What did he do?” from a dozen throats.

“Wait till I tell ye. I saw him with me own eyes send Scrapper Moloney o’ the B Division flyin’ over his shoulder like a man divin’ off the Bull Wall. I declare to me—”

“I know him well. He used to be a bobby himself once. His name is Nolan. Gypo Nolan. Didn’t ye ever hear of him?”

“Sure; usen’t he be pals with Frankie McPhillip that was shot today?”

“Sure I was,” broke in Gypo, overhearing the remark; “an’ when ye speak o’ the dead, ye might add Lord Have Mercy on him.”

“Hear, hear,” cried several voices. “Hit him a puck in the jaw. Who is he?”
===Chinese===
「他比什么公牛都壮。」

「怎么了？为什么？他干了什么？」十几条嗓子一齐问。

「且听我道来。我亲眼看见他把B分区的『打手』莫洛尼（Scrapper Moloney）从肩膀上摔出去，摔得像个人从公牛墙（Bull Wall）上跳水。我可以起誓——」

「我认得他。他自己从前还当过警察呢。他姓诺兰。吉波·诺兰。你没听说过他？」

「可不是嘛；他从前不是跟今天挨了枪的弗兰基·麦克菲利普是哥们儿吗？」

「可不是咋的，」吉波听见了这话，插嘴道，「说起死人的时候，也该添一句『愿天主垂怜他』。」

「对啊，对啊，」好几条嗓子喊。「照他下巴给他一拳。他是谁？」

===Original===
A noisy argument and a scuffle arose. The culprit was hustled away, kicked and struck about the face, until he made his escape by running at full speed up the lane. Then they all crowded around Gypo again.

He stood head and shoulders above them, revelling in the attention he was attracting. He stood so impassively with his arms folded, that he might be mistaken for a great scowling statue at a distance. Then he suddenly raised his right hand and made a circular movement with it.

“Come on,” he cried wildly. “I’m goin’ to give everybody here a feed. Come on. Come on every mother’s son in this crowd that’s hungry.”

He waved his arm towards the fried-fish and chip shop and headed off towards the door.
===Chinese===
一场吵吵闹闹的争执和扭打起来了。那闯祸的家伙被人连推带搡弄走，挨着脚踢，脸上挨着巴掌，直到他撒开两腿顺着巷子飞跑，才算脱了身。随后他们又都围拢到吉波身边。

他高出他们头肩一大截，陶醉在众人投来的瞩目里。他抱着双臂站在那里，那副不动声色的架势，远远望去准会被人当成一尊怒容满面的大雕像。随后他突然举起右手，朝空中画了一个圈。

「来吧，」他狂野地喊。「我要请这儿每一个人吃一顿。来吧。这一伙里凡是饿肚子的，每一个都来，一个不落。」

他朝那家炸鱼薯条店挥了挥手臂，径直朝店门走去。

===Original===
“Hurrah!”

“Long life to ye, me darlin’ son of Erin.”

“More power to yer elbow.”

“Up the rebels.”

Gypo strode in front of the disreputable throng as proud as a king leading his courtiers. They came after him with pattering feet, panting, pushing, snivelling, emitting that variegated murmur of sound that comes from a pack of wild things in a panic, coming from afar, unseen, without a guiding reason. They were the riffraff and the jetsam of the slums, the most degraded types of those who dwell in the crowded warrens on either bank of the Liffey. But to Gypo they were an audience to acclaim his words and his deeds.
===Chinese===
「好哇！」

「祝你长命百岁，我亲爱的爱琳（Erin）之子。」

「愿你的胳膊肘越来越使得上劲。」

「反叛万岁。」

吉波昂首阔步走在这群名声不堪的乌合之众前头，骄傲得像个领着满朝臣仆的国王。他们迈着细碎的脚步跟上来，喘着，挤着，抽着鼻子，发出那种斑驳杂乱的低语——一群恐慌中的野物发出的低语，来自远处，看不见，没有道理可循。他们是贫民窟的人渣和弃物，是挤住在利菲河（Liffey）两岸那些兔窟般拥挤住区里的人中间最堕落的品类。可对吉波来说，他们是一群看客，要为他的言语和他的功业喝彩。

===Original===
“Before long ye’ll see me cock o’ the walk around here,” he thought, as he strode into the shop. “Me an’ Gallagher. Come on, every man jack an’ woman too. Come on.”

They packed the little shop to the door. There was an overflow outside. It was warm within after the drizzling rain and the sharp wind outside. The air within the shop became almost immediately full of the vapour of human breath. The low murmur of breathing could be heard distinctly through the hum of whispered conversation.

“Hey there, towny,” cried Gypo to the shopkeeper, “chuck us a feed for all hands. I’m payin’ for the lot.”

The shopkeeper was an Italian, a dark middle-aged fellow with plaintive eyes. He looked at Gypo and then at the crowd. Curiosity, fear, suspicion and surprise raced across his face. Then he smiled and nodded his head. He said something in a foreign language to the girl who stood behind him and then he began immediately to put steaming portions of potatoes and fish into slips of old newspapers that lay ready to hand. The girl, a red-cheeked young woman with big black eyes, dressed in white, busied herself, pushing to and fro on a long arrangement like a sink, more fish and potatoes that were being fried. A crackling noise came from this frying. A hot, sweet and acrid smell permeated the whole room.
===Chinese===
「用不了多久，你们就会看见我在这一带称王称霸，」他一面跨进店门一面想。「我和加拉格尔。来吧，男人也好女人也好，一个不落。来吧。」

他们把小店塞得满满当当，直挤到门口。门外还漫出来一圈。外面下过蒙蒙细雨，刮着尖利的风，店里便显得暖和。店堂里的空气几乎立刻就充满了人呼出的水汽。低低的呼吸声，穿过窃窃私语的嗡嗡声，清晰可闻。

「喂，老乡，」吉波朝店主嚷道，「给这儿的弟兄们人人来一份吃的。钱全我出。」

店主是个意大利人，一个黑黑的中年汉子，长着一双哀苦的眼睛。他看看吉波，又看看人群。好奇、恐惧、疑忌和惊讶在他脸上赛跑似的掠过。然后他笑了，点点头。他对他身后站着的姑娘用一种外国话说了句什么，随即立刻动手，把冒着热气的土豆和鱼一份份装进手边现成的旧报纸条里。那姑娘是个红脸膛的年轻女人，黑黑的大眼睛，一身白装，正忙个不停，在一个像水槽似的长家伙上把更多的鱼和土豆来回拨弄，都在油里煎着。油炸声噼啪作响。一股滚烫的、又甜又呛的气味弥漫了整个屋子。

===Original===
The starved wastrels revelled in that smell. They looked towards the frying food with eager mouths and glistening eyes. Their nostrils smelt its heat and its savour greedily. Their faces were all fierce and emaciated. Their bodies were unkempt, crooked, weazened. But just then, the joy of an unexpected banquet had filled even their haggard and stupefied souls with a pleasure that made them laugh and chatter irresponsibly like children. The sorrows and the miseries of life were forgotten in that moment of common rejoicing. And perhaps that joyous mumble of chattering voices, rising through the steam in that slum eating-house, was a beautiful hymn of praise to the spirit of life.

And Gypo stood among them like some primeval monster just risen from the slime in which all things had their origin.

While around him crowded the others, like insects upon which he had been destined to fatten.

As he looked about him, with the slow, languorous eye movement of a resting bull, he felt the exaltation and conceit of a conqueror at the hour of victory. An intelligent being, gifted with such strength and the power to analyze his sensations, would have said: “This is the greatest moment of my life.” But Gypo did not think. There was nothing about him in relation to which he could think. A queen will not dream of flaunting her beauty and her raiment at a boors’ banquet. But she will, on a public holiday, bow to their clamorous cheers. So with Gypo.
===Chinese===
这些饿瘪了的浪汉们在那股气味里陶然陶醉。他们嘴上带着急切、眼睛放光地望着煎炸的食物。他们的鼻孔贪婪地嗅着那热气和香味。他们的脸一律凶悍而枯瘦。他们的身子邋遢、歪斜、干瘪。可就在那一刻，一顿意外盛宴的欢喜，连他们那些枯槁麻木的灵魂都灌满了快乐，乐得他们像孩子一样没心没肺地又说又笑。生活的愁苦和悲惨，在这一刻同乐里被忘了个干净。在那家贫民窟小饭馆里，那片欢快的叽叽喳喳穿过腾腾蒸汽往上升，兴许正是一曲献给生命之灵的美丽颂歌。

而吉波站在他们中间，像一头刚从万物发源之污泥里爬出来的太古怪物。

周围挤着的却是旁人，像一群虫豸，而他，是注定要靠这些虫豸长膘的。

他环顾四周，眼珠像一头歇息的公牛那样缓慢而倦怠地转动着，心里涨满了征服者在胜利时刻的那种意气和自负。一个有智识的生命，若兼有这样的膂力和剖析自身感受的能力，准会说：「这是我一生最伟大的时刻。」可吉波不思想。他身上没有任何一样东西，是能让他围着它思想的。一位女王做梦也不会想到在粗人的筵席上炫耀自己的美貌和衣装。可在公共节日里，她会向他们喧闹的欢呼鞠躬致意。吉波亦然。

===Original===
The cumbersome mechanism of his mind had been put in motion that evening by the necessity for forming a plan after leaving the police-station. The unaccustomed strain had unmoored it. It floundered about until Gallagher’s promise perched it on a foolish eminence whence it regarded the rest of humanity with contempt. It sprawled its ponderous foundations on that crazy eminence as arrogantly as if it were about to rest there for eternity.

He rolled his eyes about at the heads that were standing thickly around him, some on a level with his biceps, some on a level with his waist, while here and there a tall man like himself, stood with a red, lean, knotted neck strained forward, with throbbing throat, towards the food counter.

“Biga lot o’ people,” murmured the Italian suddenly, making a polite gesture with his hands to indicate the number of people present and the nature of his suspicions.

“That’s all right,” muttered Gypo. “Count ’em as ye hand out the grub. I’ll pay. Don’t you fret yersel’. Keep back there.”

He had been standing with his palms against the edge of the marble-topped counter. Now, in order to put his hand into his right trousers pocket, he had to pick up a small-sized man and crush him in between two women, who leaned away behind their shawls. Then he thrust his hand into his pocket and fingered the wad of Treasury notes. The very touch of them sent a wave of remembrance through his body. A slight tremor ran up, almost tangibly like a breath of cold wind in a hot place, up the extent of his body until it entered his brain. The remembrance of the origin of that wad of notes staggered him momentarily. He remembered the fat white hand, surmounted by a carefully brushed blue sleeve, that had handed him the wad over a desk, saying ever so icily: “You’ll find twenty pounds there. Go.”
===Chinese===
他那副笨重的头脑机器，那天晚上被「出警察局后必须定出一个计划」的必要性发动了起来。这番不习惯的劳作让它脱了锚。它扑腾了好一阵，直到加拉格尔的许诺把它架上一座愚蠢的高台，它便从那上头睥睨其余的人类。它把它那庞大的基座瘫摊在那座发疯的高台上，那份傲慢，仿佛打算就在那儿安歇到永远。

他转动眼珠，扫过四周密密匝匝立着的人头，有的齐他的上臂，有的齐他的腰，间或有一个像他一样的高个子，红红的精瘦脖子上筋结累累，朝食物柜台那边拼命探着，喉咙突突直跳。

「人-a-真多呀，」那意大利人突然咕哝了一句，双手做了个客气的手势，既点明在场的人数，也露出了他心底的疑虑。

「没的事，」吉波咕哝道。「发一份数一份。我付账。您呐，别操心。退后点儿。」

他本来双手撑在大理石台面柜台的边沿上站着。此刻，为了把手伸进右面裤袋，他只好把一个小个子男人拎起来，塞到两个女人中间去，那两个女人往披巾后面躲闪着。然后他把手捅进口袋，指头捻着那一沓国库纸币。只这一碰，一阵记忆的波涛便涌过了他的全身。一阵轻微的颤栗向上窜起，几乎摸得着似的，像大热天里一股冷风的气息，顺着他的身子一路向上，直到钻进他的脑子。想起这沓纸币的来路，他一时间晃了一晃。他想起了那只肥白的手，上头细心刷着一截蓝袖子，隔着一张办公桌把那沓钱递给他，说得冰冷透顶：「里面是二十英镑。去吧。」

===Original===
But after the first shock he curled his thick upper lip slightly and licked it with the tip of his tongue. The movement of his mouth had the appearance of a grin. The girl who happened to glance at him just then, found his gaze centred on her. She dropped the fish slice into the pan with some sort of an exclamation in a foreign language. But Gypo, though he was looking at her, did not see her. He was busy with his clumsy thick fingers, separating a single note from the roll without taking the roll from his pocket. At last he succeeded in doing so. He grunted and pulled out a single Treasury note.

He held it up.

“Here ye are,” he cried. “This’ll pay for the lot. Hand out the grub.”

The Italian smiled immediately and began to serve the packages into the eager hands that reached out for them. He counted out loud as he did so: “One, two, three, four …”
===Chinese===
可头一阵震动过后，他微微卷起厚厚的上唇，用舌尖舔了舔。嘴巴的这一动带着一点狞笑的意思。那姑娘恰在这时瞥了他一眼，正撞上他的目光钉在她身上。她把煎鱼铲往油锅里一丢，用外国话惊叫了一声。可吉波虽然望着她，却没有看见她。他正忙着用那笨拙的粗指头，在不把整沓钱掏出口袋的情况下，从钱卷里分出一张来。他终于分出来了。他哼了一声，抽出一张国库纸币。

他把它举了起来。

「喏，」他喊道。「这一张全管够。发吃的吧。」

意大利人立刻笑了，开始把纸包一份份递进急切伸来的手里。他一面递一面大声数着：「一、二、三、四……」

===Original===
An uproar commenced immediately. People crowded in from the door struggling to get served. Those who had been served struggled to get out into the street, with their food in steaming, dripping, paper packages in their hands. Altercations arose. The shop was full of sound. There were catcalls, whistling, cursing and laughing. Then a big docker brought the uproar to a climax by smashing his big boot through the wooden bottom of the counter, uttering a drunken yell as he did so. Then he sprawled over the counter, laughing foolishly and reaching out with his two hands towards the girl who shrank away terrified. The Italian uttered an exclamation of terror. Gypo turned towards the docker, lifted him up by the back and shouted:

“Keep quiet.”

The two words reechoed through the shop, like two rocks rolled down from opposite precipices and meeting in a glen, with two separate sounds, a heavy thick sound as they collide, a loud rasping sound as their splintered fragments fly clashing into the air.

The words had scarcely passed out the door into the night before a silence fell. Everybody stood still. One man stopped in the act of sucking a fishbone between his lips.
===Chinese===
一场大乱立刻开了场。人们从门口往里挤，争着要领吃的。领到吃的又争着往街上挤出去，手里捧着冒热气、滴着油的纸包。口角起来了。满店都是响动。有嘘声，有口哨，有咒骂，有大笑。随后一个大块头码头工人一只大靴子踹穿了柜台的木底，把这阵喧嚣推上顶峰，一面踹一面发出醉醺醺的吼叫。接着他整个人瘫趴到柜台上，傻笑着，朝那吓得直缩的姑娘伸出两只手。意大利人惊叫了一声。吉波转向那码头工人，揪住他的后背把他提了起来，喝道：

「安静。」

这两个字在店堂里回荡，像两块巨石从相对的悬崖上滚下，在山谷里相遇，发出两种各自的声响：相撞时一声沉厚的钝响，迸碎的碎块带着铿锵飞上半空时一声刺耳的锐响。

这两个字还没来得及出了店门、散进夜色，寂静就已经落下。人人呆立不动。有一个人正拿嘴唇吮一根鱼骨，吮到一半停住了。

===Original===
“Now carry on,” continued Gypo, “but don’t kick up a row like a lot o’ cannibals. Don’t disgrace yer country. A man ud think ye didn’t see a bite for a year.”

Then he himself turned towards the counter and asked the Italian how many meals had been served. Twenty-four meals had been served. He threw the pound note on the counter.

“Take out o’ that for three rounds for mesel’,” he said.

Then he pushed back his hat, drew a paper full of food towards him and began to eat. Without speaking, the Italian held the Treasury note between him and the electric light and peered at each side of it several times. Then he nodded his head and opened his till.
===Chinese===
「接着来吧，」吉波继续说，「可别像一帮食人生番似的闹腾。别丢你们国家的脸。人家还当你们一年没见过一口吃的了。」

随后他自己转向柜台，问意大利人发出去多少份了。发出去二十四份。他把那张一英镑钞票扔在柜台上。

「从里头扣出我自己三份的钱，」他说。

随后他把帽子往后一推，拉过来一包满满的食物，吃了起来。意大利人一言不发，把那张国库纸币举到自己跟电灯之间，两面翻来覆去端详了好几遍。然后他点点头，打开了钱匣。

===Original===
Mulholland had also strained his neck to peer at the Treasury note. He had been standing in the angle of the doorway all the time, silent and immovable. As soon as he saw the pound note, he drew out into the open and craned over the heads of the people to look at it. A neighbour noticed him, a ragged little fellow, who mistook the cause of Mulholland’s curiosity.

“Didn’t ye get any grub?” said the little man to Mulholland. “It’s yer own fault if ye didn’t. Come on, man. Don’t stand there hungry. Go on up to the counter.”

He caught Mulholland by the arm and tried to push him towards the counter.

“Leave me alone,” hissed Mulholland. “I don’t want any grub. Let go.”

“Go on up,” pursued the little fellow; “go on, man. Didn’t ye hear him say he was standin’ a round for everybody. Go on up.”

“Let go, I tell ye. Let go. I don’t want it, I say.”
===Chinese===
马尔霍兰德也伸长脖子去端详那张国库纸币。他一直站在门洞的夹角里，不出声，也不动弹。一看见那张一英镑钞票，他便闪进亮处，越过众人的头顶探头去看。旁边一个人注意到了他，一个衣衫褴褛的小个子，把马尔霍兰德这份好奇的缘故弄错了。

「你没领到吃的？」小个子对马尔霍兰德说。「没领到也是你自己招的。来吧，伙计。别干饿着站在那儿。上柜台前去。」

他抓住马尔霍兰德的胳膊，想把他朝柜台推过去。

「别碰我，」马尔霍兰德嘶声说。「我不要吃的。放手。」

「上去呀，」小个子不依不饶；「去啊，伙计。你没听见他说他要请每个人一份吗。去呀。」

「放手，我跟你说。放手。我不要，我说。」

===Original===
But it was no use for Mulholland to refuse. The more he refused the more the little fellow was determined that he should be fed. Others joined in, eager for some amazing reason or other that Mulholland should be fed. It seemed that they suspected something indecent and improper in Mulholland’s refusal to eat.

“Call out,” cried somebody, “call out for another ration. Bring it down to him.”

“Yes, why shouldn’t he have his share as well as the next?”

“Let me alone,” cried Mulholland in a rage; “let me alone, or I’ll smash yer skull for ye.”
===Chinese===
可马尔霍兰德怎么推辞也没用。他越推辞，小个子越是铁了心要让他吃上。旁人也掺和进来，为了这样那样莫名其妙的热心，非要马尔霍兰德吃上不可。看那意思，他们疑心他不吃里头有什么伤风败俗、不体面的名堂。

「喊一声，」有人喊，「再喊一份来。给他端过去。」

「就是，凭什么别人有份他没份？」

「别烦我，」马尔霍兰德勃然大怒；「别烦我，不然我敲碎你的脑壳。」

===Original===
That put a different aspect on the question. There were a dozen angry oaths.

“So that’s what’s the matter with ye. Yer lookin’ for fight, eh?”

“Stand back an’ let me at him,” cried somebody in the rear, pressing forward.

Mulholland tried to rush for the door, but they held on to him.

“What the hell is the matter now?” thundered Gypo, striding over.
===Chinese===
这一下事情变了样。十几声怒气冲冲的咒骂炸了开来。

「闹半天你是这么回事啊。你想打架，是吧？」

「闪开，让我去收拾他，」后面有人喊着往前挤。

马尔霍兰德想冲向门口，可人们死死拽住他不放。

「这又是他妈的怎么回事？」吉波大步过来，雷鸣般吼道。

===Original===
Immediately the scuffling stopped. Gypo came face to face with Mulholland. He saw Mulholland’s little eyes, gleaming and flashing like the eyes of a cat beset by dogs. There was a tense moment during which Gypo struggled with obscure suspicions. But suddenly the expression on Mulholland’s face changed into an expression of cunning intimacy. His face instead of being fierce and resentful suddenly seemed to say: “We are members of the Revolutionary Organization, you and I. Get this rabble out of my way.” Gypo immediately remembered Gallagher’s promise. He looked at Mulholland with a good-natured condescension. “Ha,” he thought, “this fellah’ll be useful.”

“Let him alone,” he cried arrogantly; “he’s a friend o’ mine. How are ye gettin’ on, Bartly?”

Then he continued carelessly, to impress the crowd with his own importance and his intimacy with the affairs of the Revolutionary Organization, which was the most impressive thing in the lives of those about him.

“Hear anythin’ yet about what I was tellin’ ye? I mean about the fellah that informed on Frankie McPhillip?”
===Chinese===
扭打立刻停了。吉波跟马尔霍兰德打了个照面。他看见马尔霍兰德那双小眼睛，贼亮贼亮的，像一只被群狗围住的猫的眼睛。有一阵子空气紧绷着，吉波在隐隐约约的疑心里挣扎。可突然间，马尔霍兰德脸上的表情换成了一种狡黠的亲热。他那张脸方才还凶狠怨愤，此刻却仿佛在说：「你我是革命组织的同志。把这帮乌合之众从我眼前弄开。」吉波立刻想起了加拉格尔的许诺。他带着好心的屈尊望着马尔霍兰德。「哈，」他想，「这家伙会有用处。」

「别碰他，」他傲气十足地喊道；「他是我的朋友。你混得怎么样啊，巴特利？」

随后他又漫不经心地接着说，好让人群领教他自己的分量，以及他同革命组织事务的交情——在他周围这些人的一生里，那可是最叫人肃然起敬的东西。

「我先前跟你说的事，有什么动静没有？我是说，那个告发弗兰基·麦克菲利普的家伙。」

===Original===
Mulholland was amazed for a moment. What audacity! But it was not audacity. Gypo had completely forgotten the ponderous fellow in the little tattered round hat who had gone into the police-station. His sudden conceit had completely swallowed that ponderous fellow.

“He must be drunk,” thought Mulholland. He said aloud, whispering to Gypo, as he bent his head close and turned up his face sideways in his peculiar manner, “I was just passin’ an’ saw ye. I just thought I’d drop in an’ tell ye I’d be there at one o’clock. Ye know where I mean? No, we didn’t hear anythin’ yet about that.”

He winked his right eye. Gypo winked his right eye and nodded solemnly. Then Mulholland walked quickly out the door, evidently going off somewhere in a hurry. But he halted at the corner of the lane, distended his eyes and gritted his teeth. He rubbed his chin meditatively, looking at the ground. He couldn’t make it out, whatever it was, that was troubling his mind.

Gypo turned once more to the counter and continued his meal. He ate as if he were about to travel for days and he were deliberately devouring a store of food sufficient to last to the end of the journey. Behind him and on either side of him, they were talking about his strength and praising him, but he paid no heed to them. He was immersed in dreams about his future, now that Gallagher was going to take him back again into the Organization.
===Chinese===
马尔霍兰德一时惊呆了。好大的胆子！可这并不是胆子大。吉波已经把那个戴破烂小圆帽、走进警察局的沉重大汉忘了个干净。他那突如其来的自负，把那个沉重大汉整个吞了下去。

「他准是喝醉了，」马尔霍兰德想。他出声说道——凑近吉波，用他那特有的姿势侧仰起脸，低声道：「我刚巧路过，瞧见了你。我想着进来跟你说一声，一点钟我准到。你明白我指的是哪儿吧？没有，那件事我们还没听到什么动静。」

他眨了眨右眼。吉波也眨了眨右眼，郑重地点了点头。随后马尔霍兰德快步出了店门，分明是有急事要赶到什么地方去。可他在巷子拐角上停了下来，瞪鼓起眼睛，咬得牙关咯咯响。他若有所思地搓着下巴，望着地面。不管是什么在搅扰他的脑子，他怎么也琢磨不透。

吉波又转回柜台前，接着吃他的。他那吃相，仿佛就要出门远行多少天，正存心吞吃一份够撑到旅程尽头的存粮。他背后、他两侧，人们谈论着他的力气，夸着他，可他全不理会。如今加拉格尔就要重新把他领回组织里去了，他沉浸在关于自己前程的迷梦里。

===Original===
“Aha!” cried an old woman, with watery blue eyes and a wrinkled white face, as she shook her fist upwards at him, “I wish I had a son like ye. Me own Jimmy, Lord Have Mercy on him, was killed in the big war. He was the boy that could bate the polis! Don’t be talkin’. I seen him wan night an’ it took six o’ them to pull him off a coal cart an’ he holdin’ on to the horse’s reins all the time with wan hand while he was fightin’ them with th’ other.”

She stamped on the floor and yelled, her eyes gleaming ferociously, as if the contemplation of her dead son’s fight gave her tangible pleasure. Then she walked towards the door, trailing her shawl and her arms with bravado. The poor woman was slightly insane as the result of paralysis.

A tall, sour-faced, lean man, with a red nose shaped like a reversed scimitar, who had just come in, looked after the old woman and shook his head. He mumbled something under his breath. The old lady halted and looked at him contemptuously.

“What are ye sniggerin’ at,” she cried, “you with a face like a plate o’ burnt porridge?”

There was a loud laugh.
===Chinese===
「啊哈！」一个老妇人喊道，她有一双水汪汪的蓝眼睛和一张皱纹密布的白脸，一面朝上朝他挥着拳头，「我要是有你这么个儿子就好了。我自己的吉米，愿天主垂怜他，死在了那场大战里。他才是那种打得过警察的小子呢！这可不是吹的。我亲眼见过，一天夜里，六条警察才把他从一辆运煤车上拽下来，他一只手从头到尾攥着马缰绳，另一只手还跟他们对打。」

她跺着地板叫嚷，眼里凶光闪闪，仿佛回味亡儿的那一架，能给她摸得着的快乐。随后她朝门口走去，披巾拖曳着，两条胳膊也大大咧咧地甩着，一派逞威的做派。这可怜的女人因瘫痪落下了点疯病。

一个瘦高个、刻薄脸的男人刚进门，红鼻子的形状像一柄倒转过来的弯刀。他目送着老妇人，摇了摇头，压着嗓子咕哝了句什么。老太太停住脚，轻蔑地打量他。

「你窃笑什么，」她嚷道，「就凭你那张脸，活像一盘烧糊的麦片粥？」

哄堂大笑。

===Original===
“Mary Hynes,” said the hook-nosed man, “if ye were more careful of yer son’s upbringin’ an’ of yer own immortal soul, ye wouldn’t be in the state ye are in now. Is it boastin’ of yer son’s lawlessness ye are? Are ye boastin’ of his livin’ crimes an’ he already gone to meet his God?”

The hook-nosed man raised his right hand dramatically to point at the ceiling and he glared at the old woman with fierce and menacing sorrow. But his words produced a contrary effect to that which he expected on the old woman. She looked at him contemptuously and then curled her mouth up in anger.

“Yerrah, d’ye call it a crime to bate a policeman?” she cried in amazed indignation.

“Certainly it is a crime,” cried the hook-nosed man.

“Damn an’ blast it, what are ye talkin’ about, Boxer Lydon?” cried a burly fellow coming up to Lydon and staring him excitedly and angrily in the face. “Didn’t ye hear of what the polis did today to Frankie McPhillip? D’ye call it a crime to bate that murderin’ lot? Aye or shoot them either!”
===Chinese===
「玛丽·海因斯（Mary Hynes），」那鹰钩鼻男人说，「你要是多操点心管教儿子，多顾着点你自己不朽的灵魂，也不至于落到今天这步田地。你这是在夸你儿子的无法无天吗？他人都已经去见他的天主了，你还在夸他活着时犯下的罪孽吗？」

鹰钩鼻男人戏剧性地举起右手直指天花板，带着凶狠威吓的悲怆瞪着老妇人。可他的话在老妇人身上产生的效果，跟他指望的恰恰相反。她轻蔑地望了他一眼，随即愤怒地撇了撇嘴。

「得了吧，打一顿警察也算罪过？」她叫起来，又是惊愕又是愤慨。

「当然是罪过，」鹰钩鼻男人叫道。

「该死的，遭瘟的，你胡扯些什么，『拳师』莱登（Boxer Lydon）？」一条魁梧的汉子嚷着逼近莱登，激动而愤怒地直瞪着他的脸。「你没听说警察今天对弗兰基·麦克菲利普干了什么吗？打那帮杀人的货色，你管那叫罪过？打了不算，毙了也不算！」

===Original===
“I don’t say they were justified in what they did today,” cried Lydon, raising his voice to a querulous shout in order to drown the uproar; “but neither will I say that the dead man was justified in what he done. Do none o’ ye think o’ the man McPhillip killed? Wasn’t he a fellow-man like yersel’? Wasn’t he an Irishman of the same flesh an’ blood?”

“Aw! that’s nationalism,” cried somebody. “What’s an Irishman no more than a Turk? Ye belong to the I.R.B., an’ that’s where ye get yer lingo. Up the workers!”

The hook-nosed man paused with his hand raised until the interrupter finished. Then he continued unmoved:

“Do none o’ ye think that maybe that man left a mother an’ a—”
===Chinese===
「我不是说他们今天干的事就有理，」莱登嚷道，把嗓门抬成一声尖利的吼喊，好压过喧闹；「可我也不会说那个死人干的事就有理。你们就没有一个人想想麦克菲利普杀掉的那个人吗？他不也是跟你们一样的人吗？不也是同样的血肉做的爱尔兰人吗？」

「呸！这是民族主义，」有人喊。「爱尔兰人又比土耳其人强到哪里去？你是爱尔兰共和兄弟会（I.R.B.）的人，你那套腔调就是打那儿学来的。工人万岁！」

鹰钩鼻男人举着手顿住，等打断他的人说完。随后他不动声色地接着说：

「你们就没有一个人想想，那个人兴许撇下了一个老母亲，还有——」

===Original===
But he had to stop. His voice was drowned in the uproar and the scuffling. The old woman began to sing “Kelly the Boy from Killane,” as she strolled out the door. Another man was pushing his way in through the crowd at the door towards the hook-nosed man. This newcomer had been standing at the door for some time. He was dressed from head to foot in a heavy black overcoat. He was better dressed than everybody present, but he looked as pale and haggard as the others. His face continually twitched and his eyes were bloodshot. He looked at the hook-nosed man fiercely and seized him nervously by the buttonhole. The hook-nosed man edged away.

“For God’s sake, let up on that rubbish,” cried the newcomer, stammering at each word. His upper lip was contorting as if he were in a fit.

“Let me go,” cried the hook-nosed man. “I’ll have my say, an’ I won’t be intimidated by any Socialist agitator. Keep back from me.”

“I only wanted to tell ye,” shouted the other, “I only wanted to tell ye … I say … I say …”
===Chinese===
可他不得不停住。他的声音淹没在喧闹和扭打里。老妇人一边踱出门去，一边唱起了《基兰来的少年凯利》（Kelly the Boy from Killane）。又有一个男人分开门口的人群，朝鹰钩鼻男人挤过来。这新来的在门口已经站了有一阵了。他从头上到脚下裹着一件厚重的黑大衣。他比在场所有人穿得都体面，可脸色却跟旁人一样苍白憔悴。他的脸不停地抽动，两眼布满血丝。他凶狠地瞪着鹰钩鼻男人，神经质地一把揪住他的扣眼。鹰钩鼻男人侧身躲开。

「看在天主份上，别再讲那套废话了，」新来的嚷道，一个字一个字地打着结巴。他的上唇扭曲着，像发病一般。

「放开我，」鹰钩鼻男人喊。「我的话一定要说完，随便哪个社会党煽动家都吓不住我。离我远点。」

「我只是想告诉你，」对方喊着，「我只是想告诉你……我说……我说……」

===Original===
Then nothing could be distinguished above the uproar. Everybody present took part in the argument. The ragged fellows who had come in with Gypo, curiously enough, took no interest in the argument. Those of them that had not already disappeared as soon as they got their food, now took their leave when the argument began. There was even a look of fear in their faces as they slunk away, as if this demonstration of interest in the affairs of the world terrified them, who had no interest in anything, since their souls were numbed by the hopelessness of despair. Only a few of the most wretched remained, crouching against the counter, in the comforting shadow of Gypo’s immensity. They remained because the presence of his powerful personality comforted them and gave them the imaginary feeling of having something to protect them from the menace of civilized life.

Those that were now taking part in the argument were of a better class. They were workers of all sorts, members of trade unions and respectable people. They had appeared somehow, one by one but rapidly, in that mysterious way in which crowds of people of a certain type gather in the Titt Street district and carry on an argument with furious heat.

Gypo suddenly turned around and looked at the wrangling group, at the open mouths, the listening ears, the distorted faces, the glittering eyes. He listened. He blinked. Then he laughed softly within himself. He felt a crazy desire to yell and fall on them with his fists. The mixed murmur of their agitated voices had a maddening effect on him. But he looked back at the counter. He still had food to eat. He continued his meal. The argument went on.
===Chinese===
随后，喧闹声之上就什么也分辨不出了。在场人人都掺和进了这场争论。说来奇怪，跟吉波一同进来的那些破衣烂衫的家伙，对这场争论毫无兴趣。他们之中，凡是一拿到吃的就早已溜掉的那些不算，剩下的也在争论一起头就告了辞。他们溜走时脸上甚至带着惧色，仿佛这种对世间大事的热心把他们吓着了——他们对什么都不热心，因为他们的灵魂早被绝望的无可救药冻麻木了。只有几个最不济的还留在那儿，蜷在柜台边上，躲在吉波那庞然巨躯令人安心的影子里。他们留下来，是因为他那强横人格的在场安抚着他们，给了他们一种想象中的倚仗，仿佛有什么东西护着他们，抵挡文明生活的威胁。

此刻掺和进争论的这些人，出身就要好上一等。他们是各行各业的工人、工会会员和体面人物。他们不知怎么就冒了出来，一个接一个，却来得飞快，正是那一类人在蒂特街一带聚拢起来的神秘方式——聚起来便带着狂怒的火气展开一场争论。

吉波猛地转过身，望着那群吵嚷的人，望着一张张张开的嘴、一只只侧听的耳朵、一张张扭曲的脸、一双双发亮的眼睛。他听了听。他眨了眨眼。然后他在肚子里暗暗笑了。他起了一种疯狂的欲望，想呐喊一声，抡起拳头朝他们扑过去。他们那些激动的嗓音混成一片嘤嘤嗡嗡，对他有一种逼人发狂的效力。可他回头看了看柜台。他还有吃的没吃完。他继续吃他的。争论继续着。

===Original===
The man with the long overcoat who had just arrived held the attention of the crowd. He was a well-known man in the district and all over the city. He owned a small tobacconist and newsvendor’s shop. He was called The Crank Shanahan and indeed he was a crank. He belonged to no organization, he went about alone, he attended every political meeting in the city and he was continually, in all weathers, agitating and preaching in a loud shrill voice his own peculiar philosophy of social life. That philosophy was a mixture of all sorts of political creeds, but its main basis was revolt against every existing institution, habit or belief. He was called an anarchist, but he was not an anarchist. He was just a fanatic who was dissatisfied with life. At night he was given to fearfully morbid thoughts that caused him to lock and bar himself in his room and sleep with the blankets right over his head. He was even supposed to put cotton-wool into his ears at night lest he might hear a sound. And once the policeman on duty found him wandering around the street in which he lived, at three o’clock in the morning, dressed in a torn nightshirt, trembling and gnashing his teeth with terror. He had jumped up horrified by a nightmare and rushed out in that state.

“Listen,” he cried. “I don’t agree with the Revolutionary Organization, but the man that killed McPhillip … no … no, no … I mean the man … can’t ye let me speak? … I mean the farmer that McPhillip killed, he was an agent of the capitalist class. Then it follows logically that he was an enemy of the working class. McPhillip was an agent of the working class. He was justified in killing the man. That’s the matter treated logically and brought to a logical conclusion. Everything must be approached logically. Listen. Taking the question from a wider standpoint we can get a broader judgment that will fit all cases of the kind that may arise”—he raised his voice to a scream to drown the noise of a scuffle at the door—“in the near future. We are at the base of a world revolutionary wave. According as that wave advances and gathers strength the whole process of capitalist society will crumble up. Then there will be a gradual increase in the number of these skirmishes, as it were on the …”
===Chinese===
那个刚到的长外套男人攥住了人群的注意力。他是本区乃至全城的名人。他开着一爿小小的烟草报纸铺。他被称作「怪人」沙纳汉（The Crank Shanahan），他也确实是个怪人。他不属于任何组织，独来独往，城里每场政治集会他必到，而且不论什么天气，时时刻刻都在用高亢尖利的嗓门鼓吹布道，宣讲他自己那套独特的社会人生哲学。那套哲学是各种政治信条的大杂烩，根基却只有一条：造一切现存制度、习俗、信仰的反。人家叫他无政府主义者，可他并不是无政府主义者。他只是个对生活不满意的偏执狂。一到夜里，他就爱生出种种怕人的病态念头，吓得他把自己锁进房间上了闩，睡觉连头都蒙在毯子底下。据说他夜里甚至要往耳朵里塞棉花，生怕听见一点声响。有一回，值夜的警察凌晨三点钟发现他在自家住的那条街上晃荡，身上只有一件撕破的睡衣，吓得浑身发抖，牙齿咬得咯咯响。他是做了噩梦惊跳起来，就那么一副样子冲出来的。

「听着，」他喊。「我不赞成革命组织，可是杀死麦克菲利普的那个人……不……不，不……我是说那个人……你们就不能让我说句话吗？……我是说，麦克菲利普杀掉的那个农夫，他是资本家阶级的一个代理人。由此顺理成章地推下来，他就是工人阶级的一个敌人。麦克菲利普是工人阶级的一个代理人。他杀掉那个人是有理的。这件事就是这么用逻辑来处理、用逻辑推出结论的。凡事都必须用逻辑来对待。听着。把这个问题从一个更宽的立足点来看，我们就能得出一个更宽的判断，适用于将来可能发生——」他把嗓门抬成一声尖叫，好压过门口一场扭打的喧闹——「的一切同类案件，而且为期不远。我们正处在一道世界革命大浪的浪根上。随着那道大浪推进、聚积力量，资本主义社会的整个进程就会土崩瓦解。到那时，这类前哨遭遇战就会逐渐增多，好比说，就在……」

===Original===
His voice was drowned suddenly by a big man who began to swing his arms about his head uttering a fearful torrent of oaths. He was drunk. Then Lydon shouted:

“Murder is murder, I say. Murder is always murder and the gospel of Our Lord Jesus Christ says—”

“There must be no mercy,” yelled a little man with a black moustache, who rushed into a corner where he had room to prance about. “There must be no mercy. To hell with everybody. That right, boys? Wha’?”

“What are ye talkin’ about?” cried Gypo, suddenly turning about.
===Chinese===
一个大个子突然把他的声音淹没了——那大个子抡开双臂在脑袋周围乱舞，倾泻出一大串骇人的咒骂。他喝醉了。随后莱登高喊：

「杀人就是杀人，我说。杀人永远是杀人，而我们的主耶稣基督的福音书上说——」

「绝不能留情，」一个黑胡子小个子尖叫道，他冲进一个角落，那儿宽敞，容得他蹦跶。「绝不能留情。统统都见鬼去吧。对不对，弟兄们？啊？」

「你们嚷嚷什么呢？」吉波叫道，猛地转过身来。

===Original===
Silence fell immediately. Everybody looked at him. His face was perspiring. He rubbed his hands on his chest. He curled up his lips. He gave his little hat a slight push towards the back of his head.

Then he was seized by another fit of strange humour. He yelled once more and staggered towards the crowd, with his arms hanging loose, pretending to be dead drunk. They fell away from him in amazement. He stood in the middle of the room and looked about him.

“What ye talkin’ about?” he drawled ponderously, swaying backwards and forwards.

He glared from face to face, but each pair of eyes was turned away as he sought them. He was delighted with the terror he caused. Behind the counter the Italian, still smiling, had grasped a large knife and stood perfectly still. The girl was crouching on the floor. Then Gypo broke into a loud laugh, stuck his hands in his trousers pockets and strolled towards the door.
===Chinese===
寂静立刻落下。人人都望着他。他的脸冒着汗。他两手在胸口搓了搓。他卷起嘴唇。他把小帽子朝脑后轻轻推了推。

随后他又被一阵古怪的兴头攫住了。他又呐喊了一声，两条胳膊松松地吊着，装成烂醉如泥的样子，朝人群踉跄过去。人们惊愕地从他身边退散开。他站在屋子当中，环顾四周。

「你们嚷嚷什么呢？」他沉甸甸地拖着腔调说，身子前俯后仰地晃着。

他一张脸一张脸地瞪过去，可他的目光寻到哪里，哪里的眼睛就躲开。他为自己造成的恐怖得意极了。柜台后面，那意大利人仍旧笑着，手里却已经攥住一把大刀，站得纹丝不动。姑娘蜷缩在地板上。随后吉波放声大笑，双手插进裤袋，朝门口踱去。

===Original===
He hesitated for a moment outside the door. Then he headed straight across the road. They all ran to the door to look after him. His huge long frame, clad in blue dungarees that clung to his thighs, shone in the light of the lamps as he crossed the wide road, one foot advancing past the other slowly, the trousers brushing with the sound of hay being cut with a scythe. Then the figure left the area of light and grew dim as he gained the pavement at the far side, and turned to the left under the shadow of an abrupt tall house. Then he fell away into the night.

Presently a lean slouching figure crept across the street in pursuit. He also disappeared under the shadow of the abrupt house. Nobody noticed him. It was Mulholland tracking Gypo.
===Chinese===
他在门外踌躇了片刻。随后他径直朝马路对面走去。他们全都跑到门口望着他的背影。他那硕长的身架，裹着紧贴大腿的蓝粗布工装裤，在一路街灯的光里发亮，一面横过那条宽阔的马路，一只脚缓缓地赶过另一只脚，两条裤腿相互摩擦，发出镰刀割干草般的声响。随后那身影走出了灯光的地界，暗淡下去——他到了对面的路缘，在一家陡然拔起的高房子的阴影里向左一转。随后他便隐没进了夜色。

不多时，一条精瘦佝偻的身影蹑手蹑脚穿过马路，追了上去。他也消失在那座高房子的阴影底下。谁也没有留意他。那是马尔霍兰德，正在跟踪吉波。

## X / 十

===Original===
At fifteen minutes to one, Bartly Mulholland entered Biddy Burke’s kitchen and sat by the fire. Nobody addressed him. He saluted nobody. Biddy Burke was sitting on the other side of the fire, on a stool, smoking a cigarette.

Biddy Burke was a middle-aged woman with a lowering expression in her black eyes, with puffed-out, sallow cheeks and a swollen throat. She was of the type of Irishwoman that is prone to sudden passions, due to the habit of eating enormous meals and then suffering from digestive disorders. They are tenderhearted people, utterly lacking in an aesthetic sense, violent, quarrelsome, savage, generous, inconsistent. Biddy was dressed in a white blouse and a blue skirt. She wore her greyish hair drawn back to her poll tightly and parted in the middle according to the peasant fashion.

There were other people in the room, two young women who sat on chairs and Jimmy “the fancy man,” who lay on his right side, on the settle opposite the fire.
===Chinese===
十二点四十五分，巴特利·马尔霍兰德走进碧蒂·伯克的厨房，在炉火旁坐下。没人跟他搭话。他也没跟任何人打招呼。碧蒂·伯克坐在炉火另一边的一张凳子上，抽着一支香烟。

碧蒂·伯克是个中年妇人，黑眼睛里透着一副阴沉的神色，蜡黄的面颊鼓鼓胀胀，喉咙也虚肿着。她属于那一类爱尔兰女人：惯于暴饮暴食、随后又受消化不良的罪，因而动不动就火气上撞。这类人心肠软得不行，全无审美感，暴烈、好斗、粗野、慷慨、反复无常。碧蒂上身穿一件白罩衫，下身一条蓝裙子。她那头花白头发按乡下女人的样式从正中分开，紧紧地梳拢到脑后。

屋里还有别的人：两个坐在椅子上的年轻女人，还有「小白脸」吉米（Jimmy “the fancy man”），他朝右侧着身子，躺在炉火对面的高背长椅上。

===Original===
Mulholland looked around the room slowly. Then he spoke.

“Was Gypo Nolan here this evenin’, Mrs. Burke?” he said.

Biddy Burke slowly shook her head, carefully examining Mulholland’s face as she did so. Then, as if she suddenly remembered something important, she leaned forward and bunched her lips together.

“There hasn’t a man stood within me door this blessed night,” she said in her rough, croaky voice. “No, nor damn the bottle o’ stout did I sell. That’s the God’s truth. Some people find Biddy Burke all right when they’re in trouble an’ they got nothin’, but when their tune changes they give her a wide berth. I’ll soon be in the workhouse at the rate things are goin’. I never saw anythin’ like it. The country is goin’ to the wall. That’s all there’s to it. I knew they’d make a mess of it with their revolutions an’ their shootin’ the peelers. Not that I didn’t do me bit to help the boys, God bless ’em, but ’tisn’t the boys that done the fightin’ that get the jobs. So it isn’t. It never is, if ye ask Biddy Burke. It’s them publicans an’ bishops that were always top dog in this country. ’Twas that way before an’ ’tis that way now an’ ’twill be that way when Biddy Burke is goin’ to meet her God on the day of judgment. They were talkin’ about English tyrants, but sure nobody ever saw the likes o’ these tyrants with their searches an’ their raids, an’ every divil’s wart of a farmer’s son that can pull on his breeches without his mother’s help, runnin’ around an’ callin’ himself a gineral. Aw! Gypo Nolan! He’s like the rest o’ them, Bartly Mulholland. You take it from Biddy. Indeed then, he hasn’t set a foot within me door. It’s not that I haven’t heard of his goin’s on though. Huh!”
===Chinese===
马尔霍兰德慢慢环视屋内。随后他开了口。

「今天晚上吉波·诺兰来过这儿吗，伯克太太？」他说。

碧蒂·伯克慢慢摇头，一面摇，一面细细察看马尔霍兰德的脸色。随后她像是猛然想起了什么要紧事，朝前探出身子，把嘴唇嘟成一撮。

「这个大吉大利的晚上，就没有一个男人跨过我家的门槛，」她用那副粗哑破锣般的嗓音说。「没有，连一瓶黑啤我都没卖出去，该死的。这是千真万确的实话。有些人倒了霉、一无所有的时候，倒觉得碧蒂·伯克这人不错，可他们的调子一变，就躲我躲得远远的。照这样下去，我不久就要进济贫院了。这样的事我从来没见过。这个国家是完蛋了。就是这么回事。我早知道，他们那套革命、那套朝警察开枪，早晚要闹得一塌糊涂。倒不是说我没出过一把力帮那些小伙子，上帝保佑他们，可打下江山打仗的弟兄们偏偏捞不着差事。就是这么不公。你要问碧蒂·伯克，从来都是这样。在这个国家，从来是那些酒馆老板和主教骑在别人头上。从前是这样，现在是这样，等碧蒂·伯克末日审判那天去见她的天主的时候，还会是这样。他们从前总念叨英国暴君，可真的，谁也没见过这样的暴君——抄家、搜捕，还有每一个能不用娘帮忙自己套上裤子的农夫家的癞皮儿子，都满街跑着自称将军。呸！吉波·诺兰！他跟他们那帮人一个样，巴特利·马尔霍兰德。这话你记下，是碧蒂说的。说实在的，他真没踏进过我家门。不过要说没听说过他那些勾当，倒也不是。哼！」

===Original===
“What did ye hear about him?” asked Mulholland, peering at her.

“What did I hear about him?” cried Biddy Burke. “What d’ye take me for, Bartly Mulholland? An information bureau or what? Don’t be botherin’ me.”

Mulholland sighed. Then he took out his pipe and lit it. He put his back against the wall and began to smoke in apparent comfort. There was silence. Through the open street door sounds of footsteps and of voices came in through the rain now and again. They were subdued sounds. It seemed that everything was waiting for something monstrous to happen.

The two young women began in their gruff, cracked voices to discuss the death of Francis Joseph McPhillip. They talked casually, in whispers, indifferently.

Mulholland peered at them for a moment. Then he sank back into his thoughts. His thoughts just then were not at all comfortable. He had lost track of Gypo. He had been wandering about trying to find his quarry again, absolutely without success. Gypo had been swallowed up. A more nervous man than Mulholland would have not taken the matter so philosophically, so coolly. Because if Gypo could not be found again, Mulholland’s own life would be in serious danger. But Mulholland was not considering that aspect of the affair. Mulholland was a sincere revolutionist. It was the danger to the “cause” that worried him. The “cause” was his whole existence. He did not understand any other purpose in life except the achievement of an Irish Workers’ Republic.
===Chinese===
「你听见他什么了？」马尔霍兰德问，眼睛盯着她。

「我听见他什么了？」碧蒂·伯克叫起来。「你把碧蒂·伯克当什么人了，巴特利·马尔霍兰德？当问事处还是怎么的？别来烦我。」

马尔霍兰德叹了口气。随后他掏出烟斗，点着了。他把背靠在墙上，一副显然很受用的样子抽起烟来。屋内一片沉默。敞开的临街门外，隔着雨不时传来脚步声和人语声。都是压低了的声音。仿佛一切都在等待一桩骇人的事情发生。

两个年轻女人用她们粗嘎破裂的嗓音，开始谈论弗朗西斯·约瑟夫·麦克菲利普的死。她们说得随随便便，窃窃私语，漠不关心。

马尔霍兰德盯着她们望了一会儿。随后他又沉回自己的心事里。他此刻的心绪可一点也不安生。他跟丢了吉波。他一直四处转悠，想重新找到他的猎物，却毫无结果。吉波被吞没了。换一个比马尔霍兰德更神经质的人，决不会把事情看得这么达观、这么冷静。因为要是再找不着吉波，马尔霍兰德自己的性命就会大有危险。可马尔霍兰德考虑的不是这一层。马尔霍兰德是个真诚的革命者。让他忧心的是「事业」遭到的危险。「事业」是他的整个存在。除了成就一个爱尔兰工人共和国，他不理解人生还有什么别的目的。

===Original===
Still﻿ ﻿… as he sat on the stool, stoically smoking his pipe, other worries came into his mind. If he could not find Gypo and anything serious happened to himself as a result, what would become of his wife and his six young children? He hardly ever thought of them seriously, in this way, with a view to the future. The future held a workers’ republic, somewhere in the distance, when there would be no slums, no hunger, no sick wives, no children that got the mumps and the rickets and the German measles and the whooping-cough with devilish regularity. It never worried him to think that his wife and his six children were for the moment living in a miserable slum shanty, with his wife going rapidly into a decline through hard work. That had to be. The “cause” was above all these things. Why! It was his wife who often urged him on to give all his time to the “cause” whenever he became slightly despondent or disheartened, timorous or apathetic.

Ever struggling without reward!

So he thought suddenly. But almost as soon as the thought entered his brain another thought came in mad, bloodshot pursuit. He pulled savagely at his pipe and ejected the first thought in terror.

Even “mentally” it was dangerous to think of leaving the Organization without being expelled. After all﻿ ﻿… terror was the foundation of his zeal.

He forced himself into his habitual calm. His face assumed the impenetrable aspect which he had developed during five years of constant practice. He turned to Biddy Burke again.
===Chinese===
不过……他坐在凳子上，坚忍地抽着烟斗，别样的忧愁又涌上心头。要是他找不着吉波，自己又因此遭了什么不测，他的女人和六个年幼的孩子可怎么办？他几乎从来没有这样认真地想到过他们，这样为将来盘算过。将来是有一个工人共和国的，在遥远的地方等着，到那时没有贫民窟，没有饥饿，没有缠绵病榻的女人，没有隔三差五准时闹腮腺炎、佝偻病、风疹、百日咳的孩子。想到自己的女人和六个孩子此刻正挤在一间破烂的贫民窟棚屋里、女人正让苦活儿拖得一天天亏虚下去，他也从没为此不安过。那是没办法的事。「事业」高于这一切。可不是嘛！每逢他稍有沮丧气馁、畏怯冷淡的时候，往往正是他的女人催着他，让他把全部时间都交给「事业」。

永远奋斗，不求回报！

他忽然这样想着。可这念头几乎刚一进脑子，另一个念头就红着眼睛疯狂地追了上来。他狠狠嘬了一口烟斗，心怀恐惧地把头一个念头逐了出去。

就是在「心里」想着不待开除便脱离组织，也是危险的。说到底……恐怖正是他那份热忱的地基。

他强迫自己回到惯常的平静里。他的脸上摆出那副莫测高深的神情——五年间不断修炼养成的。他又转向碧蒂·伯克。

===Original===
“Where did ye say ye saw Gypo carrying on?” he said casually.

Biddy Burke looked at him ferociously, emitting two columns of cigarette smoke through her fat nostrils.

“I didn’t say I saw him carryin’ on anywhere, Bartly Mulholland,” she said angrily. “Be the holy! These late years every one o’ ye is as smart as a corporation lawyer. Now look here, Bartly. I don’t want to have any truck atall with ye or yer crowd. Ye know that too. I know ye, me fine bucko, an’ I don’t think﻿ ﻿… eh﻿ ﻿… well o’ course, Bartly﻿ ﻿… ye know what I mane.﻿ ﻿… It’s not﻿ ﻿… uh﻿ ﻿… that I mane any harm﻿ ﻿… but a poor woman like mesel’﻿ ﻿… o’ course I’m ready as I said before to do me duty for me fellow-men﻿ ﻿… but it’s like this﻿ ﻿… what does a woman like me gain be gettin’ mixed up in politics﻿ ﻿… that is o’ course﻿ ﻿… look here,” she continued in a lower voice, “I heard he was up in Aunt Betty’s, raisin’ hell up there. He was one o’ your crowd, wasn’t he?”

Mulholland looked at her sombrely. She drew back immediately.

“Well, ye know me well, Bartly,” she muttered apologetically and nervously. “I’m not sayin’ anythin’ out o’ place. Am I, girls? Sure﻿—”

Just then an interruption came from outside. Footsteps came rushing to the door. Then gasps were heard. Then a panting sound became audible. Then Katie Fox burst into the room, with her right hand on her hip, her eyes glittering, looking about her wildly. She rushed up to Biddy Burke. She bent down from the hips towards her and began to speak immediately, gasping after each word.
===Chinese===
「你刚才说在哪儿看见吉波胡闹来着？」他随口问道。

碧蒂·伯克恶狠狠地瞪着他，两个肥鼻孔里喷出两道烟柱。

「我可没说我在哪儿看见他胡闹来着，巴特利·马尔霍兰德，」她怒气冲冲地说。「老天在上！这几年，你们一个个都精得跟市政律师似的。你听我说，巴特利。我不想跟你，也不想跟你们那一伙，有任何一丁点儿瓜葛。这你自己也知道。我看透你了，我的好汉，我可不觉得……呃……这个当然啦，巴特利……你明白我的意思。……我不是……唔……存心使坏……可像我这样一个苦命女人……当然喽，我先前也说过，我随时愿意为同胞尽我的本分……可事情是这样的……像我这样一个女人，搅进政治里去能捞着什么好处……当然也就是说……你听着，」她压低声音继续说，「我听说他在贝蒂大妈那儿，在上头闹翻了天。他原是你们一伙的，对不对？」

马尔霍兰德阴沉地望着她。她立刻缩了回去。

「呃，你是了解我的，巴特利，」她抱歉而慌张地咕哝道。「我没说一句出格的话。是不是，姑娘们？真是——」

正在这时，外头来了动静。一阵脚步声冲向门口。接着听得见喘气声。接着喘息声越来越响。随后凯蒂·福克斯一下子闯进屋来，右手叉着腰，两眼放光，狂乱地朝四下里张望。她冲到碧蒂·伯克跟前。她从腰际朝她弯下身去，立刻开了口，说一个字喘一口气。

===Original===
“What d’ye think of it, Biddy?” she cried. “D’ye know where I found him? D’ye know where I found him? The big hulkin’ waster! An’ she that’s not to fit to walk the same street as me with her big, ugly arms around his neck! She laughed in me face. She laughed”﻿—screaming﻿—“in me face! I wish to God I had hit her with the bottle I threw. That ud spoil her mug. Though it was spoiled enough the day she was born. Who was she, may I ask? Who was she, Biddy Burke? I’m askin’ ye. Ye don’t know an’ ye’d never guess in a thousand years. Who would she be but me bould Connemara Maggie! That imperent trapster that came up here last year as a skivvy in a Gaelic Leaguer’s house, one o’ them crazy fellahs that goes around in kilts. She came up here an’ before she was three months in town she was put in the family way be a soldier. Then she comes down here, with her curly locks an’ her big face like a heifer, savin’ the comparison. I pushed up past Aunt Betty in the hall an’ she shoutin’ after me. I bust into the room an’ there he was, sittin’ on the floor, with his legs spread out, drinkin’ outa the neck of a bottle, laughin’ like a fool, with her sittin’ beside him. ‘Hello, Katie,’ says he, ‘d’ye want a drink?’ ‘ ’Twill do ye good,’ says she with a giggle. Me curse on her! I gave him a bit o’ me mind an’﻿ ﻿… Biddy, for God’s sake, gimme a drink o’ water. Biddy, listen.”

She threw herself suddenly at Biddy’s feet and began to moan. But almost immediately she jumped to her feet again and cried out:

“An’ what’s more he gave three quid to that swank of an Englishwoman. He gave her three quid and he paid two quid more to Aunt Betty, money that was owin’ to her for board, an’ he never gave me a penny. Me that kept him for the last six months when I hadn’t a bite mesel’. But I’ll tell everybody. I’ll tell.”

She looked around her wildly. She saw Mulholland. She came up to him and bent down close to his face. Her hat trailed off. Her hair fell down over her eyes. She swayed. She pointed her right forefinger menacingly at Mulholland’s forehead.
===Chinese===
「你说说这是什么事，碧蒂？」她喊道。「你知道我在哪儿找见他的？你知道我在哪儿找见他的？那个傻大个废物！还有她那个贱货，拿那双又粗又丑的胳膊搂着他的脖子，也配跟我走同一条街！她当面笑话我。她当面」——尖声叫喊——「笑话我！我真希望上帝保佑，我扔的那个瓶子砸中了她。那才叫毁了她的脸盘子呢。虽说打她落地那天起，那张脸也毁得够可以的了。我倒要问问，她算老几？她算老几，碧蒂·伯克？我在问你。你不知道，你猜一万年也猜不着。除了我那位大胆的康尼马拉的玛吉，还能是谁！那个不要脸的骚货，去年上城里来，在盖尔联盟（Gaelic League）一个会员家里当打杂的女仆——就是那种穿着苏格兰短裙满街乱跑的疯家伙。她上来没满三个月，就挺起了肚子，是叫一个大兵干的。随后她就下到这边来了，顶着一头卷毛，一张小母牛似的大脸——恕我打这个比方。我从过道里推开贝蒂大妈挤上去，她还在我背后嚷嚷。我撞进房间，他就在那儿，坐在地板上，两腿劈开着，对着瓶颈灌酒，笑得像个傻子，她就坐在他旁边。『哈罗，凯蒂，』他说，『来一杯不？』『喝了对你有好处，』她咯咯笑着说。让她遭天咒！我把他数落了一顿，还有……碧蒂，看在上帝份上，给我口水喝。碧蒂，你听着。」

她猛地扑倒在碧蒂脚边，呻吟起来。可几乎立刻又蹦起身，喊道：

「还有呢，他给了那个臭显摆的英国女人三镑。他给了她三镑，又付给贝蒂大妈两镑，那是欠她的膳宿钱，可他一个子儿也没给我。我啊，这六个月一直养着他，自己连一口吃的都没有。可我要见人就说。我要说出去。」

她狂乱地朝四下里张望。她看见了马尔霍兰德。她走到他跟前，弯下腰凑近他的脸。她的帽子歪挂了下来。头发披散到眼睛上。她身子摇晃着。她伸出右手食指，指着马尔霍兰德的额头，一脸威胁。

===Original===
“Listen to me, Bartly,” she said. “You remember me when I was a good girl an’ when I was a member o’﻿ ﻿… ye know yersel’﻿ ﻿… Well, so was he, wasn’t he? Well, can ye tell me how did Frankie McPhillip get plugged? Who got the twenty quid that the Farmers’ Union gave out? Where did he get the money? I’m not shoutin’ any names. No names, no pack drills. But ye can guess for yersel’. Where did he get his money from? Was it be robbin’ a sailor at the back o’ Cassidy’s same as he told me in the pub? Was it?” She suddenly threw her hands over her head and clawed the air, shrieking. They jumped up and caught her.

Mulholland got to his feet quietly. He stole out into the street, avoiding the people who came rushing up to Biddy Burke’s door, attracted by the screaming.

Mulholland chuckled as he crossed the street. He would have plenty of news for Gallagher. After this there would be little difficulty in his getting McPhillip’s job on the Headquarters Staff. He stole quietly into the hallway of Aunt Betty’s house. He went noiselessly up the stairs without attracting the attention of the revellers who were still “on the tear.” He reached the landing. There were three doors, with light streaming through each of them. He listened at each door. The third was the right one. He stood straight. He lifted the latch suddenly and strode into the room. He called out as he did so dramatically:

“Come on, Gypo, it’s time for ye to be comin’ with me.”
===Chinese===
「你听我说，巴特利，」她说。「你还记得我从前是个正经姑娘的时候，还是……的一员，你心里清楚……对吧，他从前也是，是不是？那好，你能不能告诉我，弗兰基·麦克菲利普是怎么挨的枪？农夫工会放出的那二十镑叫谁拿了？他那钱是哪儿来的？我不指名道姓。不点名，就没有罪罚。可你自己能猜得出来。他的钱是打哪儿来的？是不是像他在酒馆里跟我说的那样，在卡西迪酒馆后头抢了个水手弄来的？是不是？」她突然把双手举过头顶，抓着空气，尖声叫喊起来。众人跳起来拉住了她。

马尔霍兰德悄悄站起身。他溜到街上，避开了那些被尖叫声引得朝碧蒂·伯克家门口奔来的人。

马尔霍兰德穿过街去，心里暗笑。他可以给加拉格尔带去一大堆消息了。这以后，他要顶上麦克菲利普在总部参谋部的那份差事，就不会有什么难处了。他悄悄溜进贝蒂大妈家的过道。他悄没声儿地上了楼梯，没惊动那些还在「纵酒作乐」的狂欢客。他到了楼梯口。那里有三扇门，每扇门里都漏出灯光。他在每扇门外听了听。第三扇是那扇对的。他站直身子。他猛地挑起门闩，大步走进房间。他一边进去一边拿腔作势地喊：

「走吧，吉波，你该跟我走了。」

===Original===
For a moment he could see nobody, owing to his excitement and the thick mist of smoke and unescaped vapours which filled the room. He stood within the door with his feet spread out wide on the bare moth-eaten boards of the floor, with his right hand in his pocket fingering his revolver. His heart was beating wildly. Then he became aware of Gypo’s presence. He felt that peculiar movement in his head that the realization of Gypo’s presence always caused, a little snapping movement of unreasoning terror. Then he heard Gypo’s voice, heavy and hoarse with drunkenness, but cordial and friendly and distinctly patronizing.

“Hello, Bartly. Sit down an’ have a drink. Plenty time yet.”

Then he turned his head towards the fireplace and saw Gypo.

Gypo was sitting on the floor to the right of the fire, in a corner, in half-darkness, bare to the waist, with his trousered legs stretched out at a wide angle, sitting bolt upright, a bottle gripped in his right hand between his knees, his feet bare.

Connemara Maggie was standing by the fire drying Gypo’s shirt, his jacket and his socks. The big boots were resting on a fender before the fire, steaming. She took no notice of Mulholland’s entrance. With her golden hair hanging in disorder over her face, with her blouse undone, with her strong, heavy-boned face covered with perspiration, with her great, soft eyes swollen and gentle like the eyes of a heifer, she busied herself tending her man, just as if she had never left the purity of her Connemara hills and she were tending her peasant spouse after a hard day’s work in the fields; instead of tending a casual lover in the sordid environment of a brothel. There was no hint of vice or of libidinous pleasure in her face or in her movements. She seemed to be, like Gypo himself, a child of the earth, unconscious of the artificial sins that are the handiwork of the city. In her two brawny arms she held the steaming shirt to the blaze. She stood silent and immovable.
===Chinese===
一时间他谁也没看见，一来因为他激动，二来满屋弥漫着浓重的烟雾和散不出去的水汽。他站在门内，两脚在虫蛀过的光地板上大大岔开，右手插在口袋里，摩挲着他的左轮枪。他的心狂跳着。随后他觉出了吉波在场。他脑子里便感到那种特有的动静——每次意识到吉波在场总会引起的那一下：一小阵毫无来由的恐怖，像什么东西咔地一爆。随后他听见吉波的声音，带着醉意，又重又哑，却是热络的、和气的，还明显带着居高临下的味儿。

「哈罗，巴特利。坐下喝一杯。时候还早得很。」

随后他把头转向壁炉，看见了吉波。

吉波坐在炉火右侧的地板上，在一个角落里，半明半暗中，光着上身，穿着裤子的两条腿大角度叉开，腰板笔直地坐着，右手夹在两膝中间攥着一只酒瓶，光着一双脚。

康尼马拉的玛吉站在炉火旁，烘着吉波的衬衫、外套和袜子。那双大靴子搁在炉前的挡灰栏上，冒着热气。马尔霍兰德进来，她理也不理。她金色的头发乱蓬蓬地披在脸上，罩衫敞着，那张骨骼粗大的结实的脸上淌着汗，一双又大又软的眼睛微微肿着，温顺得像小母牛的眼睛。她忙着伺候她的男人，就仿佛从来没有离开过康尼马拉群山的纯净，正在伺候她的庄稼汉丈夫干完一天重活之后；而不是在妓院污浊的窝里伺候一个露水情郎。她的脸上、她的动作里没有一丝邪祟或淫乐的影子。她跟吉波一样，似乎是大地的孩子，全然不懂得城市这双手造出来的种种人为的罪孽。她两条粗壮的胳膊把那件冒着热气的衬衫举向火焰。她站着，不出声，一动不动。

===Original===
There was little else in the small, whitewashed, low-ceilinged room. A bed with the clothes tousled on it, a quilt that lay on the floor by the bed, a chair on three legs and a weatherbeaten washstand, containing a basin and a broken jug, comprised the furniture.

Mulholland looked around at all this before he spoke. It was as well to get the correct details in case identification were necessary. Gypo might deny it. Then he spoke. He had recovered his nerve.

“No,” he said. “I don’t want a drink. It’s time for ye to be comin’.”

“Be off with ye, ye little divil,” yelled Gypo, suddenly jumping to his feet, with a great scraping and slapping noise. “Who are ye givin’ orders to?”
===Chinese===
这间刷得雪白、天花板低矮的小屋里，此外就没有多少东西了。一张床，被褥乱堆在上头；一床被子，撂在床边的地板上；一把三条腿的椅子；一个破旧不堪的洗脸架，架上一只脸盆、一把破水壶——这就是全部家当。

开口之前，马尔霍兰德把这一切扫了一圈。把细节看准总归不亏，万一日后需要指认呢。吉波兴许会抵赖。随后他开了口。他已经恢复了镇定。

「不，」他说。「我不喝酒。你该走了。」

「滚你的，你这个小鬼头，」吉波吼道，猛地跳起身来，带起一阵靴底刮地、手掌拍打的巨响。「你在跟谁下命令？」

===Original===
He took a pace forward and reached out his right hand, but Mulholland had drawn his revolver and taken a pace to his rear. At the same time he called out in a hissing whisper:

“It’s not me orders. It’s the Commandant’s orders, an’ ye better be careful about disobeyin’ them.”

Immediately Gypo drew himself up and let his hands drop to his sides. His face, which had lit up with anger, dropped into that peculiar wondering expression, which he wore when he was musing on the river bank before he went into the police-station. He looked at Mulholland in amazement. His forehead wrinkled. His nostrils expanded and contracted. His thick lips moved backwards and forwards, up and down. His face and his cropped skull shone in the light of the paraffin lamp that rested on the mantelpiece over the fire. The light also shone across his body, over a bulging bare shoulder that stood out white and massive and round below his brown neck. The shoulder muscles were immense. His body was white and hairless. His skin was perfectly smooth. But everywhere the muscles strained against the skin, in irregular, moving mounds. They swelled out on his breasts, at his biceps, above his hips, on his shoulders, just as if his head and neck were a massive tree growth and the body muscles were its roots, sunk into the body promiscuously and afar, during centuries of life.

He looked at Mulholland for some seconds. Then he turned to Maggie.

“Gimme me clothes, Maggie,” he said quietly.
===Chinese===
他上前一步，右手探了出去，可马尔霍兰德已经拔出左轮枪，朝后退了一步。同时他嘶着嗓子低声喊：

「不是我的命令。是加拉格尔指挥官的命令，你违抗它可要小心着点。」

吉波立刻挺直了身子，双手垂到两侧。他那张因愤怒而亮起来的脸，塌成了一种古怪的、怔忡出神的神情——他进警察局之前在河岸上出神时，带的就是那副神情。他惊讶地望着马尔霍兰德。他的额头皱起来。他的鼻孔一张一缩。他的厚嘴唇前后蠕动，上下咂动。他的脸和他那剃剪得短短的脑袋，在炉台上煤油灯的光里闪闪发亮。那光也照过他的身子，照在一个裸露的、鼓鼓凸起的肩膀上，那肩膀在棕色脖颈下面显得又白、又大、又圆。肩膀上的肌肉大得惊人。他的身子又白又无毛。他的皮肤光滑极了。可处处都有肌肉绷紧着皮肤，鼓成一块块不规则的、蠕动的肉丘。它们在胸口隆起，在肱二头肌隆起，在胯上隆起，在肩头隆起，就仿佛他的头颅和脖颈是一株巨树的主干，周身的肌肉是它的根须，在漫长世纪的生长里，杂乱而深远地扎进了这具身体。

他望了马尔霍兰德几秒。随后他转向玛吉。

「把衣裳给我，玛吉，」他平静地说。

===Original===
She handed them to him in silence. He dressed. He put on his little tattered round hat. Then he put his hand in his trousers pocket. He took out all the money he had left. Two pounds four and sixpence. He put the four and sixpence back into his pocket. He handed the two pound notes to Maggie.

“Keep one an’ give the other to Katie Fox,” he said. “Ye’ll find her down at Biddy Burke’s.”

She nodded and put the notes within her blouse.

“So long, Maggie. See you again,” he said, going to the door.

“So long,” she called after him quietly.

Gypo stalked out unsteady, followed by Mulholland.

After a little while Connemara Maggie also left the room. She went down to Biddy Burke’s.
===Chinese===
她默默地把衣裳递给他。他穿好。他戴上他那顶破烂的小圆帽。随后他把手伸进裤袋。他掏出他剩下的全部钱财。两镑四先令六便士。他把那四先令六便士放回口袋。他把两张一镑的钞票递给玛吉。

「留一张，另一张给凯蒂·福克斯，」他说。「她在碧蒂·伯克家，你到那儿就能找着她。」

她点点头，把钞票塞进罩衫里面。

「回见，玛吉。后会有期，」他说着朝门口走去。

「回见，」她在背后静静地应了一声。

吉波踩着不稳的步子大步走了出去，马尔霍兰德跟在后面。

过了一小会儿，康尼马拉的玛吉也离开了房间。她下到碧蒂·伯克家去了。

===Original===
Biddy Burke’s was now thronged with people. They were mostly women of the district and their men. They had been talking at a terrific rate when Maggie came in, but a strange silence fell upon them when she appeared. She did not take any notice of them. Going up to Katie Fox, who sat by the hearth, on the seat occupied recently by Mulholland, she took out the pound note and offered it to her.

“Gypo Nolan gave me this for ye,” she said quietly.

Katie Fox looked at the note. Then she looked at Maggie. Her under lip was quivering. Her eyes opened and narrowed spasmodically. She was moved by some complex emotion that she could not master for the moment. She did not speak. Others began to whisper. Some spoke out loud and sharply:

“Don’t take it, Katie. It’s blood money,” said one.

“Take it,” said Biddy Burke indignantly. “A pound note doesn’t smell when it’s changed.”
===Chinese===
碧蒂·伯克家这时挤满了人。多半是这一带的女人和她们的男人。玛吉进门时他们正急风骤雨地谈得起劲，可她一露面，一种古怪的沉默罩住了他们。她没理会他们。她走到坐在炉边的凯蒂·福克斯跟前——凯蒂坐的正是方才马尔霍兰德坐过的那张凳子——掏出那张一镑的钞票，递给她。

「吉波·诺兰让我把这个给你，」她平静地说。

凯蒂·福克斯看看钞票。又看看玛吉。她的下嘴唇发着抖。她的眼睛一阵阵睁大又眯起。她被某种一时克制不住的复杂情绪撼动着。她没有说话。旁人开始交头接耳。有人高声、尖利地说：

「别拿，凯蒂。那是血钱，」一个人说。

「拿着，」碧蒂·伯克愤愤地说。「一镑的票子，一找开就不带味儿了。」

===Original===
“Money is the common whore of all humanity,” stuttered a tall, lean, drunken gentleman, who dozed by the window with his head dangling.

“I bet she got more than that to give ye,” said another woman.

“Yes, I bet she has,” cried Katie Fox, suddenly settling the matter that was agitating her mind, whatever it was. “I know her. Out with it, Connemara Maggie,” she screamed, jumping to her feet and squaring herself. “Out with it an’ don’t stand there tryin’ to melt butter in me mouth with yer soft looks. How much did he give ye for me? Don’t tell me he only gave me one quid. Yer a liar before ye open yer mouth to say so. Ye﻿—”

“Well of all the stories﻿—” cried Connemara Maggie in amazement.
===Chinese===
「金钱是全人类共有的婊子，」一个瘦高个的醉绅士结结巴巴地说，他耷拉着脑袋在窗边打盹。

「我敢说她留下的比这多，」另一个女人说。

「对，我敢说她就是留下了，」凯蒂·福克斯喊道，心里那桩搅扰她的事——不管究竟是什么——忽然有了定夺。「我还不知道她。交出来，康尼马拉的玛吉，」她尖叫着，跳起身，叉开腿站稳摆开架势。「交出来，别站在那儿装出一副温柔相，想往我嘴里化黄油。他给了你多少钱，是给我的？别跟我说他只给了我一张镑票。你还没张嘴就已经在撒谎了。你——」

「嗬，这种闻所未闻的事——」康尼马拉的玛吉惊愕地叫道。

===Original===
“Don’t put on airs, Maggie,” said a woman beside her. “Don’t put on airs.”

“Out with the rest o’ the money,” cried Katie Fox.

“Yer a pack o’ dogs,” cried Connemara Maggie furiously. “Yer a pack o’﻿—”
===Chinese===
「别装模作样了，玛吉，」她身旁一个女人说。「别装了。」

「把其余的钱交出来，」凯蒂·福克斯喊道。

「你们是一群狗，」康尼马拉的玛吉狂怒地喊。「你们是一群——」

===Original===
She gasped and could say no more, astounded and hurt bitterly by the slanderous attack from Katie Fox, to whom she had never spoken in her life before, except to say good morning. She fumbled at her blouse and took out the other pound note that Gypo had given her for herself. Then she took a purse from a hiding-place on her left thigh. She abstracted another note from that. She put back the purse again. She threw the three notes at Katie Fox.

“There ye” she hissed. “That’s all his money. Take it. Maybe it’s dirty like yersel’. I am well rid of ye. If he’s yer man, keep him.”

She spat and strode out of the room, swinging her arms and knocking out of her way all who came in front of her.

Some stared after her and swore. Others looked at Katie Fox. Katie had the three pound notes in her hands and her lips were moving. Then Biddy Burke whispered something to her. Immediately Katie sighed and clutched the three notes in her hand, desperately, staring at the floor. Then she held them out to Biddy Burke rapidly, without looking in their direction. They lay crumpled in a ball on her quivering thin palm.

“Take them, Biddy,” she whispered. Then she suddenly raised her voice to an hysterical shriek. “Take them, but for God’s sake hurry and give me something at once. Quick, quick. Give it to me, Biddy. Give it to me.”
===Chinese===
她倒抽一口气，再说不出话来，被凯蒂·福克斯这场恶语中伤惊得目瞪口呆、伤透了心——她平生除了道一声早安，从来没跟凯蒂·福克斯说过一句别的话。她在罩衫上摸索着，掏出吉波给她自己的那张一镑钞票。随后她从左腿上的一处藏钱的地方摸出一只钱袋。她从里面又抽出一张钞票。她把钱袋塞了回去。她把三张钞票朝凯蒂·福克斯摔了过去。

「拿去，」她嘶声说。「他全部的钱都在这儿了。拿去吧。兴许跟你自己一样脏。跟你们这号人掰了，我倒落个干净。他既然是你的人，你就留着他吧。」

她啐了一口，挥着两条胳膊大步走出屋去，凡是挡在她跟前的，一律被她扒拉开。

有人盯着她的背影咒骂。旁人望着凯蒂·福克斯。凯蒂手里攥着三张一镑的钞票，嘴唇动着。随后碧蒂·伯克凑到她耳边说了句什么。凯蒂立刻叹了口气，死死地把三张钞票攥在手心里，眼睛盯着地板。随后她把手很快地朝碧蒂·伯克伸过去，看也不朝那个方向看一眼。三张钞票揉成一团，躺在她那发抖的瘦掌心里。

「拿着吧，碧蒂，」她低声说。随后她忽然拔高嗓门，变成歇斯底里的尖叫。「拿着，可看在上帝份上，快点，马上给我弄点来。快，快。给我，碧蒂。给我。」

## XI / 十一

===Original===
In the Bogey Hole rats scurried about, careless of the sentry who tramped up and down, from end to end of the long stone passage, with his rubber-heeled boots sounding loudly in the cavernous silence. Drops of water gathered slowly on the stone ceilings and then fell with soft, empty splashes to the stone floors. Except for the scurrying of the rats, the falling of the water and the footfalls of the sentry, there was silence.

The Bogey Hole, in which the Revolutionary Organization were about to hold their inquiry into the cause of the death of Francis Joseph McPhillip, had once been the wine cellars of a nobleman. Above it the ruins of the house still remained. But everybody had long since forgotten the name of the owner in the district. The hallway of the house was choked with rubbish. The two top stories had fallen in. Only a few rooms remained in a crumbling state. Children played in them and parties of men played cards for money there on Sundays. That’s all. But the wine cellars underneath were often used by the Revolutionary Organization as a meeting-place and for other purposes.
===Chinese===
博吉洞里，老鼠窜来窜去，全不理会那个来回踱步的哨兵。哨兵在长长的石头过道里从这头走到那头，胶底靴的脚步声在洞窟般的寂静里响得很亮。水珠在石头顶棚上慢慢聚拢，随后软软地、空空地一声轻响，溅落在石地上。除了老鼠的窜动、水的滴落和哨兵的脚步，便是一片寂静。

革命组织即将在这博吉洞里开庭，调查弗朗西斯·约瑟夫·麦克菲利普的死因。这里从前是一位贵族的酒窖。酒窖上面，宅子的残骸还在。可这一带的人早把主人的姓名忘了个干净。宅子的门厅塞满了垃圾。顶上两层早已坍塌。只剩下几个房间摇摇欲坠地立着。孩子们在里面玩耍，星期天有成群的男人聚在那里赌钱打牌。仅此而已。可底下的酒窖，革命组织常拿它当会场，也派别的用场。

===Original===
A wide stone stairway led down into the cellars from the rear of the hallway. There was a wide passage running straight through the cellars and rooms opened off the passage on either side. In the first room to the left of the stairway six men stood about. They were the guard, seven men including the man who was on sentry. They stood about the room, or sat on the floor by the wall, with their revolvers strapped outside their raincoats. A lighted lantern was placed on the floor in the centre of the room. The faces that were touched by the lantern light were haggard and pale. Farther down on the same side of the passage, a larger room was prepared for the inquiry. A small table had been placed in it. A horse blanket covered the table. There were several small forms there and a little “bedside table” to the right of the main table, with a deck chair behind it. A big lamp, turned on full, hung from the ceiling. It lit up the room so that the dampness on the walls glittered. Two tall, lean men stood by the entrance to the room, one on either side of it.

Across the passage, still farther away from the stairway, the Rat Mulligan was sitting on a form in another room. His three guards sat opposite him on a form. They had their revolvers in their hands.

All along the passage the light of the big lamp penetrated. It reached up three of the steps of the stairway. Beyond that and about the roof of the passage, there was pitch darkness.
===Chinese===
一道宽阔的石梯从门厅后部通下酒窖。一条宽宽的过道笔直穿过酒窖，过道两旁开出一间间屋子。石梯左手第一间屋里站着六个人。他们是卫队，连放哨的那个一共七个。他们在屋里四处站着，或靠着墙坐在地上，左轮枪拴在雨衣外面。一只点亮的提灯搁在屋子中央的地上。被灯光触及的那些脸，一张张憔悴苍白。同一侧再往里去，一间大些的屋子已经布置成法庭。屋里摆了一张小桌，桌上蒙着一条马毯。另有几张小长凳，大桌右手边还有一张小小的「床头桌」，桌后放着一把帆布躺椅。一盏大灯从天花板吊下，开足了亮，照得满屋通明，连墙上的潮气都闪闪发亮。两个瘦高个子的男人守在门口，一边一个。

过道对面，离石梯更远处，另一间屋里，「耗子」穆利根坐在一张长凳上。三个看守他的人在他对面的长凳上坐下，手里都握着左轮枪。

大灯的光沿着过道一路渗透过去，一直照到石梯的第三级台阶。再往上，还有过道的顶棚一带，就是漆黑一片了。

===Original===
