「什么？」他倒抽一口气，直瞪着她。
「你们打算怎么处置他，丹？」她问，声音低得几乎听不见。「我希望你们不要……」她顿住了。

===Original===
Gallagher looked at her sharply, in wonder, suspiciously, as if he had suddenly proved to himself that all his calculations had been wrong about something.
“Surely what, Mary?” he said at length, almost timorously.
“You’re not going to kill him,” she said. “That would only be another murder, added to﻿ ﻿… to the other. It wouldn’t help the dead. Lord have mercy on him.”
“Murder!” ejaculated Gallagher dreamily, as if he had heard the word for the first time in his life and he were reflecting on its significance, incredulously like a philosopher confronted unexpectedly by a stupendous superstition. Then his nostrils expanded and his face hardened into anger, as he realized her meaning and her attitude towards the sentence that was about to be passed on Gypo. “Murder, did you say? Great Scott! Do you call it murder to wipe out a serpent that has betrayed your brother? Where is your﻿ ﻿… ? Do you call yourself an Irishwoman? What? Good Lord! I don’t know what to make of it. What﻿ ﻿… ? Good Heavens!”

===Chinese===
加拉格尔锐利地看了她一眼，又是惊异又是猜疑，仿佛突然之间向自己证明了：他所有的盘算都在某一点上出了错。
「不要什么，玛丽？」他终于开口，语气近乎畏怯。
「你们不是要杀了他吧，」她说。「那无非是又一桩谋杀，加在……加在那件案子上面。这对死者没有半点好处。愿主怜悯他。」
「谋杀！」加拉格尔梦呓似的脱口喊了出来，仿佛这字眼他生平头一回听见，正在捉摸它的含义，将信将疑，活像一位哲学家冷不丁迎面撞上一桩天大的迷信。随即他咂摸出她的意思、咂摸出她对那就要落到吉波头上的判决所抱的态度，他的鼻孔张大了，脸也板出一副怒容。「谋杀，你是这么说的？老天爷！除掉一条出卖你兄弟的毒蛇，你也管这叫谋杀？你的……哪儿去了？你还自称爱尔兰女人？啊？我的天！我真闹不明白这是怎么一回事。这……？苍天哪！」

===Original===
“Listen to me, Dan,” she said, sobbing; “for God’s sake, listen to me before you do this. Listen. I didn’t know until now how awful it is. I was foolish the way I talked at home this evening when all the people were there. I was so mad the way father was talking that I thought I could shoot the man that informed on Frankie myself. But it would be murder, Dan, just the same as any other murder. And﻿—”
“Oh, hang it!” snapped Gallagher.
“Dan,” she whispered, “don’t do it, for my sake. I love you. Don’t do it, for my sake and I’ll do anything you want me. I feel I’m the cause of this.”
“Mary, do you love me?” whispered Gallagher excitedly, panting as he seized her right hand in both of his. He bent towards her. “Say it again. Say you love me.”

===Chinese===
「你听我说，丹，」她抽泣着说；「看在上帝份上，动手之前先听我说。你听着。我直到这会儿才知道这事有多可怕。今天晚上家里满屋子人的时候，我那样说话，真是糊涂。父亲讲话的那个样子让我气疯了，我寻思，告发弗兰基的那个人，哪怕叫我自己动手崩了他也使得。可那就是谋杀，丹，跟别的任何谋杀一模一样。还有——」
「哦，见鬼！」加拉格尔厉声说。
「丹，」她低声说，「别这么干，看在我的份上。我爱你。别这么干，看在我的份上，你要我做什么我都肯。我觉得，这一切都是我招来的。」
「玛丽，你爱我么？」加拉格尔激动地低声问，一面喘着气，一面用双手抓住她的右手。他朝她俯下身去。「再说一遍。说你爱我。」

===Original===
But he drew back immediately, with a strange and unnatural presence of mind. He was afraid that the passing sentry might see him.
Tears were rolling down Mary’s cheeks. She looked away towards the doorway. She kept silent. Gallagher leaned back from her, watching her face intently. He looked at her from under his bunched eyebrows. His lips were set firmly. His forehead convulsed. He appeared to be struggling with a savage passion and at the same time struggling to think coherently on the intellectual plane. He was trying to probe the movements of her mind so that he might conquer it with his mind. He wanted to conquer her mind and make her subject to him, to make her his mate on his own terms. He told himself that he was doing this, so that she might help him for the conquest of power. He refused to admit to himself that he was inspired by passion. He despised passion.
The silence was very peculiar and tense. Mary was conscious of it. But Gallagher was not conscious of it. Then Mary spoke. She talked rapidly without looking at him. She talked in an irritated tone.
“Take me out of this place immediately, Dan,” she said. “I was mad to come here with you. I had no business to come here atall. Also, if you were a gentleman you wouldn’t ask me to come. What I said just now about loving you was not true. I only said it trying to persuade you not to murder that man. Before, when I used to read in the papers about a man being shot, I used to think it was right, but it’s a different thing when a man you know does a thing like that. Frankie killed a man too, Lord have mercy on him. Oh, God, have pity on us all.” She became slightly hysterical. “Why can’t we have peace? Why must we be killing one another? Why﻿—”

===Chinese===
可他立刻就缩了回去，带着一种异样而不自然的镇定。他怕来回走动的哨兵瞧见他。
泪水顺着玛丽的双颊滚滚而下。她把目光挪开，望向门口。她始终沉默着。加拉格尔从她身子往后仰，目不转睛地端详她的脸。他从紧蹙的眉毛底下打量她。他的嘴唇抿得紧紧的。他的额头一阵阵抽搐。他像是在同一股狂野的激情搏斗，同时又竭力想在理智的层面上把思路理顺。他试图探明她心思的动向，好用他的头脑去把它征服。他要征服她的心，叫她臣服于他，照他自己开出的条件做他的伴侣。他对自己说，他这样做，是为了让她日后助他夺取权力。他不肯向自己承认，驱着他的其实是激情。他鄙视激情。
这一阵沉默十分古怪，十分紧张。玛丽觉察到了。加拉格尔却没有觉察。随后玛丽开了口。她说得飞快，眼睛不看他。她的口气带着恼意。
「马上带我离开这个地方，丹，」她说。「我跟你到这儿来，真是疯了。我压根儿就不该到这儿来。再说，你要是个体面人，也不会叫我上这种地方来。我方才说爱你，那不是真话。我那么说，不过是想劝你别去杀那个人。从前我在报上读到什么人被枪毙，总以为那是正当的事，可事情落到一个你认识的人头上，就完全是另一回事了。弗兰基也杀过人，愿主怜悯他。哦，上帝，垂怜我们大家吧。」她微微有些歇斯底里起来。「我们为什么就不能过太平日子？为什么非得自相残杀？为什么——」

===Original===
“Hush! Keep quiet. Keep quiet.”
“Isn’t it cruel, Dan?”
She let her head fall on her hands. Her body shook with silent sobs.
Gallagher stared at her dreamily.

===Chinese===
「嘘！别作声。别作声。」
「这难道不残忍么，丹？」
她把头垂落在双手上。她的身子随着无声的抽泣发着抖。
加拉格尔恍恍惚惚地盯着她。

===Original===
“I will let her alone now,” he thought. “The logical sequence of this outburst will be this. Her mind will wheel around to the other extreme if I keep quiet and don’t irritate her by attempting to convince her that I am right. Her terror and her moral excitement will exhaust themselves and go to sleep. Then she will become aware of her strange surroundings, mentally, in a different way. When her mind becomes awake and normally acute again, she will see me, this place and what’s going to be done with Gypo, in an opposite light. When her mind is groping about in this new attitude it will be easy for me to influence her. I think I’m right. At least it always held good, that rule. I remember the struggle I had with Sean Conroy. But women are supposed to be different from men a lot psychologically. But I have to chance that. It would be suicidal to interfere with her now. That’s certain. Still﻿ ﻿… I’m not sure of myself with her somehow.﻿ ﻿… It’s not like the others. And﻿ ﻿…”
Again his passion surged upwards. He sat without thought, fighting it, squeezing his palms together, with his eyes on her bent neck.

===Chinese===
「这会儿先由她去，」他想。「这场发作顺理成章的后续是这样：只要我闭紧嘴，不去想法子叫她相信我是对的、因而惹恼她，她的念头自会转到另一个极端上去。她的恐惧和她那阵道义上的激奋会自行耗尽，沉沉睡去。那时她就会换一种心境，意识到自己身处的是个古怪的地方。等她的头脑重新醒来、恢复平日的敏锐，她再看我、看这个地方、看要对吉波做的事，眼光就会倒转过来。等她的心思在这新的态度里四下摸索的时候，我再施影响就容易了。我想我是对的。至少这条规律向来灵验。我还记得当年跟肖恩·康罗伊（Sean Conroy）较量的那番周折。可女人的心理据说跟男人大有分别。这一层我也只好赌一赌了。这会儿去干涉她，无异于自寻死路。这是肯定的。不过……不知怎么，跟她在一起，我自己心里也没有准谱。……这跟别的不一样。还有……」
他的激情又向上翻涌起来。他坐着，脑中一片空白，只顾同它搏斗，两个手掌紧紧挤在一起，眼睛盯着她低垂的脖颈。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===
When Gallagher left the inquiry room, Mulholland went silently to a form and sat down. The three men stood nervously in front of the table watching him. They watched him intently, in silence, as if each movement he made was fraught with grave consequences to themselves.

He took three matches from a box and placed them beside him on the form. He handled them slowly and deliberately, with a serious contemplative expression on his face, like an old fisherman baiting his hooks under the admiring glances of a party of tourists. Then he took out a clasp knife and opened it. He cut a piece off one match. He put the knife back into his pocket.

Then, suddenly, he cleared his throat with a noise that sounded enormous in the silence. The three men started. They looked at one another fearfully, as if each had been caught by the others in the commission of an indecency.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔离开审讯室后，马尔霍兰德一声不响地走到一条长凳前坐下。三个人紧张地站在桌前望着他。他们目不转睛地、默不作声地望着，仿佛他的每一个动作都关系到他们自身的重大安危。

他从一只盒子里取出三根火柴，放在身边的长凳上。他摆弄火柴的动作缓慢而从容，脸上带着一副郑重其事的沉思神情，活像一个老渔夫在一群游客艳羡的目光下往鱼钩上装饵。随后他掏出一把折刀，打开，从一根火柴上切下一小截。他把刀子收回口袋。

随后，突然，他清了清嗓子，那声音在寂静中听来响得吓人。三个人都吓了一跳。他们惶惶然你望我、我望你，仿佛各自都被别人撞破了正在干一桩见不得人的下流事。

===Original===
Mulholland rose calmly and approached them, holding the three matches on his open palm. Without speaking he pointed to them. Two long and one short. They all examined them. Right. Each nodded his head solemnly. Not a word. Mulholland nodded and marched away to the far end of the room. They did not follow him now with their eyes. They stared painfully at the floor.

The tallest of them was a docker called Peter Hackett. He was a fair-haired young giant, slim and lean-faced, with sleepy blue eyes and a gentle mouth. His great bony hands were thickly covered with long white hairs. He stood with his arms folded on his chest, one leg thrust forward, his eyes wide open and strained, his forehead wrinkled. He was only twenty-two. This was the first time he had been chosen for an affair of this sort. It was particularly strange and odious to him, because he was a good-natured soul, loved by all on the quays where he worked. He had no conception of politics or of any problem other than hurling, football, horse racing and pitch and toss, which he played all Sunday afternoon on the Canal bank with his cronies. He often lost his whole week’s wages playing pitch and toss. On these occasions, when he went home to his young wife penniless, he would first of all dance around the kitchen in a fit of rage and perhaps break a thing or two, threatening to blow Kitty’s brains out if she said a word. Then his anger would suddenly evaporate, to be followed by a fit of sobbing. During this fit he sat by the fire with his head in his hands, moaning and begging Kitty to forgive him. His wife always felt exalted when these outbursts occurred, because the excitement of the quarrel and Peter’s kisses, which lasted far into the night afterwards, were a welcome break in the dreary monotony of everyday life as a docker’s wife, scouring, cooking, washing, with two children to look after on a docker’s wages.
===Chinese===
马尔霍兰德平静地站起身，朝他们走去，摊开的手掌上托着那三根火柴。他不发一言，只朝火柴指了指。两长，一短。他们都仔细端详了一番。没错。每个人庄严地点了点头。一句话也没有。马尔霍兰德点点头，大步走到房间另一头去了。这会儿他们不再用眼睛跟着他。他们痛苦地盯着地板。

三人中个子最高的是个码头工人，名叫彼得·哈克特（Peter Hackett）。他是个金发的年轻巨汉，身量颀长，脸瘦，一双惺忪的蓝眼睛，一张温和的嘴。他那双大骨节的手上密密地覆着长长的白汗毛。他双臂抱在胸前站着，一条腿朝前探出，眼睛睁得大大的、发直，额头蹙着。他只有二十二岁。这是他头一回被挑中来办这种事。这事在他看来格外古怪、格外可憎，因为他是个好心肠的人，在他干活的码头上人缘极好。除了板棍球、足球、赛马和掷钱币，他对政治或任何别的问题一概没有概念——每逢星期天下午，他都和一班哥们儿在运河岸边玩这些。玩掷钱币，他常常输掉整整一个礼拜的工钱。每逢这种时候，他一文不名地回到年轻妻子跟前，先要暴跳如雷地在厨房里转着圈蹦跳，兴许还摔碎一两样东西，扬言基蒂要是敢吱一声，就崩了她的脑袋。随后他的火气会突然烟消云散，接踵而来的是一阵抽抽噎噎的痛哭。哭的那阵子，他坐在炉火边，两手抱着头，唉声叹气地求基蒂饶了他。每逢这种发作，他的妻子反倒觉得心花怒放，因为吵架的刺激，还有彼得随后直到深夜还不歇的亲吻，给码头工人老婆那种日子的沉闷单调——擦锅、做饭、洗衣，靠一份码头工钱拉扯着两个孩子——带来了可喜的调剂。

===Original===
Peter had no imagination. He lacked the refined conscience and sense of injustice that attracts most gentle natures like his towards a revolutionary movement. He was not the stuff of which the other sort of revolutionary is made either. He belonged to the Organization simply because the rest of “the boys” belonged to it, and out of fanatical hero-worship for Commandant Dan Gallagher.

Dart Flynn, on the other hand, was designed by nature as a revolutionary, a man to stalk ahead of the bulk of humanity, grimly destroying obstacles, disturbing the sluggish existence of the herd, terrifying the contented ones into activity, born with a curse upon his brow, anathema to the mass of beings who always seek tranquillity and peace at any price. He was dour, dark visaged, built like the base of an oak tree, almost square. His body and face were fleshy and jealous of movement. His eyes were small. They moved horizontally. He was clean shaven, with a pink and white complexion, in spite of the fact that he was thirty-five and lived a hard life as a carter. In company, he hardly ever expressed an opinion on politics, religion, or on any other of the fundamental things that are discussed with avidity by revolutionaries who carry their lives in their hands. But in the secrecy of his own soul he thought deeply on these matters. In his little bare room in a lodging-house in Capel Street, he had several works on philosophy and economics. He had also worked out an amazing system of philosophy, based on the premise that each human being shares his soul with several different animals. The man who could discover and have constant intercourse with these animals would be supremely happy and immortal.

Flynn had no moral sense. He hated all human beings who were not Communists. He loved all children and animals. He gave most of his wages to the hungry little ruffians in the street. He had no relatives or dependents. He was an old member of the Organization, highly respected for his courage, his fidelity and his taciturn habits.
===Chinese===
彼得没有想象力。他缺少那种精细的良心和对不义的敏感——正是这种东西，把大多数像他这般温和的天性引向革命运动。他也不是造就另一种革命者的材料。他属于组织，无非因为「弟兄们」里其余的人都属于组织，再就是对丹·加拉格尔指挥官狂热的英雄崇拜。

达特·弗林（Dart Flynn）则相反，天生就是当革命者的料，是那种要大步走在人类大众前头的人：冷酷地扫除障碍，搅扰群氓怠惰的生存，把安于现状的家伙吓得动弹起来；他生来额上便带着一道诅咒，是一切不惜任何代价只求安宁太平的芸芸众生的眼中钉。他性情阴郁，面色黝黑，身架长得像橡树的根部，敦敦实实，几乎四四方方。他的身子和脸都肉乎乎的，吝于动弹。他的眼睛很小，只作左右平移。他脸刮得精光，肤色白里透红，尽管他已经三十五岁，又过着赶大车的苦日子。在人堆里，他几乎从不就政治、宗教，或任何别的根本问题发表意见——那些提着脑袋干革命的革命者，最爱起劲谈论的正是这些根本问题。可是在自己灵魂的隐秘处，他对这些事情想得很深。在卡佩尔街（Capel Street）一家公寓里他那间光秃秃的小屋中，放着好几部哲学和经济学著作。他还钻研出了一套惊人的哲学体系，其前提是：每一个人的灵魂，都与几种不同的动物共同分有。谁要是能发现这些动物并同它们经常交往，谁就至福无疆，长生不死。

弗林没有道德感。他仇恨一切不是共产主义者的活人。他热爱一切孩子和动物。他把大半工钱都给了街上那些饿肚子的小野孩。他无亲无故，也没有要他养活的人。他是组织的老成员，凭他的勇敢、他的忠诚和他沉默寡言的习惯，深受众人敬重。

===Original===
The third man, Laurence Curley, was of a totally different type from both his companions. He was also the most nervous and timorous. He was twenty-eight, pale faced, red haired, with a tall, thin frame, slightly consumptive-looking, on account of his hollow chest and stooping shoulders. His father had been a doctor in a country dispensary district. He had received a good education, but he early grew dissatisfied with life and refused to study for the Bar as his father wished. Instead he took a job in Dublin as a clerk, in order that he might plunge into the revolutionary movement.

The theory of Revolutionary Communism interested him far more than working for a revolution. He gradually became a crank, hated by everybody. He was always finding fault and reading or discussing dull works on Socialism. His views were always the most extreme and bloodthirsty. He used to whisper excitedly, whenever he met a stranger who did not know him yet, or when the least industrial disturbance occurred:

“The red flag will be hoisted any minute. Wait till you see. Then blood will spill. Wait till you see. ‘Justice’ and ‘liberty’ are bourgeois watchwords. The proletarian watchwords are ‘revenge’ and ‘bread.’ The proletariat knows how to deal out their deserts to the oppressors.”

He had always this sort of patter.
===Chinese===
第三个人，劳伦斯·柯利（Laurence Curley），跟两个同伴又是全然不同的路数。他也是三人中最神经质、最胆怯的一个。他二十八岁，脸色苍白，红头发，高挑精瘦的身架，由于胸膛凹陷、双肩伛偻，微带痨病相。他的父亲原是乡下施药辖区的一名医生。他受过良好的教育，可他很早就对生活生出不满，不肯照父亲的心愿去攻读律师，反而跑到都柏林当了一名文书，为的是好一头扎进革命运动里去。

革命共产主义的理论，比起为革命实干，远更叫他着迷。他渐渐变成了一个怪人，人人都讨厌他。他总是挑刺儿，总是抱着社会主义的乏味著作读啊讨论啊。他的观点永远是最极端、最好杀的。每逢遇到一个还不认识他的生人，或是哪里起了最轻微的工潮，他总要激动地凑近咬耳朵：

「红旗随时都会升起来。你等着瞧吧。到那时血就要流了。你等着瞧吧。『正义』和『自由』是资产阶级的口号。无产阶级的口号是『复仇』和『面包』。无产阶级懂得怎样给压迫者应得的报应。」

他嘴里老是这一套顺口溜。

===Original===
Now, however, the three of them, so different in essential characteristics, had reached a common level of emotion. The silence of the night, the phantom-filled cellars, the illegality and danger of the contemplated act, the torturing uncertainty of the choice, filled them with such delirious emotions that they were beside themselves. They were not afraid. They were beyond fear, on to a distant level of emotion, where the common impulses, that agitate the hearts of men, are unknown.

Then Mulholland approached with the matches arranged in his hand, so that their red heads alone were visible.

“Who’ll draw first?” he said carelessly, standing in front of the group.

After a moment’s pause Flynn came forward hurriedly. He stretched out a fleshy hand, fumbled awkwardly with the matches and then pulled one.

They all strained eagerly to look. It was a long match. Everybody sighed.

“Next,” said Mulholland.

Curley and Hackett looked at one another excitedly. Then each spoke.
===Chinese===
然而此刻，这三个禀性迥异的人，却达到了同一种情绪境地。夜的沉寂，魅影幢幢的地窖，那桩谋划中的勾当的非法与凶险，抽签抉择那折磨人的悬而未决，使他们满腔亢奋、如痴如狂，人都失了常态。他们不是害怕。他们已经越过了恐惧，升上了一个遥远的情绪境界，在那里，那些搅动常人心弦的寻常冲动，是他们所不知道的。

随后马尔霍兰德走了过来，火柴在手里排布妥当，只露出几个红头。

「谁先抽？」他站在三人跟前，漫不经心地说。

略停了片刻，弗林急匆匆抢上前。他伸出一只肥厚的手，笨手笨脚地在火柴里摸索了一气，随即拔出一根。

众人都急切地伸头去看。是根长火柴。人人都叹了口气。

「下一个。」马尔霍兰德说。

柯利和哈克特激动地你望我、我望你。随后两人都开了腔。

===Original===
“You go first.”

“No, you go first.”

“Go ahead. I don’t mind drawing the last.”

“What’s the difference? You’re nearest. Draw.”

“Why should I? It’s your turn. You draw.”

“Come on,” snarled Mulholland, “one of you draw. We have no time.”

They both made a movement towards the matches. Then each stopped to let the other advance. Their hands and legs were jerky. They stared at one another with hatred.
===Chinese===
「你先来。」

「不，你先来。」

「抽吧。抽到最后一个我也不在乎。」

「有什么两样？你离得近。你抽。」

「凭什么我抽？该轮到你了。你抽。」

「快点，」马尔霍兰德咆哮道，「你们哪一个抽都行。我们没时间了。」

两人都朝火柴动了动。随即又各自停住，让对方先上。他们的手和腿都在抽动。他们满怀仇恨地你瞪着我、我瞪着你。

===Original===
“Come on,” hissed Mulholland again. “Didn’t ye hear the Commandant’s orders, that we were to get outa the place as soon as possible? Are ye afraid or what?”

“Oh no,” cried both men together in an offhand tone.

They both rushed at the matches. They tussled for them.

“Keep back now. It’s my turn.”

“Keep back, you. You weren’t so quick before. Let me draw.”

“No, I won’t. I was here first.”

“For goodness’ sake,” cried Mulholland, “ye pair o’ babies. Will I have to pull me gat on ye?”

The two of them stood still, looking at Mulholland dazedly.
===Chinese===
「快点，」马尔霍兰德又嘶声催促。「你们没听见指挥官的命令吗——叫我们尽快离开这地方？你们是害怕了还是怎么的？」

「哦，没有的事，」两人齐声叫道，一副满不在乎的口气。

两人一齐朝火柴扑过去。两人扭作一团抢起来。

「退后。这回轮到我了。」

「你才退后。方才你可没这么积极。让我抽。」

「不，我不。是我先到这儿的。」

「看在老天爷份上，」马尔霍兰德喊道，「你们这一对娃娃。还要我掏枪指着你们才成吗？」

两人站着不动，茫然地望着马尔霍兰德。

===Original===
“It’s against the rules,” continued Mulholland with a great sense of importance, “but I’m goin’ to call ye in the order o’ yer rank. You draw first, Comrade Curley.”

Curley’s thin fingers shot out instantly. He drew the match. It was a long one. He gasped. Then he burst into a thin laugh.

“Comrade Hackett.”

Hackett stumbled forward. He reached for the short match that Mulholland held out to him with a strange smile.

“It’s your shot, comrade,” whispered Mulholland.

Hackett grasped the match and crushed it into fragments immediately. He threw the little bundle away in terror. He rubbed his palms slowly. Then he struck his right coat pocket suddenly with his hand. He laughed.

“Good Lord!” he blubbered, “I thought I’d lost me penknife.”
===Chinese===
「这是不合规矩的，」马尔霍兰德接着说，一副事关重大的神气，「不过我要按你们军衔高低的次序来叫。你先抽，柯利同志。」

柯利瘦削的手指立刻弹了出去。他抽出一根火柴。是根长的。他倒抽一口气。随后迸出一阵尖细的笑。

「哈克特同志。」

哈克特踉跄着上前。他伸手去接那根短火柴——马尔霍兰德正带着一种古怪的微笑把它递给他。

「该你出手了，同志。」马尔霍兰德低声说。

哈克特一把攥住火柴，立刻捏得粉碎。他心惊胆战地把那一小把碎屑扔了出去。他慢慢搓着两只手心。随后他突然用手拍了拍上衣右边的口袋。他笑了。

「天哪！」他哭腔哭调地说，「我还当我的小折刀丢了呢。」

## XIV / 十四

===Original===
For ten minutes Gypo lay perfectly still in the cell, after the door was bolted. He lay on his back. His head and neck were buttressed into an upright position by a square block of stone that jutted from the floor, by the wall farthest from the door. His feet were stretched out, wide apart. One hand lay on his right hip, palm upwards, with the fingers bent inwards, as if he had fallen asleep clawing something. The other hand lay across his eyes. He drew very deep breaths at long intervals. His face was perfectly at peace. It was bruised slightly around the mouth and on the cheekbones. Each feature was impassive, like the features of a carved image. The glossy skin, the humps, the eyebrows that were like snouts, the thick Ethiopian lips, attained a majesty during that ten minutes of abnormal rest, a majesty that was not so apparent while they were in movement, responding to the strange impulses of his mind.

Gypo rested, exhausted, while he was being condemned to death. It was a dead rest, like the rest of a child in the womb before birth, sucking strength all round for the savage struggle with life that will soon commence. Every organ and tissue and muscle was straining for a renewal of strength.

When blundering reason flees, instinct, that is fundamental and unerring, rushes to the defence of life.

At twelve minutes past three, one minute after he had been condemned to death, Gypo moved. He opened his eyes and closed the right hand that lay palm upwards on the ground. He clenched the hand rigidly until the wrist joint snapped with the tension. Then he took the other hand away from his eyes and dropped it to his bosom. He moved his eyes around from side to side, slowly, suspiciously, blinking and listening intently.

===Chinese===
门闩落下之后，吉波在牢房里一动不动地整整躺了十分钟。他仰面躺着。头和脖子抵在一块从地里凸出来的方形石头上，被撑得直挺挺竖着，那块石头嵌在离门最远的那面墙脚下。他两腿伸直，叉得很开。一只手搭在右胯上，掌心朝上，手指朝里蜷着，仿佛抓着什么东西就那么睡着了。另一只手横搁在眼睛上。他隔上好一阵才深深吸一口气。他的脸一片安详。嘴角四周和颧骨上有些轻微的青肿。五官纹丝不动，像一尊雕像的眉目。那油亮的皮肤、那些肉疙瘩、那两道猪嘴似的眉毛、那两片厚厚的埃塞俄比亚人嘴唇，在这十分钟反常的歇息里显出一种威严——它们活动起来、应和他脑子里种种古怪冲动的时候，这威严倒不这么显明。

吉波精疲力竭地歇着，这时他正被判处死刑。那是一种死气沉沉的歇息，像胎儿降生之前在子宫里的歇息，从四面八方吮吸力量，以应付那很快就要开始的与生活的野蛮搏斗。每一个器官、每一块组织、每一条肌肉都在拼命蓄积新的力气。

当跌跌撞撞的理性逃走之时，本能——那根本的、从不出错的本能——便冲上来捍卫生命。

三点十二分，他被判死刑一分钟之后，吉波动了。他睁开眼睛，攥拢了那只掌心朝上搁在地上的右手。他把那只手攥得死紧，紧得手腕关节都绷得咔地一响。然后他把另一只手从眼睛上拿开，落到胸口。他缓缓地、满腹狐疑地左右转动眼珠，眨着眼，凝神谛听。

===Original===
The cell was pitch black. Only at one point was there a speck of light. There was a dim, oblong patch of light hanging slantwise in the darkness some distance to his left front. That was the aperture near the top of the door. It did not penetrate the darkness of the cell. It merely hung there, obscurely and uselessly, like a foolish suggestion. All round was pitch dark. Gypo shivered.

He was not afraid. No. He did not feel at all in the ordinary sense of the word. But he was immediately fully conscious, as soon as he moved, of all that had happened before he had been thrown into the cell. Still more peculiar, he was quite calm and collected about everything. The darkness consoled him. He felt at home in it. It concealed him. He felt immensely big and strong in the darkness. There was nothing in his immediate neighbourhood but a darksome void that his personality overpowered. He could bellow and his voice would resound through that darkness indefinitely. There would be no resistance. There was no limit to the darkness, no wall, no horizon, no end. He was encompassed by it, sheathed in it. It wound round and round him. It was an impenetrable coat of mail, without weight, without thickness, intangible.

Beyond it somewhere were his enemies. It came between him and them. Ha!

===Chinese===
牢房里漆黑一团。只有一处透着一星光亮。一抹昏暗的长方形光斑斜悬在黑暗中，在他左前方不远的地方。那是靠近门顶的通气孔。它照不破牢房的黑。它只是悬在那儿，昏昧而无用，像一句愚蠢的建议。四下里一片漆黑。吉波打了个寒噤。

他不害怕。不。他压根儿没有什么寻常意义上的感觉。可他一动，立刻就完全清醒地意识到自己被扔进牢房之前发生的一切。更古怪的是，他对桩桩件事都镇定自若。黑暗抚慰着他。他在黑暗里如鱼得水。它遮蔽着他。他在黑暗里觉得自己硕大无朋、力大无穷。他身周的近处空无一物，只有一片阴沉沉的虚空，被他的人格压伏着。他可以吼叫，他的声音会在那黑暗里无止无休地回荡。不会有什么阻拦。黑暗没有边际，没有墙，没有地平线，没有尽头。他被它包裹着，被它罩着。它一圈又一圈地缠绕他。那是一袭刀枪不入的铠甲，没有重量，没有厚度，无从触摸。

在它外面的什么地方，是他的仇敌。它横在他和他们中间。哈！

===Original===
He gathered himself up with a sudden spring. He got to his hands and knees. Several joints snapped as he did so. His bruised body had grown stiff, lying motionless on the stone floor. Just as he lay that way on his hands and knees, he heard a rattle at the door. Immediately he threw himself down again and pretended to be asleep. But he fell so that his eyes were toward the oblong patch of light. He knew what had rattled. It was the sentry having a look at him. An electric torch was thrust through the aperture. It rested on him for a moment or two. Then it was withdrawn.

During the couple of moments that the torchlight had flooded the cell Gypo’s eyes were busy. They had darted around. Yes. The walls were hopeless. He knew that of course. He had himself guarded a prisoner in this same cell, a condemned prisoner whom he and McPhillip and Jem Linnet, the bookmaker’s clerk, had afterwards brought out in a car. He knew the whole routine. Perhaps that knowledge was responsible for his calm, partly responsible. Nothing was uncertain in the near future. In a few minutes they would come for him. Once in the car it would be impossible to escape.

All right. His only chance was in the cell. Ha! That was why he was calm and collected. After all, it was neither the darkness nor his knowledge of what was destined to happen that made him calm. McPhillip had at last made a plan. The door… the door… the door!

“Gypo,” he had said one night in Cassidy’s when he was drunk, “if we ever get… ye know what I mean, Gyp… click… you know… ye needn’t worry. I can manage that cell easy. Only I’d need you. I’m too small. Listen.”

===Chinese===
他猛地一挺，撑起身子。他撑到了双手双膝。这么一动，好几个关节咔咔直响。他挨过打的身子在石板地上一动不动躺久了，早就僵了。他正那样双手双膝撑着，忽听得门口哗啦一响。他立刻扑倒在地，装作睡着。他倒下去的时候，眼睛正朝着那道长方形光斑。他知道是什么东西在响。是哨兵来察看他。一支电筒从通气孔里探了进来。光在他身上停了一两秒，随即缩了回去。

电筒光涌进牢房的那一两秒钟里，吉波的眼睛一刻没闲着。它们朝四下里扫了一圈。是的。这几面墙是指望不上的。这他当然知道。他自己就在这同一间牢房里看守过一个囚犯，一个死刑犯，后来他和麦克菲利普，还有赌注商的伙计杰姆·林内特（Jem Linnet），用一辆汽车把那人弄了出去。这一整套程序他全知道。也许正是这份了然使他镇定，至少有一部分是。最近的将来没有什么拿不准的事。几分钟之内他们就会来提他。一旦上了汽车，就休想逃了。

好吧。他唯一的机会就在这间牢房里。哈！这才是他镇定自若的缘故。说到底，使他镇定的既不是黑暗，也不是他对注定要发生的事的预知。麦克菲利普到底定下过一个计策。那扇门……那扇门……那扇门！

「吉波，」有一夜他在卡西迪酒馆里喝醉了的时候说，「要是咱们真落到……你懂我的意思，吉波……咔嗒一声……你知道……你就用不着发愁。那间牢房我好摆弄。只是得有你搭把手。我个子太小。你听着。」

===Original===
“I’ll do it, Frankie,” mumbled Gypo to himself excitedly, as he crawled along the floor towards the door.

He moved like a bear on his hands and knees, with his head down and his haunches high in the air. He moved noiselessly until he reached the door. He felt along the edge of the wall and then drew himself gradually to his feet. For a moment he toyed with the idea of taking off his boots, but he could not remember that Frankie had said anything about that. He decided to leave them on. He reached up with his hands. He strained them to their full length before he reached the top of the stone ledge over the door.

Drawing a deep breath he hoisted up his body, using his biceps as levers.… His biceps swelled and knotted and snapped.﻿… His body rose smartly and without apparent effort. In an amazing way he swung around his legs from the hips and landed his body gently on the ledge, resting on the right side of his chest and stomach. The stone ledge was no more than six inches wide. More than half his body rested on the empty air, as it lay along the ledge. But he was as cool as if he were standing loosely on the broad, firm earth. He was acting on the plan he and McPhillip had rehearsed. His body performed the movements without his mind exercising any control, either of guidance or of warning, warning against danger that is called fear.

===Chinese===
「我来干，弗兰基，」吉波兴奋地喃喃自语，一面在地板上朝门口爬去。

他像一头熊那样双手双膝着地爬行，头朝下，屁股撅得老高。他悄没声儿地爬，一直爬到门口。他顺着墙边一路摸过去，再慢慢直起身子。有一瞬间他动了脱靴子的念头，可他记不起弗兰基说过这个。他决定不脱。他抬起双手往上够。他把胳膊抻到十足的长，方才够着门上方那条石沿。

他深深吸进一口气，把身子往上拔，拿二头肌当杠杆。……他的二头肌隆起、拧成疙瘩、绷得啪啪作响。……他的身子利利索索地升了上去，看不出费一点力气。他以一种惊人的方式从胯部把双腿抡转过去，把身子轻轻放上石沿，右侧的胸腹贴着石沿躺定。那条石沿不过六英寸宽。他顺着石沿躺下时，大半个身子悬在空里。可他镇定得很，仿佛松松快快站在宽广坚实的大地上。他是在照他和麦克菲利普排演过的那套方案行动。他的身体自行完成这一连串动作，头脑全然不加控制，既不引导，也不警示，不警示那种名叫「恐惧」的危险。

===Original===
After a slight pause, he leaned his weight on his hands and rolled his body around in a reckless movement. His legs shot into the air about two feet. He stood poised on his hands for two seconds, as if he were going to stand on his head. Then he lowered his right leg. He brought it up to his hands. Slowly, with snapping gasps, he balanced himself on the right leg and stood up straight.

He stood straight in the solid darkness for a moment. He breathed twice rapidly. Then he groped upwards for the roof. He found it about three inches above his head. He pawed the stones hurriedly, searching. He couldn’t find what he wanted. It should be there. Mother of Mercy! He pawed out farther. Nothing yet. Sweat stood out on his forehead, suddenly, as if his body had been wrung. Savage anger gained control of him. He bared his lips and distended his eyes. His last hope gone? Had they taken it away during the last six months? He reached out one inch farther. Too far.

With a muffled gasp, he hurtled forward from the ledge. His hands scraped along the roof with a rasping sound. Then, just as they fell in pursuit of the falling body, the fingers of the right hand closed on an iron ring. They closed on it like a vice. The shoulder muscles snapped. Gypo swung across the floor, brought up with a grunt, jerked and swung back again, suspended from the iron ring by his right hand.

===Chinese===
略停了停，他把重量压上双手，不管不顾地把身子翻转过去。他的双腿腾空蹿起约两英尺。他双手拄地倒立了两秒钟，像要拿大顶。然后他放下右腿。他把右腿收到手边。他一口一口短促地喘着气，慢慢在右腿上找稳了平衡，直挺挺站了起来。

他在浓实的黑暗里笔直站了片刻。他急促地呼吸了两下。然后他向上摸索屋顶。屋顶离他头顶约三英寸。他慌忙扒拉着石头，搜寻着。他找不着要找的东西。它应该在那儿。仁慈的圣母啊！他往更远处扒拉。还是没有。汗珠子霎时从他额头上冒出来，仿佛他的身子刚被人拧过一遍。狂怒支配了他。他咧开嘴唇，瞪大眼睛。他最后的指望完了？这六个月里叫他们给拆了？他又往外够了一英寸。太远了。

他闷哼一声，从石沿上朝前猛扑出去。他的双手沿屋顶刮过去，发出锉磨般的声响。就在双手追着下坠的身子落空的一刹那，右手的指头扣住了一只铁环。它们像老虎钳似的死死钳住。肩上的肌肉啪地绷响。吉波横里荡过地板，咕哝一声撞停，一挣，又荡了回来，全凭右手吊在那只铁环上。

===Original===
When he steadied himself, he changed hands on the ring and groped about with his right hand, until he found a hole in the roof about three inches away from the ring. That was the hole of the trapdoor, through which the wine had been let down into the cellar from the garden. He gripped the ring with both hands and swung up with his legs, until they found the far side of the hole. He jammed both feet against the side of the hole and rested for four seconds, breathing deeply. His knees were bent upwards.

He reached up into the hole with his right foot. The foot reached the oaken door that lay across the mouth. It had been fastened with leather hinges, but these had worn away and they had not been renewed since the house became deserted. Several inches of earth had collected on it. Gypo pushed against it and made no impression on this mass of earth and rubbish that covered it. He took another rest and then pushed with all his might. He raised it suddenly, with a sucking sound, about three inches. A mass of dirt and earth fell down with a swish. It landed on the floor beneath with a showery thud. The noise terrified Gypo. The sentries outside the door would hear it.

In a furious rage, he kicked with all his might and sent the door flying away from the hole. A whole load of earth fell with a rush and a gust of cold night air came with it, with equal rapidity, ferociously, as if it had been waiting a long time to attack.

===Chinese===
等他稳住身子，他换了一只手抓环，用右手四下摸索，终于在离铁环约三英寸的屋顶上摸到一个洞。那是活板门的洞口，从前酒就是打这里从园子里吊下地窖去的。他双手攥紧铁环，双腿往上抡，直到双脚够着洞的另一边。他两脚死死抵住洞壁，歇了四秒钟，深深地呼吸。他的膝盖朝上弓着。

他把右脚伸进洞里往上探。脚够着了横盖在洞口上的一扇橡木门。它原先用皮合页固定，可皮子早磨烂了，这所房子废弃之后也没再换过新的。门上积了几英寸厚的土。吉波往上顶，压在门上的那一大堆土和破烂纹丝不动。他又歇了一回，随后拼出全身的力气往上顶。门猛地被顶起约莫三英寸，带出一声吮吸似的响动。一大团泥污唰地落了下来，噗的一声砸在下面的地板上，溅得像一阵泥雨。这声音吓坏了吉波。门外的哨兵们会听见的。

他暴怒起来，拼死命一脚踹去，把那扇门从洞口踹得飞了出去。整整一摊土轰然泻下，一股冰冷的夜气随着涌进来，一样地迅疾，凶猛无比，仿佛早已等候多时，就等着这一刻扑上来。

===Original===
In spite of the blinding dirt and the freezing air, Gypo stuck his legs through the hole immediately and clutched the garden surface with his heels. Then he let go one hand off the ring and gripped the side of the hole. He hurt his collarbone badly as he did so. Now his body was secure in the hole. He let go the other hand, supporting himself on the thigh muscles that gripped the sides of the hole until the second hand and his head came into the hole. Then he scrambled through on to the garden. He bounded to his feet and hurtled forward on his face.

Two shots had thundered through the hole as he cleared it. They were after him. He snorted with fright. For a moment he stood still, confused by the din of voices and the rushing feet. Then he darted away headlong through the rubbish towards the house, ten yards away. His only escape lay that way. He entered the house at a bound, through a hole in the kitchen wall. He cleared the kitchen in two strides. He was in the hallway. *Flash, flash, bang, bang.* Two more shots. His fist floored a tall man. He rammed a second with his head. He floundered through the hall. *Bang, bang.* They whizzed closely past his right side. He slipped on the flags of the hall as he tried to wheel towards the right wall. He came to his hands and knees. As he rose again a man threw himself upon him, firing as he did so, so closely that Gypo smelt the explosion that flashed blindingly by his ear. Missed again. They closed, grappling one another’s bodies, with groping, shifting paws. They tumbled in the doorway. They both rose. Gypo loosed one arm and struck. The other man collapsed without a sound. Gypo dropped him. He fell on his back. It was the Dart Flynn.

Gypo grunted, bounded to his feet and wheeled to the right, into the open air. With a gurgling laugh, he bounded away into the darkness. He was away, swallowed by the night.

===Chinese===
顾不得迷眼的泥土和砭骨的寒气，吉波当即把两条腿捅进洞里，脚跟扣住园子的地皮。然后他松开一只手，扣住洞沿。这一下他的锁骨磕得不轻。此刻他的身子在洞里稳住了。他松开另一只手，靠两条大腿的肌肉夹紧洞壁撑着，直到第二只手和脑袋也进了洞。随后他连蹬带爬钻出洞口，上了园子。他一跃而起，头朝前猛冲出去。

他刚钻出洞口，两颗子弹就轰隆隆地穿洞而出。他们追上来了。他吓得鼻子直哼哼。他呆立了片刻，叫那一片人声鼎沸和杂沓的脚步搅得晕头转向。随后他一头扎进破砖烂瓦之间，朝十码开外的屋子直冲过去。他唯一的生路就在那边。他纵身一蹿进了屋，从厨房墙上的一个窟窿钻进去。他两大步跨过厨房。他到了过道里。*火光，火光，砰，砰。*又是两枪。他一拳撂倒一个高个子。又拿脑袋撞翻第二个。他跌跌撞撞冲过过道。*砰，砰。*子弹贴着他身子右侧嗖嗖地擦过。他正要朝右边的墙转身，脚下一滑，踩在过道的石板上，扑倒成双手双膝。他刚爬起来，一个人就朝他扑来，一面扑一面开枪，近得吉波都闻见了火药炸开的味儿，那一团火光就在他耳边白晃晃地闪过。又没打中。两人逼近，扭作一团，用摸索着、变幻不定的爪子抓挠对方的身子。他们在门口摔倒了。两人又都爬了起来。吉波腾出一条胳膊，一拳挥去。那人闷声不响地瘫倒了。吉波撒手丢开他。他仰面倒了下去。那是达特·弗林（Dart Flynn）。

吉波哼了一声，蹿起身来，向右一转，冲进了露天里。他喉咙里咕噜咕噜地笑着，一蹿一蹿遁入黑暗。他脱身了，被黑夜一口吞没。

## XV / 十五

===Original===
When Gallagher heard the first shot, he started to his feet angrily. He thought that his orders had been disobeyed and that they had shot the prisoner before taking him to the mountains. But even as he stood up, his anger changed to terror. He heard the rushing of feet and the babble of shouting voices, calling excitedly, in a panic:
“He’s escaped. He’s escaped.”
“The stairs. The stairs. Up the stairs, quick.”
Mary McPhillip screamed. Gallagher did not heed her. For three seconds his body was numbed with fear. He could not move a muscle. His lips blubbered. He was like an exhausted man about to have a heart attack. He stood unstably, like an uprooted tree, balancing for its fall. Mary jumped up and clung to him. He did not look at her. Then Mulholland rushed in. He was livid with fear.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔听到第一声枪响，怒冲冲地一跃而起。他还以为自己的命令被违抗了，他们没等把囚犯带上山就把他枪毙了。可他人还没站直，怒气已经变成了恐惧。他听见一阵奔跑的脚步声，一片乱哄哄的叫喊，人们在惊慌失措地激动喊着：
「他跑了。他跑了。」
「楼梯。楼梯。上楼梯，快。」
玛丽·麦克菲利普发出一声尖叫。加拉格尔没有理会她。足足有三秒钟，恐惧把他浑身子麻木了，一根筋都动弹不得。他的嘴唇哆嗦着。他像一个心力交瘁、眼看心脏病就要发作的人。他站立不稳，像一棵被连根拔起的树，晃晃悠悠地撑着不倒。玛丽跳起来，紧紧攀住他。他没有看她。这时马尔霍兰德冲了进来。他吓得面色铁青。

===Original===
“He’s escaped, Commandant,” he gasped; “he’s gone.”
Then Gallagher shook himself violently, thrusting Mary from him rudely. Uttering a volley of almost inarticulate oaths, he drew his pistol and grasped Mulholland by the throat. Mulholland yelled and struggled downwards to his knees.
“Don’t shoot me, Commandant,” he whined. “It wasn’t my fault. That man is a devil out of hell. There’s a spell on him. Don’t fire for the love of God.”
“Damn you and God,” snarled Gallagher, hurling him away.
===Chinese===
「他跑了，指挥官，」他喘着气说，「他跑没影了。」
加拉格尔猛地一个激灵，粗暴地把玛丽从身边推开。他嘴里连珠炮似的迸出一串几乎不成腔的咒骂，拔出手枪，一把掐住马尔霍兰德的喉咙。马尔霍兰德高声叫唤着，挣扎着瘫跪下去。
「别开枪打我，指挥官，」他哀嚎道。「这不是我的错。那个人是地狱里跑出来的魔鬼。他身上有邪法。看在老天爷份上，别开枪。」
「你跟你那个上帝都见鬼去吧，」加拉格尔狂吼一声，把他猛地掼开。

===Original===
He rushed out into the hall.
“After him,” he yelled. “After him. After him.”
There was nobody to take any notice of him. Everybody was on the street in pursuit of Gypo, except the sentry, who stood uncertainly in the doorway of the empty cell, with his pistol in his hand and his cap turned backwards, terrified, gaping at Gallagher.
===Chinese===
他冲出房门，奔进门厅。
「追他去，」他大喊。「追他去。追他去。」
谁也没有理会他。人人都上了街追吉波去了，只剩那个哨兵，六神无主地站在空牢房门口，手里握着枪，帽子反扣在脑后，吓呆了，张着嘴呆望加拉格尔。

===Original===
Then a rush of feet came on the stairs. Four men were coming down carrying Flynn between them.
“Who is that?” cried Gallagher.
“It’s Flynn, Commandant,” whispered one.
“His jaw is broken in a jelly,” whispered another.
===Chinese===
接着楼梯上响起一阵急促的脚步声。四个人架着弗林从楼上下来。
「那是谁？」加拉格尔喊道。
「是弗林，指挥官，」一个人低声说。
「他的下巴已经打得稀烂了，」另一个人悄声说。

===Original===
They arrived at the bottom of the stairs. Gallagher glanced at the prostrate, sagging body of Flynn. “Throw him in there on a form at once,” he said. “Mulholland. Come here. Where are those others?”
“Here they come, Commandant.”
“There’s no sight of him, Commandant,” gasped Tommy Connor, leaping down the stairs. “We thought we had better come back.”
“All right,” said Gallagher. “Are you all here now?”
===Chinese===
他们到了楼梯脚。加拉格尔瞥了一眼弗林那瘫软下垂的身子。「马上把他抬进去放在长凳上，」他说。「马尔霍兰德，过来。其他人在哪儿？」
「他们来了，指挥官。」
「连他的影子都没见着，指挥官，」汤米·康纳一边蹦下楼梯一边喘着气说。「我们想着还是回来好。」
「好，」加拉格尔说。「现在人到齐了吗？」

===Original===
He spoke in a terribly calm voice now. It was terrifying. Nobody answered for a moment.
“Hurry on, Peter,” said Connor to somebody that appeared at the top of the stairs.
It was Hackett. He rushed down, panting, with wild eyes. They were all back again.
“Who’s responsible for this?” cried Gallagher.
===Chinese===
此刻他说话的声音平静得可怕。那平静本身就叫人胆寒。一时谁也没有应声。
「快点，彼得，」康纳朝楼梯口冒出来的一个人喊道。
是哈克特。他冲下楼来，气喘吁吁，两眼发狂。人都回来了。
「这事谁负责？」加拉格尔喝道。

===Original===
Nobody answered. He swore and strode away down the passage to the cell. Connor and Mulholland followed him. The others stood spellbound. Gallagher pushed the sentry out of the way with a curse and entered the cell. He flashed his torch. He saw everything. A cold perspiration started gently around his temples. He shivered. He left the cell followed by the two men. Nobody spoke. They returned to the men at the foot of the stairway. As Connor passed the room where Mary McPhillip was, he ran in, picked her up from the floor and put her sitting on the form. Then he rushed away to Gallagher.
===Chinese===
没有人回答。他咒骂了一声，大步顺着过道朝牢房走去。康纳和马尔霍兰德跟在后面。其余的人呆若木鸡地站着。加拉格尔骂了一句，把哨兵推开，走进牢房。他打亮手电。一切都看在眼里。一层冷汗悄悄地从他两鬓渗出来。他打了个寒噤。他走出牢房，那两人跟在后面。谁也不说话。他们回到楼梯脚众人那里。康纳路过玛丽·麦克菲利普所在的那间屋子时，跑进去，把她从地板上扶起来，让她在长凳上坐好。随后他奔回加拉格尔那里。

===Original===
Gallagher stood looking at the ground for a few moments, with the men standing around him in silence. Then he looked around fiercely at every one. He spoke gently and in a friendly tone.
“Comrades,” he said, “our lives are at stake. What’s more, the Organization is in danger. The cause is in danger. Comrades—that—man—must—be—found. That man must be found if it costs a hundred men. Do you understand?”
“Yes, Commandant,” cried they all eagerly.
“Finnigan and Murphy stay here on guard. Do you hear?”
They clicked their heels in silence.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔站着，朝地面望了片刻，众人默不作声地围着他。随后他凶狠地环视每一个人。他说话的口吻却温和而友好。
「同志们，」他说，「我们的性命危在旦夕。更要紧的是，组织处在危险中。事业处在危险中。同志们——那——个——人——必——须——找——到。哪怕赔上一百条人命，也必须找到那个人。明白吗？」
「明白，指挥官，」众人都热切地喊道。
「芬尼根（Finnigan）和墨菲（Murphy）留下守在这儿。听见没有？」
他们默默立正，脚跟咔地一并。

===Original===
“Mulholland, you take the rest with you in the van and try and cut him off from the bridges. He will try and cross the river to the south to get away to the mountains. Get away immediately. Place your men and take up position yourself at the Butt Bridge. I’ll send reinforcements to you there and another officer. Slattery, you get reinforcements. Mobilize ten men from this district. Take them off your own list. Beat it. Quick. Off you go, Bartly. Remember the Cause is at stake. We are lost if that man gets away. He may be making for the police already. Run for your lives.”
===Chinese===
「马尔霍兰德，你带上其余的人坐货车走，想法子抢在桥头截住他。他会想方设法过河到南岸去，好逃进山里。马上出发。把你的人布置好，你自己到巴特桥（Butt Bridge）守着。我会往那边给你派援兵，再派一名军官去。斯拉特里（Slattery），你去调援兵。从本区动员十个人。从你自己的名册上调。快走。快。巴特利，你也去。记住，事业危在旦夕。那个人要是跑了，我们就全完了。他说不定这会儿已经在往警察局奔了。豁出命跑吧。」

===Original===
They went up the stairs, rushing with fanatical enthusiasm. In three seconds Gallagher was alone at the foot of the stairs. One sentry took up position at the top of the stairway. The other man went into the guardroom with Flynn. Mary McPhillip was standing in the doorway of the witnesses’ room, shivering, almost hysterical with fright.
===Chinese===
他们怀着狂热的激情奔上楼去。三秒钟工夫，楼梯脚下就剩了加拉格尔一个人。一名哨兵在楼梯口站了岗。另一个人随弗林进了卫兵室。玛丽·麦克菲利普站在证人室的门口，浑身发抖，吓得几乎歇斯底里。

===Original===
Gallagher stood for almost a minute, motionless, looking at the stairs, with his eyes almost shut. Then he shuddered and went into the guardroom. The sentry, a red-faced, young grocer’s assistant, was tying a red silk handkerchief around Flynn’s jaws. The only part of Flynn’s face that was visible was his eyes. Gallagher watched the sentry tying the knot at the back of Flynn’s skull. Then he looked into Flynn’s eyes.
===Chinese===
加拉格尔一动不动地站了将近一分钟，眯缝着眼睛望楼梯。随后他打了个寒噤，走进卫兵室。哨兵——一个红脸的年轻杂货店伙计——正往弗林的下巴上缠一条红绸手帕。弗林的脸只露出一双眼睛。加拉格尔看着哨兵在他后脑勺上打结。随后他注视弗林的眼睛。

===Original===
Flynn stared back coldly. Although he was suffering agonies of pain from his broken jaw, his eyes betrayed no sign of pain.
“Did you fire at him, Dart?” cried Gallagher in a whisper.
Flynn made a slight nodding movement.
“Did you hit him?”
Flynn raised his right hand and waved it from side to side, like a marker giving the signal for a washout. Gallagher sighed.
===Chinese===
弗林冷淡地回瞪着他。断颌折磨得他痛苦万状，可他的眼睛里看不出一丝痛楚。
「你朝他开枪了没有，达特？」加拉格尔压着嗓子喊道。
弗林微微点了点头。
「打中没有？」
弗林抬起右手，左右摆了摆，像个裁判员打出比赛因雨作废的手势。加拉格尔叹了口气。

===Original===
“Stick it out,” he said coldly. “We’ll get a doctor as soon as the reinforcements come. Can you swallow a drop of brandy?”
Flynn nodded.
“Here’s my flask. Use it.”
He put the flask into Flynn’s hand. He pressed the hand as he did so. Then he left the guardroom and walked over to Mary McPhillip.
===Chinese===
「忍着点儿，」他冷冷地说。「援兵一到，我们就给你请医生。你能咽下一口白兰地吗？」
弗林点点头。
「这是我的酒壶。拿去用吧。」
他把酒壶放进弗林手里，放的时候紧紧握了握那只手。随后他走出卫兵室，朝玛丽·麦克菲利普走去。

===Original===
She left the doorway when she saw him coming. He found her sitting on the form. He stood beside her, looking at the ground, wrapt in thought, gripping her shoulder with his right hand. She became terrified at his attitude, at his silence and the look on his face, which she could see dimly in the gloom. His face had become ashen pale. His eyes had sunk and grown glassy. The blood had left his lips. He was continually grinding his back teeth, slowly.
===Chinese===
她望见他过来，便离开了门口。他看见她坐在长凳上。他在她身旁站定，望着地面，陷入沉思，右手紧紧抓住她的肩膀。他这种样子，他的沉默，还有他脸上的那副神情——她在幽暗里依稀看得见的那副神情——都叫她心惊胆战。他的脸已经变成死灰色。他的眼睛凹陷下去，变得呆滞无光。血色从他唇上褪尽了。他还在不停地、缓缓地磨着后槽牙。

===Original===
“Dan,” she whispered at length, “what’s the matter with you?”
He did not answer for several seconds. Then he started, gasped, and let go her shoulder. He took two paces rapidly towards the door. He halted and put his hand to his forehead. He wheeled about and looked at her curiously.
“Oh yes,” he said calmly. “I forgot. Excuse me. I was thinking of something and I didn’t hear what you said. Let me see. Yes.”
He sat down beside her. He took her right hand gently into both his own and began to fondle it, with the soft gentle movements of a cat. He began to speak gently, in a soft, sad voice, looking at the floor in front of him.
“You’ll have to stay here with me now, Mary,” he said, “until I’m leaving here. Maybe we’ll have stay here two hours, maybe more. Gypo has escaped. I can’t move until I get news of him. The prisoner has escaped,” he repeated almost inaudibly. “If he can’t be found it will be the end of me, Mary. He knows so much.”
===Chinese===
「丹，」她终于低声开了口，「你怎么了？」
好几秒钟他没有回答。随后他猛地一惊，倒抽一口气，放开了她的肩膀。他疾步朝门口走了两步，又停住，把手按在额头上。他蓦地转过身来，好奇地打量她。
「哦，对了，」他平静地说。「我忘了。原谅我。我刚才在想事情，没听见你说什么。让我想想。是了。」
他在她身边坐下。他把她右手轻轻拢进自己双手里，爱抚起来，动作轻柔得像一只猫。他开始温和地说话，嗓音又软又哀伤，眼睛望着面前的地板。
「这会儿你得陪我待在这儿，玛丽，」他说，「直到我离开这里。也许咱们得在这儿待上两个钟头，也许更久。吉波跑了。得不到他的消息，我动不了。囚犯跑了，」他几乎听不见地又说了一遍。「要是找不到他，我就完了，玛丽。他知道的事情太多了。」

===Original===
Mary turned towards him eagerly and swallowed her breath. Her eyes grew moist and her lips quivered. The gentle tone of his voice went straight to her heart. It drew her towards him, not with the dreadful fascination with which she was drawn towards him before, but with a soft, gentle attraction, like what she had imagined love would be. Not the calm, calculating, respectable affection she experienced for the man she intended to marry, Joseph Augustine Short, but that tumultuous, devouring passion which she had expected real love to be, the love that was written of in books and poems. Ah! How she could love him like this! Soft and gentle like this! She could approach him and touch him, touch something in him that was soft and gentle and sympathetic and human. He was in danger. Good God! It was good that he was in danger, if it helped to disclose to her his real self. It had made him weak, this danger, ridding him of the horrid, impenetrable strength, that kept him cruel and cold. If she could have him to herself like this, she would sacrifice even her religion for his love. Aye! She would even forsake God for him like this.
===Chinese===
玛丽急切地朝他转过身去，屏住了呼吸。她的眼睛湿润了，嘴唇发颤。他嗓音里的温柔直透她的心田。这温柔把她朝他吸引过去——不再是先前那种叫她心惊肉跳的魔力，而是一种柔软、温存的引力，就像她想象中爱情该有的样子。不是她对那个打算嫁的人——约瑟夫·奥古斯丁·肖——所怀的那种平静的、精打细算的、体面的情意，而是她曾经期待真正的爱情该有的那种狂风骤雨、吞噬一切的热情，是书里诗里写的那种爱情。啊！她多想就这样爱他！就这样温存柔软地爱他！她可以走近他，抚摸他，抚摸他身上某种柔软、温存、富于同情而有人性的东西。他正处于危险之中。老天爷！他在危险中倒是好事，倘若这危险能让她看清他真实的自我。这危险使他软弱下来，剥去了他那层可憎的、坚不可摧的强硬，正是那层东西使他冷酷无情。要是她能这样独自拥有他，为了他的爱，她连自己的宗教都可以牺牲。是啊！为了他，她甚至可以这样背弃上帝。

===Original===
So she thought, looking at him with tears in her eyes.
She smoothed his shoulder gently with her hand and whispered to him:
“Dan,” she said, “you are in danger. Can I help you, Dan? Dan, you know I’d give my life for you.”
Gallagher turned towards her slowly.
===Chinese===
她这样想着，含着眼泪望着他。
她用手轻轻抚了抚他的肩头，对他低语：
「丹，」她说，「你有危险。我能帮上你吗，丹？丹，你知道的，我连命都可以给你。」
加拉格尔缓缓朝她转过脸来。

===Original===
“You would, Mary,” he said softly.
She nodded. He took her suddenly in his arms.
“You love me, Mary. Say you love me, Mary.”
“I love you, Dan,” she breathed on his lips.
===Chinese===
「你肯的，玛丽，」他轻声说。
她点了点头。他突然把她搂进怀里。
「你爱我，玛丽。说你爱我，玛丽。」
「我爱你，丹，」她把这句话的气息吐在他唇上。

===Original===
They kissed passionately, with strange abandonment. Then they sat for a minute, with their cheeks together, hardly conscious of anything but of a strange exaltation that was undefinable. A hot feeling of joyous exaltation pervaded their bodies. But it was not the exaltation of love. It was an abandoned sadness born of grief. The grief of two human souls clinging together for solace. It was beautiful and pure like love, that exaltation, born of fear, and of the eternal melancholy of the entramelled Irish soul, struggling in bondage.
===Chinese===
他们狂热地亲吻，带着一种忘乎所以的放纵。随后他们脸颊贴着脸颊坐了一分钟，对什么都几乎没了知觉，只觉得有一种难以名状的、奇异的亢奋。一股欢愉亢奋的热流涌遍他们全身。然而那不是爱情的亢奋。那是一种由悲哀生出的、放任的哀伤。是两个相互依偎着寻慰藉的灵魂的悲哀。那亢奋美丽而纯净，一如爱情——它生于恐惧，生于被缠缚住、在枷锁中挣扎的爱尔兰灵魂那永恒的忧郁。

===Original===
For Mary perhaps, it was almost pure mating love. For she loved that gentle voice; the last remnant of the gentle nature, that had been devoured in the struggle of life and replaced by a cold, callous, ambitious nature. She loved, but she only loved a phantom, a shy ghost come for an hour of the night, to fly from the dawn.
But for Gallagher, his caresses were a mask. He had hidden behind his gentle nature for the moment, as behind a mask, to rest and plot. Men like him always lean on women for support in moments of extreme danger.
===Chinese===
对玛丽来说，那也许几乎算得上纯粹的结合之爱。因为她爱的是那副温柔的嗓音，那温柔天性的最后残余——这温柔天性早已在生活的挣扎里被吞噬掉，换上了一种冷酷、无情、野心勃勃的天性。她爱着，可她爱的只是一个幻影，一个羞怯的鬼魂，夜里来上一个时辰，天一亮就飞走。
可对加拉格尔来说，他的温存不过是一副面具。他暂时躲进自己温柔的天性里，就像躲进一副面具后面，好歇口气，好谋划。像他这种人，在极度危险的关头，总要倚靠女人来支撑。

===Original===
Even as he sat with her arms about him, with her breathing words of love on his lips, he was thinking, not of her, but of the great danger that confronted him. Would Gypo inform again before he was caught?
At length, with a low exclamation he got to his feet, releasing himself hurriedly from her embrace. He clenched his fists.
“Mary,” he said without looking at her, “you see how I need you. I need somebody to talk to, somebody to trust. There is nobody else but you I can trust, Mary. And I don’t know why I trust you.”
He paused. She was not listening. She was suffering a reaction from her exaltation. Why was he talking like this? A lover did not talk like this. He was only thinking of himself.
===Chinese===
他坐在那里，她的手臂搂着他，她的气息把爱的絮语吐在他唇上，而他心里想的却不是她，而是横在他面前的巨大危险。吉波会不会在被抓住之前，再去告一次密？
终于，他低呼一声，站起身来，慌忙从她的怀抱里挣脱出来。他攥紧了双拳。
「玛丽，」他说着，并不看她，「你瞧我多需要你。我需要有人说话，需要有个可以信任的人。除了你，我再没有第二个可以信任的人了，玛丽。可我自己也不知道为什么信任你。」
他停住了。她没有在听。她的亢奋正在退潮，一阵空虚的反噬涌上来。他怎么这样说话？恋人可不这样说话。他心里只有他自己。

===Original===
“But since the first time I saw you, standing in the crowd with another girl, while I was addressing a strike meeting, I knew I could trust you. I remember thinking as I saw your face, that you were the woman for me. It was queer and I can’t explain it. Something in your face told me that you were my woman. It’s very queer, that. You see thousands of faces every day. There is something queer and mysterious in them all, something suspicious and hostile. Then you see one face that you have been looking for all your life as it were. There is nothing hidden or mysterious in that face. It can hold nothing hidden from you. It’s queer. I haven’t worked it out yet. It’s in the eyes, I think. The eyes are the doors of the mind. But I haven’t worked it out yet. But what am I talking about? It’s a sure sign that I’m worried when I ramble off like this. I talk to myself in my room, for want of a listener, when I’m up against it. I talk all night, sitting up in bed, with a pistol in my hand.” He lowered his voice and smiled with his lips, while his eyes glittered. He looked at her for a moment. “If the boys knew that I get the wind up now and again, they wouldn’t be afraid of me. And then.…” He drew his hand across his windpipe. “Sure. That’s what keeps me safe. They are afraid of me. That’s all it is. It’s not love. Oh no. I wouldn’t have it, anyway. There’s nothing like fear. Nobody loves me. Not even that slobber of a fellow Hackett, who stooped down one day on the quays to tie my shoelace. He’d die for me, but only because he believes I’m cold and hard and callous and that I could shoot him dead without a quiver of an eyelid. You see… he’s the opposite from… There you are, Mary. Good God! I must be very bad tonight. I’m wandering. Mary, does your right knee tremble and you can’t stop it?”
===Chinese===
「可是打我头一回见你，你跟另一个姑娘站在人群里，我正在罢工集会上讲话，那时候我就知道我可以信任你。我记得当时望着你的脸就在想：你就是我要的那个女人。这事很古怪，我说不明白。你脸上有种什么东西告诉我，你是我的女人。这事真是古怪得很。你每天要看见成千上万张脸。每张脸里都有点古怪、神秘的东西，有点可疑的、带敌意的东西。后来忽然见到一张脸，仿佛是你一辈子一直在找的那一张。那张脸上没有任何遮遮掩掩、神秘莫测的东西。它对你藏不住任何东西。真怪。我到现在也没想明白。大概是在眼睛上。眼睛是心灵的门。可我到底没想明白。不过我这是在说什么呢？我一这样东拉西扯，就准是心里烦乱透了。走投无路又没人听我说的时候，我就在自己屋里自言自语。我能说上一整夜，坐在床上，手里握着枪。」他压低嗓音，嘴唇笑着，眼睛却在发亮。他望了她片刻。「要是弟兄们知道我时不时也会吓得心里发毛，他们就不会怕我了。那么往后……」他用手在自己喉咙上横着划了一下。「可不是嘛。我就是这样保住性命的。他们怕我。就是这么回事。不是爱。哦不。反正我也不要爱。什么都比不上恐惧管用。没有人爱我。连哈克特那个滥情的家伙都不爱我——有一天他在码头上弯下腰，给我系过鞋带。他肯为我死，可那只是因为他相信我冷酷、硬心肠、没心肝，相信我眨也不眨一眼就能把他崩了。你瞧……他跟……正相反。就是这样，玛丽。老天爷！我今晚一定是很不对劲。说话都乱了。玛丽，你的右膝盖是不是会发抖，自己怎么也止不住？」

===Original===
“Dan, Dan,” cried Mary, seizing his right knee in both her hands, “don’t worry. Don’t worry, Dan.” She began to rub the knee. “That’s nothing. My father often gets it. It’s only nerve tension. A nurse out of the Mater Hospital told me all about it. You can live to be a hundred with it. She says it’s due to tea drinking. But… Dan, why are you so hard and cynical all of a sudden about everything? Can’t you give it all up and settle down? You said you—”
===Chinese===
「丹，丹，」玛丽喊道，双手抓住他的右膝盖，「别烦心。别烦心，丹。」她开始揉那膝盖。「那不算什么。我爸爸就常犯。不过是神经紧张。马特医院（Mater Hospital）的一个护士跟我说得清清楚楚。带着这毛病活到一百岁都成。她说那是喝茶喝出来的。可是……丹，你怎么忽然对什么都这样冷硬、这样愤世嫉俗？你就不能把这一切都撂下，安安稳稳过日子吗？你说过你——」

===Original===
“Settle down?” cried Gallagher, jumping to his feet and looking at her fiercely, as if she had suggested a heinous crime. “Give it up! How do you mean? Pooh! Women, women, women! You don’t understand that it’s my life. It’s my life, I say. You might as well tell me stop breathing and… After all…” He seemed to think of something startlingly unexpected, for he looked at her with open lips. He continued, shyly almost, in a scarcely audible voice, as if he were soliloquizing. “After all, you weren’t affected the way I expected you would be. You would never understand. You would never join me in the way… Hm! I see.”
===Chinese===
「安安稳稳过日子？」加拉格尔喊道，跳起身来狠狠地瞪着她，仿佛她撺掇他犯一桩十恶不赦的大罪。「撂下！你这是什么意思？呸！女人，女人，女人！你们不明白，这就是我的性命。我说，这就是我的性命。你还不如叫我不许喘气，再……话说回来……」他像是猛然想起一件出人意料的惊人之事，张着嘴望住了她。他接着往下说，几乎带着几分羞怯，声音低得勉强听得见，仿佛在自言自语。「话说回来，你并没有像我料想的那样受到震动。你永远也不会明白。你永远不会照……那种方式跟我同路。嗯！我明白了。」

===Original===
“Now what have I said, Dan?” she whispered nervously, biting her fingers.
She was terrified that she had lost him… yes, in a way, strangely enough, she was terrified at losing his love, as if she had him securely in her possession, as a loving husband for a long time… that she had lost him by some foolish phrase.
“Nothing,” he muttered solidly.
He crossed his hands on his chest and began to pace up and down once more. It was a long time until he spoke. She tried to get enraged with him and could not do so. She began to pity herself.
===Chinese===
「我这会儿说了什么啦，丹？」她紧张地低声问，咬着手指。
她害怕自己失去了他……是的，说来也怪，从某种意义上说，她害怕失去他的爱，仿佛她早已稳稳地占有了他，像占有一位恩爱多年的丈夫……害怕自己因为一句蠢话把他失去了。
「没什么，」他沉沉地咕哝了一声。
他双手交叉在胸前，又开始踱来踱去。好半天他没有说话。她想对他发一顿火，却发不起来。她开始可怜起自己来。

===Original===
“It’s waiting like this that’s hard,” he said suddenly in a whisper. “I don’t mind dying. It’s not that I mind. It’s waiting without a chance of knowing what’s going to happen. They talk of the bravery of those louts that get the V.C. What are they but stupid carrot-heads? Theirs is the bravery of the dull-witted ox. A man must be intelligent to be brave. It’s only the intelligent man that can visualize danger. If he is brave he never seeks danger, but he seeks dangerous methods of life. You see the difference? Well, it doesn’t matter anyway. I had this all worked out a long time ago so I don’t need to discuss it very much. But this is the point I have to explain now. There is no danger in open warfare. There’s merely death, and death is not dangerous. The Russians proved that. Not recently, but in Bielinsky’s time. That is, of course, they proved it in relation to their own needs. But according to my own calculations and discoveries, death brings us back into the great consciousness of the Universe, which is eternal. Therefore death, properly speaking, is not death. It is a second stage of birth. No, that’s quite wrong. I can see where that would lead me. There is neither birth nor death. But… All that’s out of the count. We have to tackle a minor question. Obviously it’s a minor question. Now that’s better. Now we see death is not a danger. But defeat is a danger. Defeat by one’s enemies. Not defeat by one’s friends. But of course there are no friends. Friends is a bourgeois word. It has no longer any meaning. So defeat in the true sense means defeat by one’s enemies. It’s synonymous. Well, I face defeat. Therefore.…” Suddenly he waved his right hand in a circular fashion above his head and then pointed it fiercely at the wall to his left. “It’s waiting like this that’s hard,” he cried fiercely. “I’ve been out with a gun many a time. I’ve been shot at. I have two holes in me. That’s nothing. You don’t know what’s happening because you become an animal. But waiting is different. You are in command. That’s different. A brain, a mind, a great eye, probing the unknown. But…” He stopped suddenly and tittered audibly in his throat.
===Chinese===
「难熬的是这样干等着，」他忽然低声说。「死我倒不怕。我不是怕死。难的是干等着，一点也不知道接下来会出什么事。人们讲起那些得了维多利亚十字勋章（V.C.）的粗坯多么勇敢。他们算什么？不过是些愚蠢的红毛傻蛋。他们那种勇敢，是笨牛的勇敢。人得有智慧才算得上勇敢。只有聪明人才能把危险想得具体。他若是真勇敢，就决不去寻危险，而是去寻危险的生活方式。你明白其中的分别吗？唉，反正也无所谓。这些道理我早就推演得妥妥帖帖，用不着多谈。可眼下有一点我得说清楚。公开的战斗里没有危险。只有死，而死不是危险。俄国人证明过这一点。不是近来，是在别林斯基（Bielinsky）那个年代。当然喽，他们是就他们自己的需要证明的。可照我自己的推算和发现，死亡把我们带回到宇宙的大意识里去，那意识是永恒的。所以严格说来，死不是死。它是出生的第二阶段。不，这话说得全不对。我看得出这么说会把我引到哪里去。根本就无所谓生，也无所谓死。可是……这些都不算数。我们得来对付一个小问题。显然是个小问题。这就对了。现在我们看清楚了：死不是危险。但失败是危险。败在敌人手里。不是败在朋友手里。当然喽，根本就没有朋友。朋友是个资产阶级的字眼。早就没有任何意义了。所以严格意义上的失败，就是败在敌人手里。这本是一而二、二而一的事。好，我面临的就是失败。所以……」他突然把右手在头顶上挥了一个圈，随即凶狠地指向左边的墙壁。「难熬的就是这样干等着，」他狂喊。「我提着枪出去过多少回。人家朝我开过枪。我身上带着两个窟窿。那算不了什么。那时候你不知道出了什么事，因为你变成了一头畜生。可等待不一样。等待时你是指挥者。那就不一样了。一个头脑，一颗心智，一只巨眼，探入那未知里去。可是……」他突然打住，喉咙里发出一声听得见的窃笑。

===Original===
“Jesus, Mary and Joseph protect me,” Mary began to murmur rapidly to herself. She shut her eyes and tried to think of Heaven. Her mind had suddenly become void of all sense of knowledge and emotion. She felt an intense cold in every pore of her flesh. As she rambled through the prayer over and over again with her lips, a ridiculous rigmarole of a song went through her mind with a tintillating sound, about, “Piping Tim of Galway.”
===Chinese===
「耶稣、玛利亚、约瑟护佑我，」玛丽开始急急地喃喃自语。她闭上眼睛，努力去想天国。她的头脑忽然变得空空如也，知识和情感都失了踪。她觉得肉身的每一个毛孔里都透着一股彻骨的寒气。她嘴里翻来覆去、颠三倒四地念着那篇祷文，脑子里却叮叮咚咚地转着一支荒唐的歌谣，唱的是「戈尔韦的吹笛人蒂姆」。

===Original===
He sat down beside her on the form, bent towards her and kissed her coldly on the forehead. His cold lips remained on her forehead for three seconds. Then he sighed and got to his feet again. He must keep in movement. He must keep talking. He could not stop his brain from thinking at an enormous rate and the only way to relieve the congestion was by talking aloud. The formation and enunciation of the words deflected a fraction of the brain forces and liquidated them. Faster, faster, wilder, wilder he must talk, to keep pace with the tremendous speed of his heated brain.
===Chinese===
他在长凳上挨着她坐下，俯身在她的额头上冷冷地吻了一下。他冰凉的嘴唇在她额头上停留了三秒钟。随后他叹了口气，又站起身来。他必须不停地走动。他必须不停地说话。他的脑子正以惊人的速度转着，怎么也停不下来，唯一的疏解办法就是大声说话。字句的组织和吐纳能分走一分脑力，把它消耗掉。他必须说得更快、更快、更狂、更狂，好跟上他那发烧的脑子那雷霆万钧的速度。

===Original===
“Where is he now?” he whispered with a kind of cackle in his throat that was like a laugh. “Where is he now? Why can’t we see with the mind, long distances? How very stupid I am after all in spite of my philosophy. He might be in the police station at this very moment, with a big, fat sergeant taking down his statement.” He shuddered and bit his lip. “Good Lord Mary! If you only knew what a statement he could make. Ha! ha! He and Francis are the only two men in the Organization who could tell anything worth while. And Francis is dead.”
===Chinese===
「他这会儿在哪儿？」他低声说，喉咙里带着一种近似笑的咯咯声。「他这会儿在哪儿？我们为什么不能用心眼看见远处？瞧我到底还是多蠢，哲学也白学了。说不定他这会儿正在警察局里，一个肥头大耳的警佐正往纸上记录他的口供呢。」他打了个寒噤，咬住嘴唇。「老天爷，玛丽！你要是知道他能做出一份什么样的口供就好了。哈！哈！他和弗朗西斯是组织里仅有的两个能说出点有价值事情的人。而弗朗西斯已经死了。」

===Original===
He paused. Mary bit her teeth, dispelled the tintillating rigmarole of a song and began another prayer, one to Our Lady of Perpetual Succour.
“Ye see, Gypo was so useful. There were things he could do that no other man could do. Not so much by his immense strength, as on account of his particular mental qualities. It’s easy to get as strong a man, but a mind like that is hard to find. I doubt if there is another. He was priceless. Damn him. He’s a superhuman monster. Why did I say *was* before? He is. He is. That’s the worst of it. I wish he… The government would give a million pounds for that statement. Good Lord! I never thought Gypo could turn informer. It must have been a mistake. I couldn’t be wrong about him. Some mistake. Sure. He isn’t the type. Sure. I swear he isn’t. How could he be? He responds to me like, like a needle to a magnet. Then how did he inform? On his own pal too! That’s the strange thing about it. I’ve been studying him for eight years and he never showed any signs of personal initiative. Never once. I shouldn’t have dropped him for six months. But of course I had to keep up respect for the rules of the Organization. Good Lord!” he cried pathetically, looking at the ceiling and wringing his hands almost in despair, “I’m alone with nobody to help me. Mary, there’s nobody to give me advice. Why did nobody warn me against expelling Gypo? What?”
===Chinese===
他停了停。玛丽咬紧牙关，赶走脑子里那支叮咚乱响的荒唐歌谣，又换了一篇祷文，这回是向永助圣母（Our Lady of Perpetual Succour）祷的。
「你瞧，吉波那时候多有用来着。有些事只有他干得了，别人谁都干不了。倒不全是凭他那一身蛮力，而是凭他那种特出的心性。跟他一样强壮的人好找，那样的一个头脑可难寻。我怀疑天下还有没有第二个。他是无价之宝。去他的。他是个超人的怪物。我方才为什么说他『曾经』是？他现在也是。他现在也是。糟糕就糟糕在这里。我但愿他……政府肯出一百万镑买那份口供。老天爷！我从没想过吉波会当告密者。那一定是个错。我看他不会看走眼。总是哪里弄错了。可不是嘛。他不是那种人。可不是。我发誓他不是。他怎么会是呢？他响应我，就像，就像铁针响应磁石。那他怎么会去告密？还告的是他自己的哥们儿！怪就怪在这里。我研究了他八年，他从来没显出过一点自作主张的苗头。一回也没有。我不该把他撂下六个月不管。可我当然得维护组织规矩的体面。老天爷！」他悲怆地喊道，眼睛望着天花板，绞着双手，几乎绝望了，「我孤身一人，没人帮我。玛丽，没有一个人给我出主意。当初为什么没有人拦着我，别开除吉波？啊？」

===Original===
He paused. She did not reply. She shuddered and did not look. It was difficult to pray. She was so tired. And it was terrifying not to pray. Then she might have to listen to him.
===Chinese===
他停住了。她没有回答。她打了个寒噤，眼睛不朝他看。祷告难以念下去。她太累了。可不祷告又心惊胆战。那样她就不得不听他说话了。

===Original===
Then suddenly she was startled into an upright position, with her eyes staring and her mouth wide open. Gallagher had uttered a strange sound. Then he ran crouching to the form. He hurled himself upon it. He clutched at her knees. He was looking with wild, strained eyes at a point on the wall. He jabbered in a dry parched voice.
“There he is, Mary. I see him. I see him. I see the sergeant writing it down. They are giving him a drink. D’ye see him, Mary, with his little hat perched at the back of his head, making the statement? D’ye hear him say my name? D’ye hear him?”
She drew his head towards her with both hands, trying to make him look at her face, trying to get his staring eyes away from the wall, but he struggled against her. His eyes were fixed wildly on some point in the wall. He writhed.
===Chinese===
忽然，她吓得猛地坐直了，眼睛瞪着，嘴巴大张。加拉格尔发出了一声怪响。随后他猫着腰奔到长凳前，一头扑上去。他抓住她的膝盖。他瞪着一双狂乱发直的眼睛，死盯住墙上的一点，用一种干哑焦渴的声音急急地叨念。
「他在那儿，玛丽。我看见他了。我看见警佐正在往纸上写。他们在给他酒喝。你看见他了吗，玛丽，小帽子翘在后脑勺上，正做口供？你听见他说我的名字了吗？你听见了吗？」
她双手把他的头扳向自己，想让他看着自己的脸，想把他那双发直的眼睛从墙上引开，可他挣抗着不肯。他的眼睛狂乱地死死钉在墙上的某一点上。他扭动着身子。

===Original===
Then suddenly he sighed, turned towards her and smiled. It was a natural, healthy smile. His eyes danced humorously as he smiled. His terror had passed away, giving place to a momentary joy. He felt hilarious, like a woman drunk with wine. He took Mary suddenly into his arms and kissed her. He tickled her neck playfully with his fingers, laughing all the time.
But she struggled to free herself, panting. He loosed her and stopped laughing, looking at her in surprise.
“Did I frighten you, Mary?” he said casually. “That’s all right. I often get a fit of the blues like that. Don’t worry. Did you think I was mad?” he added with a little laugh.
“Oh, you’re all right now, Dan, ha, ha.”
She was trying to laugh to cheer herself, but she made a poor job of it.
“Sure I am, Mary. As right as rain. Everything will be all right. Of course it will. Don’t worry.”
There was a long silence. They sat close together, looking at the ground.
===Chinese===
随后他忽然叹了口气，朝她转过脸来，笑了。那是一抹自然的、健康的微笑。他微笑的时候，眼睛诙谐地闪动。他的恐惧已经过去，让位给一阵短暂的欢悦。他觉得乐不可支，像个喝醉了酒的女人。他突然把玛丽搂进怀里亲吻。他用手指顽皮地搔她的脖颈，一路笑个不停。
可她喘着气挣扎着想脱身。他放开她，止住笑，诧异地望着她。
「我把你吓着了，玛丽？」他随口说道。「没事的。我常常这样犯一阵忧郁。别担心。你以为我疯了吗？」他带着一丝笑补充道。
「哦，你这会儿没事了，丹，哈，哈。」
她努力笑一笑给自己打气，却笑得很不像样。
「当然没事，玛丽。好得很。一切都会好起来的。当然会好。别担心。」
是长长的一阵沉默。他们紧挨着坐在一起，望着地面。

===Original===
“Tell me, Dan,” whispered Mary awkwardly, “did you see anything that time? When you were looking at the wall? Did you see anything? Tell me, quick. It’s such a queer place, this. I think there are devils in it.”
“Damn it!” snapped Gallagher. “Why did you bring the subject up again, when I want to forget it? Devils! Huh! Devils!”
He jumped to his feet and took two paces forward, stretching his hands out over his head with peculiar intensity, like a man with a rheumatic twinge in his shoulder blades. Then he shrugged himself and rattled off with startling suddenness, in a quite calm voice, cheerful and debonair.
“You are right,” he said, “after all, in asking the question. I should have explained at the time,” he yawned, “what I meant by seeing him. Of course I was speaking figuratively. There are no such things as devils, at least not supernatural creations, as the current superstition understands them to be. The only devils to be afraid of are human devils. I know numbers of them. They are real enough. But they wear sheep’s clothing. Respectable, law-abiding fellows. I’ll see them again in a few hours, if Gypo gets to the police station with his story. They’ll drawl out slowly their sentence on me. Ha! Pretty boys. And here I am doing nothing while they are…”
===Chinese===
「告诉我，丹，」玛丽尴尬地低声说，「你方才看见什么了吗？你望着墙的时候？你看见什么了吗？快告诉我。这地方真邪门。我看这里头有魔鬼。」
「见鬼！」加拉格尔厉声道。「我正想忘掉，你偏又提这个？魔鬼！哼！魔鬼！」
他跳起身，朝前走了两步，两只手往头顶上方猛力一伸，那架势怪得很，像个肩胛骨风湿痛突然发作的人。随后他浑身一抖，以一种吓人的突然，用一种全然平静的、快活而潇洒的声音滔滔不绝地说了起来。
「你问得对，」他说，「说到底问得有理。当时我就该解释清楚的，」他打了个哈欠，「我说看见他，究竟是什么意思。当然喽，我那是打比方。世上根本没有魔鬼这东西，至少没有眼下迷信所理解的那种超自然的造物。唯一值得害怕的魔鬼，是人形的魔鬼。这种人我认识一大把。他们可真实得很。只不过披着羊皮罢了。一本正经、奉公守法的老好人。要是吉波揣着他那一套进了警察局，过几个钟头我就要再见到他们了。他们会慢吞吞地拖长调子，宣判我的罪名。哈！漂亮的小伙子们。而我呢，眼下无所事事，由着他们……」

===Original===
He moved rapidly up and down again, clutching his hands behind his back, jerking his body about and crunching his teeth.
“I am alone,” he continued. “Alone. I stand alone. They can easily buy off the rest of the Executive Committee. They’ll be only too glad to get away free, with their lives, at any cost, if it comes to a fight. If evidence is found against me, sufficient to prove certain things, they can strike at me with impunity. My own rank and file would be the first to stone me to death. Their damn superstitions always stand in the way of revolutionary beliefs. They talk at International Headquarters about romanticism and leftism and all sorts of freak notions. What do they know about the peculiar type of hog mind that constitutes an Irish peasant?”
===Chinese===
他又疾步踱来踱去，两手紧攥在背后，身子一抽一抽地扭动，牙齿咬得咯咯响。
「我是孤家寡人，」他接着说。「孤家寡人。我孤身独立。执行委员会里其余的人，他们轻而易举就能收买。真到了火并的时候，那些人巴不得保住性命脱身，什么代价都在所不惜。只要找到对付我的证据，足以证实某些事情，他们就能毫无顾忌地对我下毒手。头一个拿石头砸死我的，就会是我自己的弟兄。他们那些该死的迷信，永远横在革命信念的路上。他们在国际总部里高谈什么浪漫主义啊、左倾主义啊，还有种种稀奇古怪的念头。他们哪里懂得爱尔兰农民那种特殊类型的猪猡脑筋？」

===Original===
“How dare you?” cried Mary indignantly.
He looked at her. Her eyes were flashing. She sat erect on the form. He had never seen a woman wild and imperious like that. He smiled weakly.
“Sorry to hurt your feelings,” he said cynically. “But I’m beyond that. Pish! I’ve got the whole country in a fine net and I’m within the law until they find something definite to go upon. I can snap my fingers at the lot of you.” He grew fierce and arrogant. “You and your patriotic ideas! I was wrong about you. I don’t want you. I never wanted you. Do you hear? I snap my fingers at the whole world. That hulking swine can do his best. I will drain his blood before dawn. Mark my words. He’ll never reach the police station. My destiny stands against him. And—”
===Chinese===
「你怎么敢这么说？」玛丽义愤填膺地喊道。
他望着她。她的双眼放着火光。她笔直地坐在长凳上。他从没见过哪个女人这样狂野、这样倨傲。他虚弱地笑了笑。
「伤了你的感情，抱歉，」他冷嘲地说。「可我早过了讲究这个的阶段。呸！我把整个国家织进了一张漂亮的网里，只要他们拿不到确凿的把柄，我就站在法律这一边。你们这一帮人，我统统可以打个响指不放在眼里。」他变得凶狠而狂傲。「你，还有你那套爱国理想！我把你看错了。我不要你。我从来就没要过你。听见没有？全世界我都可以打个响指不放在眼里。那头大块头蠢猪尽管使出他浑身解数。天亮之前我要放干他的血。记住我的话。他到不了警察局。我的命运拦在他面前。还有——」

===Original===
Just then the sentry’s challenge rang out. Gallagher immediately stood stock-still and listened. Then he rushed into the passage, drawing his pistol and muttering something. Two men were hurrying down the stairs. The first of them came up smartly to Gallagher and clicked his heels.
He was a small, slight man, with hawk’s eyes and a long, pointed, curved nose. He wore a loose raincoat and a check cap. He was Billy Burton, an Insurance Agent, a captain in the Revolutionary Organization. Gallagher shook hands with him eagerly.
“Glad they found you in, Billy,” he said. “You’re the very man I want.”
He led Burton into the guardroom and rapidly explained the situation. Then he detailed a plan. He detailed the plan coolly and minutely as if he had spent weeks at it.
===Chinese===
正在这时，哨兵喝问口令的声音响了。加拉格尔当即僵立不动，侧耳听着。随后他拔出手枪，嘴里咕哝着什么，冲进过道。两个人正匆匆下楼。前头那个利落地走到加拉格尔跟前，脚跟咔地一并立正。
他是个瘦小灵便的人，一双鹰眼，一只又长又尖的弯鼻子。他穿一件宽大的雨衣，戴一顶格子鸭舌帽。他就是比利·伯顿（Billy Burton），一个保险代理人，革命组织里的一名队长。加拉格尔热切地同他握手。
「真高兴他们登门时你正好在家，比利，」他说。「我正需要你这个人。」
他把伯顿领进卫兵室，飞快地说明了情况。接着他详细布置了一个计划。他把那计划布置得冷静而周密，仿佛为此花过几个礼拜的工夫。

===Original===
Burton listened, blinking his little eyes, sniffing, biting his nails, fondling the butt of his automatic pistol in his breast pocket.
Over on the form, Flynn was sitting, with his broken jaw swathed in a red silk handkerchief. He sat impassively, inscrutably communing with himself. He seemed to be unconscious of his surroundings, with his mind fixed immutably on some infinite problem.
The only sounds in the room were the drip, drip of the water from the many roofs and the patter of Gallagher’s voice.
His voice was again cold, hard, dominating, vital.
===Chinese===
伯顿听着，眨巴着他的小眼睛，抽着鼻子，咬着指甲，摩挲着胸前口袋里那支自动手枪的枪柄。
那边的长凳上坐着弗林，断了的下巴裹在红绸手帕里。他一动不动地坐着，莫测高深地同自己神交。他仿佛对周围的一切毫无知觉，心思一成不变地钉在某个无穷的问题上。
屋里唯一的声音，是四面屋顶淌下的水声嘀嗒、嘀嗒，和加拉格尔连珠般的语声嗒嗒不绝。
他的嗓音又变得冰冷、坚硬、威凛、劲健。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===
At a quarter to four the drizzling rain ceased. A sharp bustling wind arose. It came screaming down from the mountains upon Dublin. It was a hard, mountainous wind, a lean, sulky, snowy wind, that rushed through the sleeping city savagely, so that even the drops of rain on the muddy sidewalks leaned over before it, with frills on them.

The clouds arose, their hanging rumps cut away by the newborn wind. They hung high up in the heavens, gashed and torn, with a sour expression on their grey, slattern bodies. Here and there a rent came in the dishevelled panorama of cloud and the sky appeared, blue and chaste and a long way off.

This change in the mood of nature occurred when Gypo was bounding away from the Bogey Hole, trembling shakily with excess of energy. He ran through a short, narrow lane, so narrow that his shoulders grazed either side as he dashed through. He crossed a thoroughfare in four strides, casting a look on either side as he leaped across. He saw a sloppy roadway on one side, with a watchman’s glowing brazier at the far end and on the other side a hill. Tall tenement houses lined the thoroughfare, their battered old walls rambling up towards the sky, their squalor hidden by the majesty of the night.

He fled across the road and entered a dark archway. Then he bumped suddenly against an old cart and went head-over-heels with a smothered exclamation. The concussion and weight of his body propelled the cart a distance of three yards on its crazy wheels, with the shafts scraping along the ground. He struggled to his feet and was about to rush away again, when a human voice, coming from beneath him, made him stand still. He looked down fiercely. It was only some homeless derelict, who used the archway and the cart as a house and a bed.

===Chinese===
三点三刻，蒙蒙细雨住了。一阵尖利而躁动的风刮了起来。它一路尖啸着从群山扑向都柏林。那是一股硬邦邦的山风，一股精瘦、乖戾、带着雪意的风，凶蛮地灌过沉睡的城市，连泥泞人行道上的雨珠也在它面前倾侧了身子，边沿上还掀起一圈裙裾。

云升起来了，它们低垂的臀部叫新生的风削去了。它们高悬在天上，遍体裂着口子、撕得破破烂烂，灰色的、邋遢的身子板着一副酸溜溜的面孔。在这片蓬乱狼藉的云景上，这里那里裂开一道口子，天空便露了出来，湛蓝、贞静、离得很远。

天色的这番变卦，正发生在吉波从博吉洞夺路奔逃的时候，他浑身力气多得发颤，抖抖索索地跑着。他穿过一条又短又窄的巷子，窄得他冲过去时两边的肩膀都蹭着墙。他四步跨过一条大路，跃过去时朝两边扫了一眼。他看见一边是泥泞的路面，尽头处燃着守夜人红彤彤的火盆；另一边是一座小山。大路两旁排列着高高的分租楼，破旧的老墙歪歪斜斜地往天上蹿，它们的腌臜全叫夜的威严遮住了。

他逃过马路，钻进一条漆黑的拱道。跟着他猛地撞上一辆破旧的板车，闷哼一声，栽了个跟头。他身子这一撞的冲劲和分量，推得那辆板车踩着散架似的轮子滑出去三码远，车辕在地面上刮擦着。他挣扎着爬起来，正要再跑，脚底下响起一个人声，叫他站住了。他恶狠狠地朝下看。不过是个无家可归的流浪汉，拿这拱道当家、拿这板车当床。

===Original===
“The curse of﻿—” began a cracked, shivering voice.

Gypo was gone, with a clatter of boots, over the cobblestones of the archway. He debouched into a wide street of new, redbrick houses. He gripped a wall and peered around him, panting for breath, wild with the excitement of his escape.

It was then that he noticed the wind, the lifting clouds, and the faraway sky. He smelt the wind as he breathed in great gasps through his nostrils, to ease the pressure on his heart and lungs. Then suddenly, he longed for the mountains and the wide undulating plains and the rocky passes and the swift-flowing rivers, away to the south in his own country. Freedom and solitude and quiet, with only the wind coming through the bog heather! Hiding in some rocky fastness of the mountains, listening to the wind! Away, away, where nobody could catch him! To the mountains! To the mountains! Dark blue mountains with bulging sides and little sheep roaming over them, that he could catch and kill!

A wild ferocity of joy overcame him. He stared with dilated nostrils ahead into the rim of the sky above the houses, towards the south. He gazed, as if he were measuring the distance between him and the mountains, so as to take a giant leap, that would carry him at once into the heart of their solitude.

===Chinese===
「挨天咒的——」一个破裂的、哆哆嗦嗦的声音开了腔。

吉波已经跑没影了，靴子踩着拱道的鹅卵石，一路嗒嗒作响。他冲出到一条宽阔的街上，两边是新造的红砖房子。他抓住一面墙，朝四下里张望，喘着粗气，为这场脱逃兴奋得发狂。

直到这时他才留意到风、正在升高的云，和遥远的天空。他张大了鼻孔一口一口猛吸，闻着风里的气味，好减轻心肺上的压力。跟着，他突然渴望起群山来——还有宽广起伏的平原、多岩的隘口、湍急的河流，都在南边他自己的家乡。自由、孤独、清静，只有风穿过沼地上的石楠！藏进山里某处岩石的堡垒，听着风声！走啊，走啊，到谁也抓不到他的地方去！到山里去！到山里去！深蓝色的山，山腹鼓鼓的，小小的羊儿在上面漫游，他可以逮住它们、宰了它们！

一阵野蛮的狂喜攫住了他。他鼻孔张大地凝望着前方房顶上那一圈天际，望着南边。他凝神注视，仿佛在丈量自己和群山之间的距离，好纵身一跃，一下子跳进那孤独的腹地。

===Original===
Then he bent down, looking ahead intently. He spat on his hands. He put his hand to his head to settle his hat. But his hat was not there. His skull was bare and damp. He felt all over it and found a patch of clotted blood at the rear base, where it had been kicked during the struggle in the inquiry room. He took no notice of the blood, but kept feeling all over the skull, with a dazed look in his eyes, muttering:

“What am I to do without a hat? I had it this two years.”

In the same dazed way he felt all over his body. He uttered a little shout. He had found it in his trousers pocket, where he had stuffed it, during the inquiry, when he heard that ominous ring in Gallagher’s voice. He clapped it on to his skull, all wrinkled and tattered and tiny. He beat it with his palms, as if it were a mattress. Then, with a gentle sigh, he darted away, headed due south for the mountains.

===Chinese===
随后他弯下腰，专注地望着前方。他朝手心啐了口唾沫。他抬手去扶头上的帽子。可帽子不在了。他的脑壳光着，湿漉漉的。他把脑壳摸了个遍，在后脑根上摸到一片凝结的血，那是审讯室里搏斗时挨的一脚。他没理会血，只顾把脑壳摸来摸去，眼睛里一片茫然，喃喃着：

「没了帽子可怎么好？我戴了它两年了。」

他还是那样茫然地摸索全身。他小声欢呼了一下。他在裤袋里找到了它——审讯的时候，听见加拉格尔嗓音里那不祥的调门，他就把它塞在了那儿。他把它扣到脑壳上，皱皱巴巴、破破烂烂、小小的一顶。他拿手掌拍打它，像拍一床褥垫。然后，轻轻叹了口气，他一蹿而去，正朝南边，直奔大山。

===Original===
He ran recklessly, without thinking of the way, or taking any precautions. It was the slum district which he knew so well, the district that enclosed Titt Street, the brothels, the Bogey Hole, tenement houses, churches, pawnshops, public-houses, ruins, filth, crime, beautiful women, resplendent idealism in damp cellars, saints starving in garrets, the most lurid examples of debauchery and vice, all living thigh to thigh, breast to breast, in that foetid morass on the north bank of the Liffey. He ran through narrow streets and great, wide, yawning streets, lanes and archways, streets patched and buttressed, with banks of earth from fallen houses almost damming them in places, pavements strewn with offal, soddened by the rain.

He never made a mistake. He was headed for the mountains. The smell of the mountains was in his nostrils, flooding his lungs, making his heart pant with longing.

At last he entered Beresford Place and saw the river. Instinctively he paused, leaning against a wall, to examine the Bridge. He gasped and trembled.

Two men were standing at the near side of the Butt Bridge. They had already forestalled him. He listened. He toyed with a last hope. He moved cautiously across the open space, to reach the shelter of the ruins of the Custom House. He reached it. He peered closer at the men. They were still indistinct. After all, they might be robbers, workmen, homeless fellows trying to pass the night, students coming from the brothels and having a last drunken argument on their way home. He crawled nearer. Then his little eyes blinked and narrowed.

One of the men crouched against the biting wind. Gypo recognized the crouching figure silhouetted against the sky. It was Mulholland. And the other man, standing stiff, with his hands in his pockets, was Peter Hackett.

Gypo’s head became hot and stuffy. His eyes closed, as a sudden pain struck him in the forehead. He had an impulse to rush forward at the two men and strangle them. But he did not move. He was not afraid of the two of them, in spite of their being armed. He did not fear their guns. But they were part of the Organization. The Organization was at the Bridge. It had got there before him. He could not pass. Gallagher’s cold, glassy eyes were on the Bridge. He could not pass.

===Chinese===
他不管不顾地跑，不认路，也不提防。这是他熟得不能再熟的贫民窟地带，圈着蒂特街、窑子、博吉洞、分租楼、教堂、当铺、酒馆、断墙残垣、污垢、罪恶、漂亮女人、在潮湿地窖里闪闪发光的理想主义、在阁楼上挨饿的圣徒、最骇人听闻的荒淫与堕落——这一切大腿贴大腿、胸口贴胸口地活着，挤在利菲河北岸那片恶臭的泥沼里。他跑过窄街，跑过豁着大口的宽街，跑过小巷和拱道，跑过用撑壁补缀的街道——塌屋的土堆有些地方几乎把路拦腰堵死——人行道上狼藉着垃圾脏水，叫雨泡得稀烂。

他从没走错过。他一路朝大山去。山的气味在他鼻孔里，灌满他的肺，让他的心渴望得直喘。

末了他进了贝雷斯福德广场（Beresford Place），看见了河。他本能地停住，倚着一面墙，察看那座桥。他喘着气，直发抖。

两个人站在巴特桥（Butt Bridge）靠他这头。他们已经抢在他前头了。他听着。他还把玩着最后一丝指望。他小心地挪过那片空地，想躲进海关大楼（Custom House）的废墟里。他到了。他凑近细看那两个人。还是看不真切。说到底，他们也许是劫道的、做工的、寻地方过夜的无家可归的家伙、从窑子里出来、回家路上还要醉醺醺争到最后一场的学生。他又往前爬近了些。跟着他的小眼睛眨了眨，眯了起来。

一个人弓着身子抵挡砭骨的风。吉波认出了那个映在天幕上的弓身人影。是马尔霍兰。另一个直挺挺站着、双手插在兜里的，是彼得·哈克特。

吉波的头发热发胀。他闭上了眼，一阵突如其来的疼痛扎在他额头上。他有一股冲动，想冲上去掐死这两个人。可他没有动。他不怕他们俩，尽管他们带着枪。他不怕他们的枪。可他们是组织的一部分。组织就在桥上。它赶在他前头到了那儿。他过不去。加拉格尔那双冷冰冰、玻璃似的眼睛就盯着这座桥。他过不去。

===Original===
The smell of the mountains left his lungs and his nostrils. The wind still blew about his crouching body. But it had lost its odour. Now it was only sharp and biting, an enemy that drove him backwards, skulking and dumbfounded. Where was it driving him? Where was it driving him?

He crouched away before it, without taking counsel with himself, with his head hanging limply on his breast. He crouched across the open space and entered a roadway that led northwards. There was nothing within him with which he could take counsel. Within him he was blank and dark, like a bottomless abyss filled with thick fog. His hulking figure was driven by the wind to some boundless region where there was no shelter. He was driven by the wind to some boundless region where everything was coloured a dim grey, amorphous, terrible.

The vision of an abyss, grey, without shape, swayed before his eyes as he strode northwards, moving uncertainly, staggering slightly, without guidance. His footsteps became slower. He came to a halt and looked about him curiously. He was under a railway bridge that crossed the street sideways over his head, encased in a black covering. A little dark laneway opened to his right. He walked three paces up the laneway and leaned his shoulder against the damp wall.

There was shelter there. The wind did not come in. Only an odd gust swivelled around the corner and stirred the damp, musty air for a dying moment. It was quiet and dark, like the interior of a cave. He sighed.

Gradually he grew composed. He grew calm and very weary. He wanted to lie down and go to sleep for a long, long time. There was no use struggling any farther. He was alone. The darkness of the night enveloped him.

“There’s nobody here,” he murmured aloud.

===Chinese===
山的气味离开了他的肺和鼻孔。风还吹在他蜷缩的身子上。可它没了那股气息。如今它只剩下尖利和砭骨，是一个敌人，把他往后赶，赶得他缩头缩脑、目瞪口呆。它要把他赶到哪儿去？它要把他赶到哪儿去？

他在风前躬着身子退走，也不同自己商量什么，脑袋有气无力地垂在胸口。他缩着身子蹭过空地，进了一条朝北去的路。他心里空了，没什么可同自己商量的。他的内里一片空白、一片黑，像个灌满浓雾的无底深渊。他那庞大的身躯被风驱赶着，去往某个没有遮蔽的无边地带。他被风驱赶着，去往某个无边地带，那里的一切都蒙着一层昏灰，无形无状，狰狞可怖。

一个深渊的幻影，灰的、无形无状的，在他眼前晃荡，他迈步往北去，走得没个准头，微微踉跄，无人引路。他的脚步慢了下来。他站住，好奇地四下张望。他正在一座铁路桥底下，那桥横着从他头顶跨过街道，裹着一层黑色的罩子。右手边展开一条黑黢黢的小巷。他往巷子里走了三步，把肩膀靠上潮湿的墙。

这儿有遮蔽。风吹不进来。只有偶尔一阵旋风绕过拐角，把潮湿发霉的空气搅动一个垂死的瞬间。这里安静而黑暗，像个山洞的内部。他叹了口气。

渐渐他平静下来。他安静了，而且累极了。他想躺下，睡上很长很长的一觉。再挣扎也没用了。他孤单一人。夜的黑暗包裹着他。

「这儿没有人，」他出声地咕哝。

===Original===
The ground was a puddle. The walls were blank. He felt with his feet, seeking a dry spot to lie down. Everywhere his boot sank into a puddle. He cursed and moved on a pace. He felt again with his feet. Still more puddles. He moved along still farther and tried again. No use. Then he began to walk along mechanically, feeling the ground at intervals. Then he kept walking slowly without feeling the ground. He had forgotten about lying down.

He came to the end of the lane and saw a wide street in front of him. He halted excitedly.

“Where am I going?” he cried aloud.

He started at the sound of his voice and peered suspiciously over his shoulder. Of course there was nobody there. Then he steadied himself and tried to think of where he was and what had happened. It was a terrific struggle.

Slowly he began to remember recent events. Fact after fact came prowling into his brain. Soon the whole series of events stood piled there in a crazy heap. Everything rushed towards that heap with increasing rapidity, but nothing could be abstracted from it. It was just as if the facts were sinking in a puddle and disappearing. It was utterly impossible for him to reason out a plan of action.

“I must make a plan,” he murmured aloud.

===Chinese===
地上是一洼泥水。墙壁光秃秃的。他用脚探着，找一块干的地方躺下。他的靴子踩到哪儿都是水洼。他骂了一句，往前挪了一步。他又用脚探。还是水洼。他再往前挪，再试。没用。于是他顺着巷子机械地走下去，隔一阵用脚探探地。再后来他就只管慢慢走，也不探地了。躺下的事他已经忘了。

他走到巷子尽头，看见面前一条宽街。他激动地站住了。

「我这是往哪儿去？」他大声喊道。

他叫自己的声音吓了一跳，疑心地回头张望。那儿当然没有人。随后他定住神，努力去想他在哪儿、出了什么事。那是一场了不得的苦斗。
